A viewer keeps a port's full contents open on the canvas (index, value or shape type) while the user works. [Grasshopper 1](https://developer.rhino3d.com/guides/grasshopper/gh-algorithms-and-data-structures/data-structures/) uses "the Panel component to preview the values in a list", and [Rhino 9](https://www.rhino3d.com/9/new/) adds a [Content Viewer](https://www.rhino3d.com/features/grasshopper/content-viewer/) to "inspect both geometry and non-geometry data without leaving your definition". [Dynamo](https://primer2.dynamobim.org/5_essential_nodes_and_concepts/index-of-nodes.md)'s Watch node will "Visualize the output of node". In [Blender 4.x](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/modeling/geometry_nodes/output/viewer.html), "Use Shift-Ctrl-LMB on any node or socket to connect it to the active viewer". [Sverchok](https://nortikin.github.io/sverchok/docs/nodes/text/stethoscope_v28.html)'s Stethoscope draws data in the node view, showing the first and last 20 sublists. [Unreal](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/blueprint-debugger-in-unreal-engine) watches "keep track of Blueprint node pin values". Users want counts and items always in view: "Too many times I need to scroll down the list just to check the number of items in the list" ([forum, 2022](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/panel-wishes-gh1-gh2/151561)). Flow would add a pin to the existing hover peek, turning it into a viewer card attached to that port, with index, value or type, count, and the first and last items. Blender's Ctrl+Shift+click on a port would open one directly.
**Status:** PARTLY: "Output ports show count badges and a hover peek of the first items" · **Size:** S · **Value:** 5 · **Kind:** COMPLETION (hover peek) · **Depends on:** hover peek; count badges

<a id="data-05"></a>

#### DATA-05 Spreadsheet view of a port
A docked table shows a port's items as rows and their properties and attributes as columns, with sorting, filters and row counts. In [Blender 4.x](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/editors/spreadsheet.html), "Each column corresponds to an attribute or data property", rows are elements, sidebar row filters apply, and the status bar reports rows left after filtering; it can show the active Viewer node. The [Houdini](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/ref/panes/geosheet.html) Geometry Spreadsheet shows points, vertices, primitives and detail attributes, with a group (row) filter and an attribute (column) filter. [n8n](https://docs.n8n.io/data/) lets users "Choose between Schema, Table and JSON views" of node output. Grasshopper users ask for live search in the Panel and an easier way to copy items, which they call "too tedious" today ([forum, 2022](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/panel-wishes-gh1-gh2/151561)). Flow would show the selected port in a table. Columns would hold index, path for lists of lists, shape type, key measures (area, length, count) and attribute keys, with sort, filter, search and a status line ("212 rows, 37 shown").
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** M · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** DATA-02 Viewer node; attribute key sources; count badges

<a id="data-06"></a>

#### DATA-06 Graft, flatten, simplify and reverse on ports
Small modifiers on an input or output reshape the data in place: give each item its own sublist (graft), merge all into one list (flatten), remove empty or extra levels (simplify), or reverse. In [Grasshopper 1](https://modelab.gitbooks.io/grasshopper-primer/content/1-foundations/1-5/3_creating-data-trees.html), users "right-click the desired input or output and select Flatten, Graft, or Simplify", and "The component will display an icon to indicate that the tree is being modified" (Mode Lab primer, third party). McNeel's [guide](https://developer.rhino3d.com/guides/grasshopper/gh-algorithms-and-data-structures/advanced-data-structures/) defines graft as "each element is placed in its own branch". The jargon hurts: "I still find myself guessing as to whether something needs to be grafted, flattened, etc." ([forum, 2018, old](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/best-resources-for-understanding-more-about-data-trees/64442)). David Rutten warns that flatten "throws away all tree structuring" ([forum, 2019, old](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/newbie-understanding-flatten-and-grafted-data/82729)). Flow would show port modifier glyphs with plain names: "Each item separately", "All in one list", "Remove extra levels" and "Reverse order". The wire stroke would update before the run, and the hover peek would show the shape before and after.
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** M · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** wire strokes; hover peek; DATA-01 Matching rule

<a id="data-07"></a>

#### DATA-07 Viewer rows linked to the drawing
Rows in a viewer or table are linked both ways to shapes in the drawing: hover a row to highlight its shape, select rows to select shapes, and select shapes to filter rows. In the [Houdini](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/ref/panes/geosheet.html) Geometry Spreadsheet, selection transfer options push spreadsheet selections to the viewer, and "When you select components in the viewer, the geometry spreadsheet highlights the associated row headers"; "Filter Selected" limits the rows. [Blender 4.x](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/editors/spreadsheet.html) has "Show Only Selected" in Edit Mode. Grasshopper users ask for "being able to select one/several rows in the panel, and those specific items get highlighted in Rhino" ([forum, 2022](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/panel-wishes-gh1-gh2/151561)). This matters more in a CAD program than in a game engine, because urban designers think in parcels and blocks, not indices. Flow would reuse the flow's own layer, the Made-by badge and the Select pill: hovering a row would flash the shape, and selecting shapes in the drawing would filter the viewer.
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** M · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** DATA-02 Viewer node or DATA-05 Spreadsheet view; Show and Select pills; Made-by badge

<a id="data-08"></a>

#### DATA-08 Structure viewer for nested lists
A compact view shows the shape of a list of lists: how many sublists, how many items in each, where the empty ones are. The [Grasshopper 1](https://developer.rhino3d.com/guides/grasshopper/gh-algorithms-and-data-structures/advanced-data-structures/) Parameter Viewer "shows each branch address (called "Path"), and the number of elements in that branch", in text or graphic mode. [Sverchok](https://nortikin.github.io/sverchok/docs/nodes/viz/data_tree_viz.html)'s Data Tree Viz "displays the shape of input data in the node editor's window", and its [List Levels](https://nortikin.github.io/sverchok/docs/nodes/list_struct/levels.html) node shows a table of nesting levels and their shapes. [Dynamo](https://primer2.dynamobim.org/5_essential_nodes_and_concepts/5-4_designing-with-lists/3-lists-of-lists.md) shows levels "at the bottom of the Preview Bubble". In a thread titled "I Hate that I don't Understand Trees", helpers added a tree viewer to the file, and the poster found that "Simplify was a big part of what I was missing" ([forum, 2020, old](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/i-hate-that-i-dont-understand-trees/101458)). Flow would add a "Shape" line to the peek and the viewer ("12 lists, 3 to 8 items each, 64 in all, 2 empty"), with a collapsible outline for lists of lists.
**Status:** PARTLY: "strokes for one item, a list, a list of lists" and "count badges" · **Size:** M · **Value:** 3 · **Kind:** COMPLETION (count badges and hover peek) · **Depends on:** count badges; hover peek; DATA-02 Viewer node

<a id="data-09"></a>

#### DATA-09 Pick items from a list visually
The user picks some items from a computed list by searching or clicking shapes in the drawing, and the choice is stored in the flow instead of typed as index numbers. [Rhino 9](https://www.rhino3d.com/features/grasshopper/value-picker/)'s Grasshopper Value Picker is made to "Find, select, and filter from a long list of values", with search modes and multiple selection. [Houdini](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/nodes/sop/blast.html)'s Blast node "is designed to remove geometry that you select interactively in the viewport". Grasshopper users otherwise rely on workarounds: "Cull list item is a handy as well as cherry picker" ([forum, 2023](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/panel-wishes-gh1-gh2/151561)). Flow has "the selection" as a source, but that picks drawing objects, not items of a list a node computed. Flow would add a Pick node with a searchable checklist of the incoming items and a "Pick in drawing" mode. It would store picks by a stable key (attribute or lineage) rather than by position, and warn when a picked item disappears upstream.
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** M · **Value:** 3 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** lineage ("Every result carries lineage"); DATA-07 Viewer rows linked to the drawing; Made-by badge

<a id="data-10"></a>

#### DATA-10 Copy and export a port's data
Any port's contents can be copied to the clipboard as a table or sent to a CSV or spreadsheet file in one step. In Grasshopper 1, users "right-click "Copy Data Only" paste it in Notepad" from a Panel ([forum, 2022](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/panel-wishes-gh1-gh2/151561)), or stream Panel contents to a text file that updates "as they change" ([forum, 2010, old](https://www.grasshopper3d.com/forum/topics/export-textfile)). In Grasshopper 2, David Rutten "added an html export button to the viewer toolbar" ([McNeel forum, 2025](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/new-grasshopper-2-0-alpha-2025-feb/199833)). [Dynamo](https://dynamobim.org/dynamo-core-2-15-release/)'s Graph Node Manager can "export this data to CSV or JSON", although that covers node information, not port data. The same Panel thread calls copying items "too tedious". Flow already reads "a spreadsheet range" as a source. Flow would add "Copy as table" and "Export CSV" to the hover peek, the viewer and the spreadsheet view, using the same columns as DATA-05.
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** S · **Value:** 3 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** DATA-02 Viewer node; DATA-05 Spreadsheet view

<a id="data-12"></a>

#### DATA-12 Work on sublists per input (list levels)
An input can be told to work on each sublist or each item of a list of lists, without wrapping the node in a loop. In [Dynamo](https://primer2.dynamobim.org/5_essential_nodes_and_concepts/5-4_designing-with-lists/3-lists-of-lists.md), List@Level adds "Use Levels" and "Keep list structure" to any input, with levels L1, L2 and so on. [Sverchok](https://nortikin.github.io/sverchok/docs/nodes/list_main/match.html) list nodes take a Level parameter, and its [List Levels](https://nortikin.github.io/sverchok/docs/nodes/list_struct/levels.html) node flattens or wraps chosen levels from a table. Dynamo introduced it because "To access the innermost lists in Dynamo, you would normally have to use List.Map" ([Dynamo blog, 2016, old](https://dynamobim.org/?p=47455)). Numbered levels are abstract, and urban-design data rarely nests deeper than blocks, then parcels, then buildings. Flow would add a plain choice on an input, "Use: whole list / each sublist", previewed in the hover peek. Deeper cases would be left to the existing for-each container.
**Status:** PARTLY: "containers (loop ...; for-each)" · **Size:** M · **Value:** 2 · **Kind:** COMPLETION (for-each container) · **Depends on:** for-each container; wire strokes; DATA-01 Matching rule

### Containers, groups, frames and sub-flows (CONT)

14 features: 5 EXISTS, 8 PARTLY, 1 MISSING.

<a id="cont-01"></a>

#### CONT-01 Groups with title and colour
A group is a titled, coloured box drawn behind related nodes that moves them together; it is the usual way to label the stages of a flow ("site input", "massing", "metrics"). Blender has [Frame nodes with a label, label size and Shrink](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/interface/controls/nodes/types/layout/frame.html), Houdini has [network boxes with a title, colours from a palette and nesting](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/network/organize.html), Unreal has [comment boxes with a colour and a Group Movement mode](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/comments-in-unreal-engine), Nuke has [Backdrop nodes with label, colour and Z order](https://learn.foundry.com/nuke/Content/comp_environment/organizing_scripts/grouping_nodes.html), Node-RED has [groups with outline, fill and label styles](https://nodered.org/docs/user-guide/editor/workspace/groups), Substance 3D Designer has [frames that "Group nodes with label and color-coding"](https://experienceleague.adobe.com/en/docs/substance-3d-designer/using/workspace/graph-view/graph-items/graph-items), Dynamo rebuilt groups with [a title, description and header bar in 2.13](https://dynamobim.org/dynamo-core-2-13-release/), and n8n added [Canvas Groups in 2.31.0](https://docs.n8n.io/build/understand-workflows/workflow-components/canvas-groups.md). Users group constantly; one Blender user said "It feels like I'm wasting years of my life framing and de-framing nodes" ([Blender devtalk, 2023](https://devtalk.blender.org/t/testing-wanted-for-joining-nodes-into-frames/28241)). Flow already has groups with title and colour, Ctrl+G and one-step group move; CONT-05, CONT-11 and CONT-12 complete them.
**Status:** EXISTS · **Size:** - · **Value:** 5 · **Kind:** - · **Depends on:** -

<a id="cont-02"></a>

#### CONT-02 Collapse a selection into a sub-flow and expand it back
A sub-flow packs selected nodes into one node whose ports are the wires that crossed the edge of the selection, and can be unpacked again. Blender makes a node group with [Ctrl-G, adding Group Input and Output nodes where links cross the boundary, and ungroups with Ctrl-Alt-G](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/interface/controls/nodes/groups.html); Houdini has ["Collapse Selected into Subnet"](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/network/organize.html); Unreal has [Collapse Nodes and Expand Node](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/collapsing-graphs-in-unreal-engine); Nuke has [Collapse To Group and Expand Group](https://learn.foundry.com/nuke/Content/comp_environment/organizing_scripts/grouping_nodes.html); Max has [Encapsulate and De-encapsulate, which create inlets and outlets automatically](https://docs.cycling74.com/userguide/subpatchers/); ComfyUI [builds a subgraph from the selection and can unpack it](https://docs.comfy.org/interface/features/subgraph); Node-RED has [Selection to Subflow](https://nodered.org/docs/user-guide/editor/workspace/subflows); Unity VFX Graph has [Convert to Subgraph](https://docs.unity3d.com/Packages/com.unity.visualeffectgraph@17.0/manual/Subgraph.html). Users need it to keep big flows readable, but it must be faithful: Max's guide warns that encapsulating resets the objects' internal state, "a common source of confusion when starting out with Max" ([Max docs](https://docs.cycling74.com/userguide/subpatchers/)). Flow already has this (Ctrl+Shift+G); CONT-04, CONT-07 and CONT-08 complete it.
**Status:** EXISTS · **Size:** - · **Value:** 5 · **Kind:** - · **Depends on:** -

<a id="cont-03"></a>

#### CONT-03 For-each container
A for-each container runs the nodes inside it once per item (a plot, a block, a building) and gathers the results. Blender's [For Each Geometry Element zone runs nodes "for each element of a geometry", with Domain and Selection inputs, and joins the Generated outputs](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/modeling/geometry_nodes/utilities/for_each_geometry_zone.html); it arrived in [Blender 4.3](https://developer.blender.org/docs/release_notes/4.3/geometry_nodes/). Houdini's [Block End node loops by count or By Pieces and can Merge Each Iteration](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/nodes/sop/block_end.html), and Unreal PCG's [Loop node "Executes another graph as a subgraph, once per data in the loop pins"](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/procedural-content-generation-framework-node-reference-in-unreal-engine). Urban designers hit this need early: a Grasshopper beginner computing site coverage for hundreds of lots wrote "I have been copying the script for each lot" ([McNeel forum, 2018, old](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/site-coverage-map-for-numerous-lots-help/62438)). Flow already has a for-each container. CONT-06 (one pass per key value) and CONT-13 (inspect a single pass) extend it, and PREV-05 would let a user look at one pass's shapes in the drawing.
**Status:** EXISTS · **Size:** - · **Value:** 5 · **Kind:** - · **Depends on:** -

<a id="cont-04"></a>

#### CONT-04 Open a sub-flow in place (enter, edit, go back up)
Opening a sub-flow in place shows and edits its inner nodes on the canvas without expanding it, then returns to the parent. Blender enters a group with [Tab and leaves with Tab or Ctrl-Tab, with breadcrumbs showing where you are](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/interface/controls/nodes/groups.html); Houdini [goes in with double-click, I or Enter and up with U](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/network/navigate.html); TouchDesigner [enters a component with i and leaves with u](https://docs.derivative.ca/Network_Editor); Dynamo opens a custom node in ["a workspace with a yellow background"](https://primer2.dynamobim.org/6_custom_nodes_and_packages/6-1_custom-nodes/2-creating.md); ComfyUI [enters a subgraph by double-click and exits with Esc](https://docs.comfy.org/interface/features/subgraph); Nuke's [Group View edits a group's nodes inside the main graph](https://learn.foundry.com/nuke/Content/comp_environment/organizing_scripts/grouping_nodes.html). Grasshopper 2 beta testers asked for "a visual indication we're inside one", adding "the blue background of GH1 was quite effective" ([McNeel forum, 2026](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/gh2-bug-cluster-editor-is-very-slow/220906)). Today a Flow sub-flow must be expanded and collapsed again to change one value. Flow would open a sub-flow on double-click of its title or I, go up with U or the path bar, tint the canvas, draw the exposed ports as fixed In and Out rails at the canvas edges, and record the trip in canvas history; Ctrl+Shift+G expand stays for unpacking for good.
**Status:** PARTLY: "sub-flows (collapse a selection into one node with exposed ports, expand back)" · **Size:** M · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** COMPLETION (sub-flows) · **Depends on:** NAV-04 Breadcrumb path bar; canvas history; per-tab canvas state

<a id="cont-05"></a>

#### CONT-05 Collapse a group to its title bar
A collapsed group folds into one header that keeps its title and shows only the wires leaving it, so a finished stage takes the space of one node without becoming a sub-flow. Dynamo 2.13 says ["Collapsed groups look and behave like a giant node"](https://dynamobim.org/dynamo-core-2-13-release/), surfacing unwired ports and ports wired from outside, with a count of the nodes inside; Houdini can [minimize a network box to its top bar, and Ctrl-click makes other nodes move to make room](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/network/organize.html); n8n's [Canvas Groups collapse by clicking the header](https://docs.n8n.io/build/understand-workflows/workflow-components/canvas-groups.md), with [Alt+G and Shift+Alt+G to expand or collapse all groups](https://docs.n8n.io/build/keyboard-shortcuts.md). The demand is to read a large flow as a handful of stages; Dynamo and n8n both shipped it as a headline group feature in their release notes and docs above. Houdini's docs also warn that hidden nodes can confuse collaborators who don't notice them ([Houdini](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/network/organize.html)), so the folded header must say what is inside. Flow would add a chevron on the group title (and let H, already "collapse node", act on a selected group) that folds it into a bar showing title, node count, the boundary ports with their count badges, and any error or disabled state; folding is canvas-only. Modo's Schematic does this too: "Backdrops allow you to group nodes into a container that can be collapsed and moved around" ([Modo 12.2v1 release notes, 2018, old](https://learn.foundry.com/modo/content/release_notes/12/modo_12.2v1_releasenotes.html)).
**Status:** PARTLY: "groups with title and colour", "H collapse node" · **Size:** M · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** COMPLETION (groups) · **Depends on:** CONT-12 Group membership by enclosure; output port count badges

<a id="cont-06"></a>

#### CONT-06 For-each by key (one pass per attribute value)
Here the for-each runs once per distinct value of a key (district, land-use code, block id) instead of once per item, and each pass receives every shape that shares that value. Houdini's Block End iterates By Pieces, where a Piece Attribute defines pieces and "Partition attributes can only be integers or strings" ([Houdini](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/nodes/sop/block_end.html)); Unreal PCG's [Loop runs a subgraph once per data item on its loop pins](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/procedural-content-generation-framework-node-reference-in-unreal-engine); Blender's For Each zone takes a [Domain and a Selection](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/modeling/geometry_nodes/utilities/for_each_geometry_zone.html) but iterates elements, not keys. The pain is real for urban work: in a site-coverage thread the answer had to group buildings by plot by searching for each building's centre, so buildings over several plots were missed ([McNeel forum, 2018, old](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/site-coverage-map-for-numerous-lots-help/62438)). Flow would add a "Group by" field to the for-each container: choose an attribute key and each pass receives one group of shapes plus its key value; the gathered outputs keep the key, so tables, labels and colours line up per district or per plot without list juggling.
**Status:** PARTLY: "for-each" · **Size:** M · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** COMPLETION (for-each container) · **Depends on:** for-each container; attribute key source; lineage

<a id="cont-07"></a>

#### CONT-07 Sub-flow interface editor (port names, order, defaults, ranges, tooltips, sections)
An interface editor lets the author name, order and document a sub-flow's ports and set defaults, slider ranges and collapsible sections. Blender's group interface has inputs, outputs and panels; each socket has a Description shown as its tooltip, a Default, and Min and Max, and panels can be "Closed by Default" ([Blender](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/interface/controls/nodes/groups.html)); panels came in [4.0: "Panels can be used to group sockets in a collapsible section"](https://developer.blender.org/docs/release_notes/4.0/node_editor/). Dynamo custom nodes take renamed inputs, defaults and comments that ["appear when the user hovers over an input or output"](https://primer2.dynamobim.org/6_custom_nodes_and_packages/6-1_custom-nodes/2-creating.md); n8n's sub-workflow trigger can ["define individual input names and data types"](https://docs.n8n.io/flow-logic/subworkflows/); ComfyUI (v0.3.66+) can [reorder and show or hide subgraph widgets without entering the subgraph](https://docs.comfy.org/interface/features/subgraph); VFX Graph exposes [Blackboard properties as inputs](https://docs.unity3d.com/Packages/com.unity.visualeffectgraph@17.0/manual/Subgraph.html). The pain is that generated ports carry inner names: Dynamo notes inputs are first "named based on the original nodes" ([Dynamo](https://primer2.dynamobim.org/6_custom_nodes_and_packages/6-1_custom-nodes/2-creating.md)). Flow would add an Interface panel to a sub-flow: drag to reorder, rename, set default, range, unit and tooltip, gather ports into collapsible sections, and mark a main input that receives wires dropped on the node. Cables.gl's "Manage Op" tab lets the author of a subpatch op "reorder ports, rename them or, as with any other op, clone, rename and document the op", and port moves "reflect immediately in the patch field" ([cables.gl blog, 2024](https://blog.cables.gl/february-2024-release/)).
**Status:** PARTLY: "exposed ports" · **Size:** M · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** COMPLETION (sub-flows) · **Depends on:** sub-flows; property editor; value nodes (slider ranges)

<a id="cont-08"></a>

#### CONT-08 Shared sub-flow definition (edit once, every copy updates)
With a shared definition, a sub-flow placed several times is one definition with many instances: edit any copy and all of them change, and the node shows how many copies exist. In Houdini, edits to a digital asset apply to its definition, so ["Other instances of the same asset will get the same changes"](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/assets/edit.html); Max abstractions point to a file, and ["Changes to an abstraction will modify the original file, and hence all copies of that abstraction"](https://docs.cycling74.com/userguide/subpatchers/); Node-RED subflow instances share one template, and deleting it removes ["all instances of it"](https://nodered.org/docs/user-guide/editor/workspace/subflows); Blender node groups are data-blocks placed again with [Add > Group](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/interface/controls/nodes/groups.html). ComfyUI's blueprint instances do the opposite and ["will not affect each other"](https://docs.comfy.org/interface/features/subgraph). Users want one fix to reach every use; n8n presents sub-workflows as the way to [build modular workflows](https://docs.n8n.io/flow-logic/subworkflows/). Flow already shares tools across flows ("usable as a node in another flow"). Inside one flow, Flow would make a pasted sub-flow a linked instance by default, with "Make unique" to detach, show an instance count on the node, and let editing any instance (CONT-04) change all of them as one canvas-history step. Cables.gl rebuilt its subpatches in 2024 as reusable ops so there are "No more copies and different versions of subpatches in one patch", after "plenty of feature-requests, bug-reports and general nuisances with SubPatches" ([cables.gl blog, 2024](https://blog.cables.gl/february-2024-release/)).
**Status:** PARTLY: "usable as a node in another flow", "import as a copy or a link" · **Size:** M · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** COMPLETION (sub-flows; linked flows) · **Depends on:** CONT-04 Open a sub-flow in place; canvas history; import as a link

<a id="cont-09"></a>

#### CONT-09 Sticky notes
Sticky notes are free text blocks on the canvas that explain intent, inputs and caveats. Houdini has [sticky notes with background and text colours, text size and minimize](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/network/organize.html), meant for explaining the network to others or to your future self; n8n's [sticky notes render Markdown, offer seven preset colours plus custom ones, and can be dragged behind nodes "to visually group nodes"](https://docs.n8n.io/workflows/components/sticky-notes/); Unreal has [comment boxes and node comment bubbles](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/comments-in-unreal-engine); Dynamo 2.14 lets notes be [pinned to a node and unpinned again](https://dynamobim.org/?p=53338); Substance 3D Designer lists [comments among its graph items](https://experienceleague.adobe.com/en/docs/substance-3d-designer/using/workspace/graph-view/graph-items/graph-items). The demand for notes that can be found again is visible in a 2026 Unreal plug-in that adds note and bookmark nodes with a ["Project-wide Notes Manager"](https://forums.unrealengine.com/t/z11-studio-blueprint-notes-manager-todo-bug-bookmark-system/2764031) (third party). Flow already has sticky notes (Shift+S). Possible follow-ups are pinning a note to a node so it travels with it, as Dynamo does, and listing notes in the outline panel (NAV-06).
**Status:** EXISTS · **Size:** - · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** - · **Depends on:** -

<a id="cont-10"></a>

#### CONT-10 Loop container with pass count, stop condition and limit
A loop container repeats its inner nodes, feeding each pass's output into the next, until a count or a stop condition is met. Houdini's Block End offers [Feedback Each Iteration, a Stop Condition expression and a Max Iterations safety cap](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/nodes/sop/block_end.html); Blender's [Repeat zone "executes a set of nodes multiple times", passing each iteration's outputs to the next](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/modeling/geometry_nodes/utilities/repeat_zone.html); Unreal PCG's Loop has [feedback pins where "subsequent iterations will get the data from the previous iteration"](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/procedural-content-generation-framework-node-reference-in-unreal-engine). Grasshopper needs plug-ins such as Anemone for loops, and a forum reply notes that looping plug-ins cannot be in clusters because "Clusters solve one time" ([McNeel forum, 2019](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/loop-in-grasshopper/90030)), which shows how much users want loops that compose with sub-flows. Flow already has a loop with pass count, stop expression and time limit, which covers Houdini's stop condition and iteration cap. CONT-13 (inspect one pass) completes it, and Flow should keep loops working inside sub-flows (see the do-not-copy list). Anemone, the plug-in Grasshopper users install for this, "enables you to create loops in Grasshopper" with a Loop Start and a Loop End, where "Loop End sends data back to Loop Start" ([Anemone group page](https://www.grasshopper3d.com/group/anemone), Mateusz Zwierzycki, third party).
**Status:** EXISTS · **Size:** - · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** - · **Depends on:** -

<a id="cont-11"></a>

#### CONT-11 Bypass or disable a whole group
Group actions apply a node state to every node in a group in one step, for example switching off design option B or a slow daylight analysis. ComfyUI's properties panel sets Normal, Bypass or Mute on the selection, and ["Changes apply to every item in the selection, so you can set the same state or color for a whole group at once"](https://docs.comfy.org/interface/right-side-panel); its tutorials tell users they "can use `Ctrl-B` to bypass the marked node group" and can bypass a whole group to turn the workflow into a simpler variant ([ComfyUI tutorial](https://docs.comfy.org/tutorials/flux/flux-1-uso)). Node-RED's Explorer can [enable or disable a node or a whole flow](https://nodered.org/docs/user-guide/editor/sidebar/explorer). Urban designers keep alternatives side by side in one flow and want to switch one off without hunting for each node; ComfyUI's tutorials rely on exactly this to offer variants of one workflow. Flow would let D (disable) and B (bypass) act on a selected group or from the group's title menu, apply the state to every node inside as one canvas-history step, and show it on the group header (a struck or striped title) so a collapsed group (CONT-05) still reads as off.
**Status:** PARTLY: "D disable, B bypass", "groups with title and colour" · **Size:** S · **Value:** 3 · **Kind:** COMPLETION (groups; disable and bypass) · **Depends on:** CONT-12 Group membership by enclosure; D disable; B bypass

<a id="cont-12"></a>

#### CONT-12 Group membership by enclosure, auto-fit and nesting
This decides which nodes belong to a group: a node belongs when it sits inside the box, the box grows to fit nodes dropped at its edge, and groups can sit inside groups. Blender frames add nodes with [Ctrl-P, remove them with Alt-P, and Shrink to fit their contents](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/interface/controls/nodes/types/layout/frame.html); Houdini [nests network boxes and has "Resize network boxes to fit"](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/network/organize.html); Nuke's Backdrop moves ["all the nodes that overlap the box"](https://learn.foundry.com/nuke/Content/comp_environment/organizing_scripts/grouping_nodes.html); Unreal comments have a [Group Movement mode](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/comments-in-unreal-engine); Dynamo 2.13 [adds a node by dragging it into a group and nests groups one level deep](https://dynamobim.org/dynamo-core-2-13-release/). Blender users called explicit frame parenting tedious ("wasting years of my life framing and de-framing nodes"), and one argued "We are trying to solve the issues of explicit parenting", proposing that enclosure decide membership as in Unreal ([Blender devtalk, 2023](https://devtalk.blender.org/t/testing-wanted-for-joining-nodes-into-frames/28241)). Flow would make membership follow enclosure, highlight the target group while a node is dragged, grow the group when a node is dropped on its border, allow one level of nesting for sub-stages, and remove a node when it is dragged clear of the box.
**Status:** PARTLY: "groups with title and colour", "one-step group move" · **Size:** M · **Value:** 3 · **Kind:** COMPLETION (groups) · **Depends on:** groups; one-step group move

<a id="cont-13"></a>

#### CONT-13 Inspect one pass of a loop or for-each
Pass inspection picks one iteration of a container and shows that pass's inner values and shapes, so a loop can be checked on one plot instead of all of them. Blender's Repeat zone has an Inspection Index, ["The number of the iteration to show in socket inspection and in the Viewer Node"](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/modeling/geometry_nodes/utilities/repeat_zone.html), and the For Each zone has [the same property for elements](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/modeling/geometry_nodes/utilities/for_each_geometry_zone.html); Houdini's Block End has Single Pass: ["When on, runs a single iteration at the given offset"](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/nodes/sop/block_end.html), described as a way to debug individual iterations or pieces. Without it, a wrong value for plot 37 of 200 is buried in a gathered list. Blender's 4.3 notes also warn that evaluating maths inside the zone is far slower than outside it ([Blender 4.3 release notes](https://developer.blender.org/docs/release_notes/4.3/geometry_nodes/)), so running one pass is also a speed-up while editing. Flow would add a pass picker on the container header ("Pass 37 of 200", or "District 4" with CONT-06), make port hover peeks and drawing previews inside the container show that pass, and offer "Run this pass only" while the user edits the inside.
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** M · **Value:** 3 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** loop and for-each containers; hover peek; PREV-05 Isolate one node or port

<a id="cont-14"></a>

#### CONT-14 Promote a sub-flow to a library tool
Promotion turns a sub-flow built inside one flow into a reusable library item and replaces the local copy with an instance of it. Houdini can [create a digital asset from a subnet and warns about references to nodes outside it](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/assets/create.html); ComfyUI (frontend 1.27.7+) can [publish a subgraph to the node library as a Subgraph Blueprint](https://docs.comfy.org/interface/features/subgraph); Unreal can [Collapse to Function, callable from other Blueprints](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/collapsing-graphs-in-unreal-engine); Dynamo [creates a custom node from a selection with a name, description and category](https://primer2.dynamobim.org/6_custom_nodes_and_packages/6-1_custom-nodes/2-creating.md); n8n has [Sub-workflow conversion of selected nodes](https://docs.n8n.io/flow-logic/subworkflows/). The useful piece of a flow is usually discovered after it was built, and these vendors made the step from local piece to shared tool a single command, as their docs above show. Flow already lets a whole flow become a tool with its own command id; Flow would add "Save sub-flow as tool..." to the sub-flow menu, asking for name, keywords and icon, warning about references to things outside the sub-flow (as Houdini does), saving an .urbflow to the library and replacing the local sub-flow with a linked instance. Natron's Group node has an "Export as Python plug-in" button that writes a PyPlug which, after a restart, appears in the left toolbar and the node graph's Tab menu like a built-in node ([Natron 2.5 docs](https://natron.readthedocs.io/en/rb-2.5/devel/groups.html)). GenerativeComponents' Generate Node Type tool turns chosen nodes into a new node type with named, typed input and output ports (optional, with initial values), stores it in the design file or the GC environment, lists it under Generated in Node Types and can replace the original nodes with an instance of it ([GC Help v6](https://docs.bentley.com/LiveContent/web/GenerativeComponents%20For%20BuildingDesigner%20Help-v6/en/GcGenerateNodeTypeTool.html)).
**Status:** PARTLY: "A flow can expose a slider and become a tool with its own command id", "Flows save to a library folder (.urbflow)" · **Size:** M · **Value:** 3 · **Kind:** COMPLETION (flow as a tool; library) · **Depends on:** CONT-07 Sub-flow interface editor; library folder; import as a link

### Navigation and large graphs (NAV)

14 features: 4 EXISTS, 2 PARTLY, 8 MISSING.

<a id="nav-01"></a>

#### NAV-01 Find in flow (Ctrl+F) by name, value or warning
Find locates nodes already on the canvas by name, type, port label, value or warning text, lists the matches and jumps to each. Blender's [Find Node (Ctrl-F) searches by name, socket label, warning messages or certain socket values and centres the view on the match](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/interface/controls/nodes/selecting.html); Houdini's [Find dialog (Ctrl+F) filters by name, type, parameters, flags, errors and note text, and jumps on double-click](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/network/find.html); Unreal's [Find in Blueprints searches nodes, pins, pin values and comments](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/searching-in-blueprints-in-unreal-engine); Node-RED's search ["indexes all properties of the nodes"](https://nodered.org/docs/user-guide/editor/workspace/search) with a toolbar to step through results; Grasshopper 1 has a [canvas Find dialog](https://developer.rhino3d.com/api/grasshopper/html/T_Grasshopper_GUI_Canvas_GH_Canvas.htm). Flow's Tab search adds nodes but cannot find placed ones. The pain shows in the GHPick author describing "searching through the canvas to find which component was generating it" ([McNeel forum, 2026, third party](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/ghpick-pick-grasshopper-components-directly-from-the-rhino-viewport/222932)). Flow would open a Find bar on Ctrl+F, fuzzy over node names, renamed titles, group titles, note text, port values and warnings; Enter and Shift+Enter step through matches, selecting and framing each, and matches inside sub-flows show their path.
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** M · **Value:** 5 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** fuzzy search (Tab search); fit; NAV-04 for sub-flow paths

<a id="nav-02"></a>

#### NAV-02 Pan, zoom and fit
Pan, zoom and fit move the view around the canvas and frame either everything or the selection. Houdini [fits all nodes with H and the selected nodes with G](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/network/navigate.html); Substance 3D Designer ["focus on the selected node(s), or the whole graph if nothing is selected, with the F key"](https://experienceleague.adobe.com/en/docs/substance-3d-designer/using/workspace/graph-view/the-graph-view); ComfyUI has [Fit view (".") and a zoom popover from 1% to 1000%](https://docs.comfy.org/interface/canvas-controls); n8n [zooms to fit with 1](https://docs.n8n.io/build/keyboard-shortcuts.md); Dynamo has [zoom-to-fit, zoom and pan buttons in both graph and 3D views](https://primer2.dynamobim.org/3_user_interface/1-workspace.md). New users get lost on a big canvas; a 2026 beginner lesson tells students to "zoom-extents the canvas so every component fits the view" to get back on track ([Studio Matrx, third party](https://www.studiomatrx.org/students/computational-parametric-design/the-grasshopper-canvas)). Flow already has pan and zoom and fit (F, Home, A), with per-tab canvas state. Nothing needs adding here; NAV-01, NAV-04 and NAV-11 build on fit to jump to a found node, a sub-flow level or a saved place.
**Status:** EXISTS · **Size:** - · **Value:** 5 · **Kind:** - · **Depends on:** -

<a id="nav-03"></a>

#### NAV-03 Tidy (automatic layered layout)
Tidy lays nodes out automatically in layers so wires run in one direction without overlaps. Houdini has [Layout all nodes and Layout selected nodes, and A-drag to lay out a branch upstream or downstream](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/network/layout.html); Blender relies on the [Node Arrange extension, which arranges selected nodes with Ctrl+Shift+A](https://extensions.blender.org/add-ons/node-arrange/). Demand is high: that extension shows 254,885 downloads on its page, which also says "No matter the node tree complexity, nodes will be pleasingly arranged, organised, and aligned" ([Blender Extensions, third party](https://extensions.blender.org/add-ons/node-arrange/)). Flow already has tidy (layered layout) on Ctrl+L. Houdini's docs advise that layout on individual nodes or branches "will often give better results" than relaying out everything ([Houdini](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/network/layout.html)); that completion is folded into NAV-10 (tidy selection or branch, align, distribute).
**Status:** EXISTS · **Size:** - · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** - · **Depends on:** -

<a id="nav-04"></a>

#### NAV-04 Breadcrumb path bar for nested sub-flows
A path bar shows which sub-flow you are in ("Site study > Plots > Setbacks"); clicking a level goes up to it, and back and forward retrace the trip. Blender's manual says ["You can refer to the breadcrumbs in the top left corner of the node editor to see where you are in the hierarchy"](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/interface/controls/nodes/groups.html); Houdini has a [path gadget whose levels are clickable, plus Alt+Left and Alt+Right history](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/network/navigate.html); TouchDesigner shows the [network path with Back and Forward buttons](https://docs.derivative.ca/Network_Editor); ComfyUI's [navigation bar shows the current level in nested subgraphs](https://docs.comfy.org/interface/features/subgraph). Grasshopper 2 testers asked for "a visual indication we're inside one" ([McNeel forum, 2026](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/gh2-bug-cluster-editor-is-very-slow/220906)), and a new user who has opened a sub-flow needs to know how to get out. Flow would draw a path bar across the top of the canvas when inside a sub-flow or container, make each level clickable, add Alt+Left and Alt+Right history, mark read-only linked sub-flows with a lock, and tint the canvas slightly per depth; the path bar would carry accessible names like every other control.
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** S · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** CONT-04 Open a sub-flow in place

<a id="nav-05"></a>

#### NAV-05 Select upstream or downstream and dim the rest
This extends the selection to everything that feeds, or is fed by, the selected nodes, and optionally fades everything else so one chain stands out. Blender has [Select Linked From (L), which "Expands the selection to all nodes connected to the inputs of the selected nodes", and Select Linked To (Shift-L)](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/interface/controls/nodes/selecting.html); Node-RED selects [upstream or downstream nodes with Shift-click on a node's left or right side, or Alt-s u and Alt-s d](https://nodered.org/docs/user-guide/editor/workspace/selection); n8n has [Shift+Arrow to "select all nodes left of the current one"](https://docs.n8n.io/build/keyboard-shortcuts.md) or right of it; Dynamo [draws a selected node's wires in the same aqua highlight](https://primer2.dynamobim.org/4_nodes_and_wires.md). "What feeds this number?" is the first question in a borrowed flow, and Dynamo 2.13 added [ghosted display of hidden wires when a connected node is selected](https://dynamobim.org/dynamo-core-2-13-release/) to answer it. Flow would add L and Shift+L (Blender's keys) to extend the selection upstream and downstream, and a Focus toggle that fades nodes and wires outside the selected chain without changing the flow; it pairs with "Explain this number", which works from the drawing side. Modo 12.2 added Show Connected Nodes: "You can now isolate a nodal network based on the selection of a single node" ([Modo 12.2v1 release notes, 2018, old](https://learn.foundry.com/modo/content/release_notes/12/modo_12.2v1_releasenotes.html)).
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** S · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** rectangle selection; lineage

<a id="nav-06"></a>

#### NAV-06 Outline panel of groups, notes, sub-flows and nodes
An outline panel lists the flow as a tree (groups, then sub-flows, notes and nodes), filterable, where a click frames the item on the canvas. Node-RED's [Explorer sidebar gives an outline of flows and nodes, searchable with the main search syntax, with buttons to reveal an item in the workspace and to enable or disable it](https://nodered.org/docs/user-guide/editor/sidebar/explorer); Unreal's [Bookmarks window lists bookmarks and Comment nodes, and double-click jumps to them](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/unreal-engine/working-with-bookmarks-for-blueprint-graphs-in-unreal-engine); ComfyUI's Nodes tab ["lists every node in the workflow with its inputs, searchable and collapsible"](https://docs.comfy.org/interface/right-side-panel). The demand shows in a 2026 Unreal plug-in whose central manager lets users "search every note across your project" and "Double-click to open and jump directly to the related node" ([Epic forums, third party](https://forums.unrealengine.com/t/z11-studio-blueprint-notes-manager-todo-bug-bookmark-system/2764031)). Flow would add an Outline side panel: groups in canvas order, their sub-flows and nodes beneath, notes by first line, exposed sliders and nodes with errors flagged; a filter box; click to frame, double-click to open a sub-flow. Because every control already has an accessible name, the outline would also give screen-reader users a structured way through the flow.
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** M · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** groups; sticky notes; accessible names; NAV-01 Find in flow

<a id="nav-07"></a>

#### NAV-07 Minimap
A minimap is a small overview of the whole canvas with the visible area marked; clicking or dragging it moves the view. Houdini [shows a small schematic in the bottom-left corner with O, and it can appear when needed, always or never](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/network/navigate.html); ComfyUI has a [minimap on Alt+Shift+M whose settings control whether it draws node colours, links, groups, bypass state and error state](https://docs.comfy.org/interface/canvas-controls); Node-RED's [view navigator "provides a scaled down view of the entire workspace, highlighting the area currently visible"](https://nodered.org/docs/user-guide/editor/workspace/). The evidence here is vendor docs rather than user requests: Node-RED explains that dragging the navigator helps reach nodes near the edges of a large workspace ([Node-RED](https://nodered.org/docs/user-guide/editor/workspace/)). Flow already has a minimap (O). A small completion, taken from ComfyUI's settings, would be drawing group colours and error states on the minimap so problems are visible from the overview.
**Status:** EXISTS · **Size:** - · **Value:** 3 · **Kind:** - · **Depends on:** -

<a id="nav-08"></a>

#### NAV-08 Keyboard moves between connected nodes
Keyboard moves shift the selection along the chain to the next input or output node without using the mouse. Houdini uses [PgUp for the input node and PgDn for the output node](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/network/navigate.html); n8n uses [arrow keys to "select node left of the current one" or right of it, and up or down for siblings](https://docs.n8n.io/build/keyboard-shortcuts.md). The evidence is vendor docs (no user thread found): both treat keyboard travel as a standard way through a graph, and it is the basis for keyboard-only and screen-reader use. Flow already has arrows between nodes and Enter to the property editor, plus accessible names on every control. NAV-09 would extend it so that a key can also follow a specific wire when a port has several.
**Status:** EXISTS · **Size:** - · **Value:** 3 · **Kind:** - · **Depends on:** -

<a id="nav-09"></a>

#### NAV-09 Follow a wire to its other end
Following a wire shows where it comes from and goes to and jumps the view to the other end, which matters when the ends are screens apart. Houdini lets you [right-click a wire and choose Frame input or Frame output](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/network/navigate.html); Dynamo 2.13's wire hover preview [shows the source node, the destination node and a truncated preview of the data](https://dynamobim.org/dynamo-core-2-13-release/). Dynamo's release notes present this, with new wire selection options, as a way to move through a graph more quickly ([Dynamo 2.13](https://dynamobim.org/dynamo-core-2-13-release/)). Flow would show "from Node A, port x, to Node B, port y" when hovering a wire, add Frame source and Frame target to the wire's context menu, and on a port with many wires list the targets so the arrow keys can step through them; the jump uses the existing fit. In Modo's Schematic, a connector whose linked node is outside the view is drawn "with a yellow outline", and double-clicking it makes the connected element visible ([Modo 17.1 docs](https://learn.foundry.com/modo/content/help/pages/animation/schematic_view.html)).
**Status:** PARTLY: "arrows between nodes" · **Size:** S · **Value:** 3 · **Kind:** COMPLETION (keyboard arrows between nodes) · **Depends on:** arrows between nodes; fit

<a id="nav-10"></a>

#### NAV-10 Align and distribute selected nodes
Align and distribute line selected nodes up on an edge or centre and space them evenly; a related tool tidies only the selection or one branch. Houdini has [Align nodes horizontally or vertically, Distribute nodes, and Shift+A or Alt+A drags to align or distribute around one node](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/network/layout.html); Node-RED has [Alt-a with l, r, t, b, m or c to align and Alt-a h or v to distribute](https://nodered.org/docs/user-guide/editor/workspace/arrange). Houdini's docs advise that layout on individual nodes or branches "will often give better results" than a full relayout ([Houdini](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/network/layout.html)), and Blender users install a layout extension in large numbers ([Node Arrange, third party, 254,885 downloads](https://extensions.blender.org/add-ons/node-arrange/)). Flow would add Align (left, centre, right, top, middle, bottom) and Distribute to the selection's context menu and the toolbar, and make Ctrl+L with a selection tidy only the selection, keeping everything else where the user put it. In Grasshopper, David Rutten answered a grid-snap request with "What is needed I think is an object snap" ([McNeel forum, 2017, old](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/wish-list-grid-snap-for-grasshopper-canvas/41169)), and WeArk's SnappingGecko plug-in "provides object snapping features that help you keep definitions tidy" (translated from [McNeel's Korean blog, 2018, old](https://blog.kr.rhino3d.com/2018/08/snappinggecko-jumpingfrogrobot-weark.html)), but a 2025 user found it shifted components inside groups a little on every move until they drifted out of alignment ([McNeel forum, 2025](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/snapping-gecko-alternative/203106)).
**Status:** PARTLY: "tidy (layered layout)" · **Size:** S · **Value:** 3 · **Kind:** COMPLETION (tidy) · **Depends on:** tidy; rectangle selection

<a id="nav-11"></a>

#### NAV-11 Canvas bookmarks and quick views
Bookmarks save places on the canvas (position, zoom and sub-flow) and jump back to them by key or from a list. Houdini has [quick marks on Ctrl+1 to 5 that "are not saved with the scene file", and bookmarks that "are saved with the scene file"](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/network/navigate.html); Unreal has [named bookmarks plus Ctrl+0-9 to store and Shift+0-9 to return](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/unreal-engine/working-with-bookmarks-for-blueprint-graphs-in-unreal-engine); Nuke has a [bookmark flag and a J jump menu, with Backdrops bookmarked by default](https://learn.foundry.com/nuke/8.0/content/getting_started/using_interface/bookmarking_nodes.html) (Nuke 8.0 docs, old); Substance 3D Designer has [navigation pins on F2](https://experienceleague.adobe.com/en/docs/substance-3d-designer/using/workspace/graph-view/the-graph-view); TouchDesigner has [bookmarks saved in the project](https://docs.derivative.ca/Bookmark). A 2026 Unreal plug-in adds BOOKMARK nodes with a central list ([Epic forums, third party](https://forums.unrealengine.com/t/z11-studio-blueprint-notes-manager-todo-bug-bookmark-system/2764031)), showing the demand. Flow would treat every group title as an automatic bookmark (as Nuke does with Backdrops), list them in a J jump menu with fuzzy filter, and add numbered views on Ctrl+Alt+digit (store) and Alt+digit (recall), saved with the flow; bare digits stay free for typed numbers.
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** S · **Value:** 3 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** groups; fit; per-tab canvas state

<a id="nav-12"></a>

#### NAV-12 Zoom-dependent detail (semantic zoom)
Semantic zoom shows different detail at different zoom levels: large titles and status marks far out, extra controls close in. Grasshopper's zoomable interface reveals variable-parameter controls when you zoom in; ["The interface appears when zoomed in on a component that supports variable parameters"](https://www.grasshopper3d.com/video/variable-parameter-zui) (2011, old), with a ["global Zui zoom threshold"](https://developer.rhino3d.com/api/grasshopper/html/P_Grasshopper_CentralSettings_CanvasZuiZoomLevel.htm) in the SDK; Dynamo 2.14's [Zoom States show node states as glyphs and colour overlays past a zoom threshold](https://dynamobim.org/?p=53338); ComfyUI [switches to simplified rendering below a font-size threshold](https://docs.comfy.org/interface/settings/lite-graph); Unreal's ["The text of comment boxes will scale as the graph is zoomed"](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/comments-in-unreal-engine). Dynamo built Zoom States so errors and warnings stay visible on a zoomed-out graph ([Dynamo 2.14 release notes](https://dynamobim.org/?p=53338)). Flow would use three bands: far, where group titles grow and node errors, disabled and bypassed states show as coloured marks; middle, as today; near, where inline value fields and add-port buttons appear on nodes. The 60 fps budget for 1,000 nodes is the constraint to design within. In Grasshopper, the Sunglasses plug-in draws each component's name when icons are shown and "enriches the capsules when you zoom in to understand the component and visualise its data at a quick glance"; one reply found it "could be handy for teaching" and another said it "could perfectly replace" the Data Viewer ([McNeel forum, 2021, old, third party plug-in](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/sunglasses-gh-plugin-to-display-object-names/115550)).
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** M · **Value:** 3 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** groups; node states (errors, disabled, bypassed)

<a id="nav-13"></a>

#### NAV-13 Search across sub-flows and the library (where used)
This widens Find beyond the open canvas to every nested sub-flow and to every flow in the library, and answers "where is this tool used?". Unreal's Find in Blueprints searches the current Blueprint by default, and unchecking "Find in Current Blueprint Only" searches all Blueprints in the project, after a slow indexing step ([Unreal](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/searching-in-blueprints-in-unreal-engine)); Houdini's Find has a Search child networks option, and ["Normally, the dialog does not search inside locked assets"](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/network/find.html); Node-RED's search has [is:subflow and is:unused filters](https://nodered.org/docs/user-guide/editor/workspace/search). Unreal also offers Find References to search for one specific element ([Unreal](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/searching-in-blueprints-in-unreal-engine)), which is the "where used" question. Flow would add a scope switch to Find (this canvas, this flow with its sub-flows, the whole library folder) and a "Where used" command on a tool that lists the flows placing it, including linked ones; results open the flow and frame the node.
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** M · **Value:** 2 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** NAV-01 Find in flow; library folder; linked flows

<a id="nav-14"></a>

#### NAV-14 Split view of one flow
A split view shows two canvases of the same flow side by side, for example the parent and the inside of a sub-flow, or the inputs and the outputs of a long flow. TouchDesigner's window ["can be split into 2 or more panes"](https://docs.derivative.ca/Pane); each pane has its own network path in its Pane Bar, and a control can link one pane to another ([TouchDesigner](https://docs.derivative.ca/Pane)). No user request for this was found in the forums opened for this study; TouchDesigner's pane layouts are the main evidence that node users work this way, so the value is rated low. Flow would let the flow window split into two canvases on the same flow, each with its own view (and sub-flow level once CONT-04 exists), sharing one selection, so wiring between distant parts or editing a sub-flow while watching its parent does not need constant panning.
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** M · **Value:** 2 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** per-tab canvas state; CONT-04 Open a sub-flow in place

### Previews in the drawing and inspection (PREV)

11 features: 3 EXISTS, 4 PARTLY, 4 MISSING.

<a id="prev-01"></a>

#### PREV-01 Live results in the drawing on the flow's own layer (Show and Select pills)
Live preview draws each node's results in the CAD drawing as the flow runs, without making real objects. Dynamo shows geometry in a [3D background preview, switched with the graph view by Ctrl+B](https://primer2.dynamobim.org/3_user_interface/1-workspace.md); Houdini's display flag ["marks the node whose geometry appears in the 3D viewer"](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/network/flags.html); Grasshopper previews results in Rhino in "a soft green (selected components preview brighter)" ([Studio Matrx, third party](https://www.studiomatrx.org/students/computational-parametric-design/the-grasshopper-canvas)). Users depend on preview so much that controlling it is a recurring forum topic; one asks to "preview selected components regardless of the component's preview setting" ([McNeel forum, 2020 to 2026](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/grasshopper-preview-selected/107427)). Flow already shows results in the drawing on the flow's own layer, with Show and Select pills (drawing, flow, both), and every command previews before applying. PREV-03 to PREV-07 refine which results show and how.
**Status:** EXISTS · **Size:** - · **Value:** 5 · **Kind:** - · **Depends on:** -

<a id="prev-02"></a>

#### PREV-02 Bake results to a layer
Bake copies previewed results into real drawing objects. Grasshopper bakes into Rhino; McNeel's [Rhino 8 baking guide](https://www.rhino3d.com/docs/guides/grasshopper/baking/) notes that before its Content Cache component, baking was manual or needed plug-ins such as Elefront; Elefront lets users ["bake geometry to the Rhino model with the option of specifying attributes"](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/elefront-for-grasshopper/1026) (McNeel forum announcement, 2013, old). Users treat baking as essential: "I think such an important task as baking shouldn't be resolved by 3-rd party plugins" ([McNeel forum, 2020](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/expanded-baking-options-in-vanilla-gh/114125)). Flow already has Bake, which copies results to a layer, so the result becomes ordinary drawing content. CAD-01 completes it with updating the previous bake instead of adding duplicates, and PREV-06 and PREV-07 would let colours and labels bake too.
**Status:** EXISTS · **Size:** - · **Value:** 5 · **Kind:** - · **Depends on:** -

<a id="prev-03"></a>

#### PREV-03 Per-node preview on and off
A per-node preview toggle decides whether one node's results draw, so intermediate steps stop cluttering the drawing. Dynamo marks a node whose geometry preview is switched off [with a gray status bar and an eye icon](https://primer2.dynamobim.org/4_nodes_and_wires.md); Houdini sets per-node [display and template flags](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/network/flags.html); TouchDesigner toggles a node between icon and viewer with [the viewer flag](https://docs.derivative.ca/Node_Viewer); Grasshopper components each have preview on or off, and a user complains: "In larger files, switching between hidden and visible component states becomes cumbersome" ([McNeel forum, 2024](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/grasshopper-disable-preview/180882)). New users are confused when every intermediate offset and copy draws on top of the result. Flow's Show pills act on the whole flow; Flow would add an eye toggle on each node header and on each group header (one click for the whole group), and default new flows to previewing only nodes at the end of a chain, with intermediate nodes hidden until the user turns them on.
**Status:** PARTLY: "Show and Select pills: drawing, flow, both" · **Size:** S · **Value:** 5 · **Kind:** COMPLETION (Show pills) · **Depends on:** flow's own layer; Show pills

<a id="prev-04"></a>

#### PREV-04 Highlight the selected node's results and frame them
Selecting a node draws its results in a highlight colour, even when its preview is off, and a key zooms the drawing to them. Dynamo: ["Selected Nodes will render the corresponding geometry (if the Node creates geometry) in the background the highlight color"](https://primer2.dynamobim.org/5_essential_nodes_and_concepts/5-2_geometry-for-computational-design/1-geometry-overview); Grasshopper draws selected components' previews brighter ([Studio Matrx, third party](https://www.studiomatrx.org/students/computational-parametric-design/the-grasshopper-canvas)); Houdini's template flag keeps other geometry visible ["even if the node doesn't have the display flag on"](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/network/flags.html). Grasshopper users ask to "preview selected components regardless of the component's preview setting" ([McNeel forum, 2020 to 2026](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/grasshopper-preview-selected/107427)) and to see a clicked component "even if it is hidden (with another color?)" ([McNeel forum, 2023](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/grasshopper-preview-feature-request/167776)). Flow's Select pills sync selection between flow and drawing; Flow would add a distinct highlight colour (not the CAD selection colour) for the selected node's results, show them even when the node's eye is off, and add "Frame in drawing" (for example Shift+F) to zoom the drawing to them.
**Status:** PARTLY: "Show and Select pills: drawing, flow, both" · **Size:** S · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** COMPLETION (Select pills) · **Depends on:** PREV-03 Per-node preview; Select pills

<a id="prev-05"></a>

#### PREV-05 Isolate one node or port (viewer) with the rest ghosted
Isolation shows only one node's or one port's shapes in the drawing while the flow's other results draw faint for context. Blender's Viewer node: ["Use Shift-Ctrl-LMB on any node or socket to connect it to the active viewer"](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/modeling/geometry_nodes/output/viewer.html), with values also in the spreadsheet; Houdini combines the [display flag with template flags that keep other geometry visible](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/network/flags.html); Grasshopper has a selected-only preview mode, and David Rutten pointed users to the Preview Boundary tool ([McNeel forum](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/grasshopper-preview-selected/107427)); Substance 3D Designer shows a node's output in its 2D view on [double-click](https://experienceleague.adobe.com/en/docs/substance-3d-designer/using/workspace/graph-view/the-graph-view). Users want isolation with context: the same thread proposes a middle level between "all" and "selected only", and in 2026 a third-party plug-in added a "follow selection" mode ([McNeel forum, 2020 to 2026](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/grasshopper-preview-selected/107427)). Flow would add Isolate on any node or port (button on the port peek, or Ctrl+Shift+click): that port's shapes draw in full colour, other flow results draw ghosted, and Esc returns to normal; isolating a port inside a container uses the pass picked in CONT-13.
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** M · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** PREV-03 Per-node preview; PREV-04 Highlight; flow's own layer

<a id="prev-06"></a>

#### PREV-06 Colour by value with a legend (thematic preview)
Thematic preview colours a port's shapes by a number or a category, with a ramp or palette and class breaks, and shows a legend in the drawing: plots by floor area ratio, blocks by land use. Houdini's Color visualizer works by ["mapping attribute values to a color ramp"](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/basics/visualizers.html), per node or for the scene; Dynamo uses [Color Range with Display.ByGeometryColor, which maps numbers from 0 to 1 onto a gradient](https://primer2.dynamobim.org/5_essential_nodes_and_concepts/5-3_the-building-blocks-of-programs/5-color.md) but does not describe a legend; Grasshopper's [Custom Preview takes geometry and a material](https://grasshopperdocs.com/components/grasshopperdisplay/customPreview.html) (third-party docs site). Doing it today is hard: a building-heights heatmap question was answered with C# code setting mesh vertex colours ([McNeel forum, 2021](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/building-heights-heatmap/115797)), and legends need the native legend, the Human plug-in or PDF+ ([McNeel forum, 2025](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/how-to-make-a-legend/203191)). Flow would add "Colour by" on any output port: pick an attribute or port value, a ramp or category palette and breaks; the preview draws on the flow's own layer with a legend docked in the drawing, and Bake can carry the colours and the legend. Ladybug, the Grasshopper environmental-analysis plug-in, has a Spatial Heatmap component to "Color a mesh as a heatmap using values that align with the mesh faces or vertices", with a legend output ([Ladybug Primer: Spatial Heatmap](https://docs.ladybug.tools/ladybug-primer/components/4_extra/spatial_heatmap), plug-in docs), and a Legend Parameters component that sets "the colors, numerical range, and/or number of divisions of any Ladybug legend" ([Ladybug Primer: Legend Parameters](https://docs.ladybug.tools/ladybug-primer/components/4_extra/legend_parameters)), yet a user who wanted unequal class breaks was told "Ladybug doesn't support unequal intervals" ([Ladybug forum, 2018, old](https://discourse.ladybug.tools/t/legendpar-unequal-intervals/2938)). In Grasshopper 2 "Metadata can be visualized in the Rhino viewports using custom display rules", which "assign properties like colors or dash patterns to shapes with metadata" ([McNeel, Grasshopper 2 page, undated](https://www.rhino3d.com/stories/grasshopper-2/)), and David Rutten wrote that "Display Settings and Display Rules have been completely reworked" in the February 2025 alpha ([McNeel forum, 2025](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/new-grasshopper-2-0-alpha-2025-feb/199833)).
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** M · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** flow's own layer; attribute key; Bake

<a id="prev-07"></a>

#### PREV-07 Value labels in the drawing (measurement overlays)
Value labels show a port's values as preview-only text on each shape: area on each plot, GFA on each block, coverage per lot. Houdini's Marker visualizer ["Displays text next to points in the viewport, for example attribute values"](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/basics/visualizers.html); Grasshopper's Text Tag 3D ["Represents a list of 3D text tags in a Rhino viewport"](https://grasshopperdocs.com/components/grasshopperdisplay/textTag3D.html) (third-party docs site); Blender's Viewer shows field values in the viewport through the [Viewer Overlay](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/modeling/geometry_nodes/output/viewer.html). Urban flows exist to produce numbers per shape; the site-coverage asker needed a coverage percentage for each of hundreds of lots ([McNeel forum, 2018, old](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/site-coverage-map-for-numerous-lots-help/62438)), and reading them from a list is error-prone. Flow would add a Label toggle on any output port: each item's value appears at its shape's centre on the flow's own layer, with unit, rounding and text size; labels are preview-only until baked, and clicking one opens "Explain this number" for that figure.
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** M · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** flow's own layer; Explain this number; Bake

<a id="prev-09"></a>

#### PREV-09 Pick a shape in the drawing to jump to its node
Picking goes from the drawing to the canvas: click a previewed shape and the node that made it is selected, revealed and framed. The only shipping example found is GHPick, a free Grasshopper plug-in where Alt+Click on preview geometry lists candidate components, highlights each on hover, and jumps to the chosen one on the canvas ([McNeel forum, 2026, third party](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/ghpick-pick-grasshopper-components-directly-from-the-rhino-viewport/222932)). The wish is older: "It would be nice to be able to click on the Rhino preview to find the component that generates it" ([McNeel forum, 2021](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/grasshopper-ui-wishlist/122573)), and GHPick's author describes the pain as "searching through the canvas to find which component was generating it". Flow already shows a Made-by badge on hover and can reach the node through "Explain this number". Flow would add "Show in flow" on the badge (and a modifier-click on any flow result) that brings the flow window forward, opens the sub-flow path that contains the node (CONT-04, NAV-04), selects it and frames it; if several nodes share the spot, a short list appears.
**Status:** PARTLY: "a Made-by badge on hover", "Explain this number ... down to the shapes and the node that made them" · **Size:** S · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** COMPLETION (Made-by badge) · **Depends on:** Made-by badge; CONT-04 Open a sub-flow in place; NAV-04 Breadcrumb path bar

<a id="prev-10"></a>

#### PREV-10 Made-by badge on hover
The Made-by badge names, on hover in the drawing, the node that produced a flow result. No studied program ships the same thing natively; the closest is GHPick, a 2026 Grasshopper plug-in that highlights candidate geometry on hover and lists the components that made it ([McNeel forum, third party](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/ghpick-pick-grasshopper-components-directly-from-the-rhino-viewport/222932)). Users have asked for this link from drawing to node for years: "It would be nice to be able to click on the Rhino preview to find the component that generates it" ([McNeel forum, 2021](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/grasshopper-ui-wishlist/122573)). Flow already has a Made-by badge on hover over results, plus lineage on every result and "Explain this number". PREV-09 turns the badge into a jump to the node, and PREV-04 gives the reverse direction from node to shapes.
**Status:** EXISTS · **Size:** - · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** - · **Depends on:** -

<a id="prev-11"></a>

#### PREV-11 Preview colour per node or group
A per-node or per-group preview colour lets option A draw blue and option B orange, so alternatives read apart in the drawing. Grasshopper's Custom Preview takes ["The material override"](https://grasshopperdocs.com/components/grasshopperdisplay/customPreview.html) (third-party docs site); Houdini's [node visualizers apply only to the geometry from one node and are saved on the node](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/basics/visualizers.html); Dynamo colours geometry with [Display.ByGeometryColor](https://primer2.dynamobim.org/5_essential_nodes_and_concepts/5-3_the-building-blocks-of-programs/5-color.md). Users ask for "custom preview colors for every component" ([McNeel forum, 2021](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/grasshopper-ui-wishlist/122573)), and another describes hiding every component just to see swatch colours ([McNeel forum, 2024](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/grasshopper-disable-preview/180882)). Flow would add a colour swatch to node and group headers; a group's results can take the group's canvas colour ("Preview in group colour"), linking canvas and drawing for new users. Downstream colours win over upstream, and nodes without a colour keep the default preview style.
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** S · **Value:** 3 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** flow's own layer; groups with colour

<a id="prev-12"></a>

#### PREV-12 Thumbnails of results on the node
On-node thumbnails draw a small picture of a node's result on the node itself, so a flow can be read without looking at the drawing. Unity Shader Graph nodes have a preview that ["displays the main output value at that stage in the graph"](https://docs.unity3d.com/Packages/com.unity.shadergraph@17.0/manual/Node.html), with a Collapse control; Unreal's Material Editor has All Node Previews, which recompile intermediate shaders after every change, so large graphs can slow down ([Unreal](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/unreal-engine-material-editor-user-guide)); Substance 3D Designer can [compute node thumbnails from each node's first output](https://experienceleague.adobe.com/en/docs/substance-3d-designer/using/workspace/graph-view/the-graph-view); TouchDesigner's [viewer flag switches a node between icon and viewer, with a "Viewer On by Default" preference](https://docs.derivative.ca/Node_Viewer). The evidence is vendor docs; no user thread for CAD-style thumbnails was found, so value is moderate. Flow already peeks the first items on hover; Flow would add an optional plan thumbnail (top view of the node's shapes, fitted to the box) per node, off by default, drawn from the existing preview and skipped while zoomed out to protect the 60 fps budget.
**Status:** PARTLY: "Output ports show count badges and a hover peek of the first items" · **Size:** M · **Value:** 3 · **Kind:** COMPLETION (hover peek) · **Depends on:** hover peek; PREV-03 Per-node preview

### Parameters and user interfaces (PARAM)

14 features: 2 EXISTS, 7 PARTLY, 5 MISSING.

<a id="param-01"></a>

#### PARAM-01 Player: run a library flow from a simple form
A player is a run panel for people who do not author flows: it lists the saved flows in a folder, shows each flow's exposed inputs as a form, runs it, previews the result and lets the user keep or discard it, all without opening the canvas. Revit's [Dynamo Player](https://help.autodesk.com/cloudhelp/2026/ENU/Revit-HaveYouTried/files/GUID-E8205058-9CA4-4CFF-95F5-A6AA1CDA7346.htm) runs graphs "through an easy-to-navigate dialog", ships sample graphs, manages folder locations and can show thumbnails, author and a documentation link; only nodes flagged [Is Input](https://help.autodesk.com/cloudhelp/2022/ENU/Alias-NURBS-Modeling/files/Generate-designs-with-Dynamo/Alias-Dynamo-Player/GUID-7C9864A5-5738-44F2-A455-F989863043F9.html) appear. [Dynamo in Forma](https://primer2.dynamobim.org/dynamo-in-forma-beta/run-graphs-in-dynamo-player) adds Is Output results, a preview eye and Add. [ComfyUI App Mode](https://docs.comfy.org/interface/app-mode.md) hides the graph and shows only the author's chosen inputs with Run and Cancel; [n8n's Form Trigger](https://docs.n8n.io/integrations/builtin/core-nodes/n8n-nodes-base.formtrigger/) generates a form page that starts a workflow. The demand is clear: ComfyUI built App Mode because node graphs are "notorious for being difficult for beginners" ([Gigazine, 2026, third party](https://www.gigazine.net/gsc_news/en/20260312-comfyui-app-mode)), and a Rhino user needed a definition for a client who "isn't Grasshopper-savvy" ([forum, 2021](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/grasshopper-definition-to-toolbar-button/135082)). Flow would add a Player pane: choose a library folder, see flows as cards, fill the exposed inputs, Preview, then Apply as one undo step or Bake. Grasshopper's Human UI plug-in (open source since 2016) builds separate app windows with "tabbed views, dynamic sliders, pulldown menus, checkboxes, and even 3D viewports and web browsers" ([Human UI on GitHub](https://github.com/andrewheumann/humanui), third party), interfaces "entirely separate from the Grasshopper canvas" for "designers and clients with no understanding of Grasshopper" ([McNeel forum, 2016, old](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/human-ui-interface-paradigm-for-grasshopper/29976)), and one user's Human UI front end has "around 150 parameters" ([McNeel forum, 2022](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/interacitve-gui-human-ui-and-shapediver-etc/140623)).
**Status:** PARTLY: "A flow can expose a slider and become a tool with its own command id, on the rail and in search"; "Flows save to a library folder (.urbflow)" · **Size:** M · **Value:** 5 · **Kind:** COMPLETION · **Depends on:** PARAM-03 Expose any value as a tool input; LIB-06 Item details; Bake; run modes

<a id="param-02"></a>

#### PARAM-02 Run-time prompts for picks and values
Run-time prompts let a flow-tool ask for its inputs the way a normal CAD command does: "Select the site boundary", then "Setback distance <6>" typed at the command line, with Enter keeping the default and Undo stepping back. Rhino's [Grasshopper Player](https://www.rhino3d.com/features/grasshopper/player/) brings "inputs/outputs from Grasshopper to the Rhino Command Line"; McNeel staff explain that authors add "Get" components from Params > Util and a Context Bake ([forum, 2021](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/grasshopper-definition-to-toolbar-button/135082)), and Get String, Get Integer and Get Number keep preset values that show on the command line ([forum, 2022](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/grasshopper-player-custom-tools-wish-list/114844?page=9)). [Dynamo Player](https://help.autodesk.com/cloudhelp/2022/ENU/Alias-NURBS-Modeling/files/Generate-designs-with-Dynamo/Alias-Dynamo-Player/GUID-7C9864A5-5738-44F2-A455-F989863043F9.html) offers selection inputs with geometry-type filters, and [Dynamo in Forma](https://primer2.dynamobim.org/dynamo-in-forma-beta/run-graphs-in-dynamo-player) switches to a selection mode where the user picks and confirms. Users keep asking for polish: an Undo option on Get Integer and Enter to accept a predefined number ([wish-list thread, 2020](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/grasshopper-player-custom-tools-wish-list/114844)), and in 2025 a report that a Get Point "drops" the selection ([same thread](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/grasshopper-player-custom-tools-wish-list/114844?page=9)). Flow would let any exposed source (selection, layer, pasted shapes) or value be marked "ask when run", so a flow-tool started from the rail prompts in order, filters by shape type, offers defaults, Enter and Undo, then runs and applies as one step.
**Status:** PARTLY: "sources (a layer, the selection, ...)"; "become a tool with its own command id" · **Size:** M · **Value:** 5 · **Kind:** COMPLETION · **Depends on:** PARAM-03 Expose any value as a tool input; flow-as-tool (PARAM-14)

<a id="param-03"></a>

#### PARAM-03 Expose any value as a tool input, with label, tooltip and default
