Today a flow can expose a slider; this feature lets an author expose any value or source (text, choice, true/false, number, a layer or selection) as a tool input with a label, tooltip, default, unit and place in the list. [Blender](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/interface/controls/nodes/groups.html) node group inputs carry a Description shown as the tooltip, Default, Min, Max, Subtype and "Hide in Modifier"; [Unreal Blueprint](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/blueprint-variables-in-unreal-engine) variables have Instance Editable, Tooltip, Category and Expose on Spawn; the [Unity Shader Graph Blackboard](https://docs.unity3d.com/Packages/com.unity.shadergraph@17.0/manual/Blackboard.html) marks properties Exposed and sorts them in categories; [Nuke's User Knob Editor](https://learn.foundry.com/nuke/content/comp_environment/configuring_nuke/managing_controls.html) gives each knob a Label and Tooltip; [Archicad PARAM-O](https://help.graphisoft.com/AC/29/INT/_AC29_Help/065_PARAM-O/065_PARAM-O-7.htm) sets "Create Parameter" to true and names the parameter for the Object Settings dialog; a [Marionette object](https://app-help.vectorworks.net/2024/eng/VW2024_Guide/Marionette/Step_5_Create_a_Marionette_object.htm) shows its parameters in the Object Info palette. A Grasshopper user asked for value lists and input components to be publishable because "a lot of components could be really handy" if they could be ([forum, 2020](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/publishing-to-remote-panel/102747)). Flow would add "Expose as input" on every value and source node, plus an inputs list in the flow's properties where the author edits label, tooltip, default, unit and order; the tool, the inputs panel and the Player all read this one list.
**Status:** PARTLY: "A flow can expose a slider and become a tool" · **Size:** M · **Value:** 5 · **Kind:** COMPLETION · **Depends on:** values (number, slider, text, choice, bool); sub-flows with exposed ports; flow-as-tool (PARAM-14)

<a id="param-04"></a>

#### PARAM-04 Slider settings: whole, even, odd, step, limits and units
Slider settings make a number input behave like the quantity it controls: decimal or whole numbers, even-only or odd-only, a step, a soft range for dragging with a hard limit for typed values, and a unit (metres, per cent, degrees) with display precision. Grasshopper's slider has four accuracies, [Float, Integer, Even and Odd](https://developer.rhino3d.com/api/grasshopper/html/T_Grasshopper_GUI_Base_GH_SliderAccuracy.htm); [Blender](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/interface/controls/nodes/groups.html) inputs have a Subtype such as Percentage, Factor or Angle, and Min/Max that limit only the control; [TouchDesigner](https://docs.derivative.ca/Custom_Parameters) separates the slider range (normMin/normMax) from clamped limits; [Unreal](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/blueprint-variables-in-unreal-engine) numeric variables can have a Slider Range. The pain shows where this is loose: [Dynamo Player](https://help.autodesk.com/cloudhelp/2022/ENU/Alias-NURBS-Modeling/files/Generate-designs-with-Dynamo/Alias-Dynamo-Player/GUID-7C9864A5-5738-44F2-A455-F989863043F9.html) does not support slider step values, so authors add a code block such as x*0.5; an expression on a Rhino 8 integer slider gave 5.3999 instead of 5.4 and was logged as a bug ([forum, 2024](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/slider-with-expression-in-rh8/175736)); and a range that depends on another slider has needed a workaround since 2012 ([forum, 2012, old](https://www.grasshopper3d.com/forum/topics/parametric-sliders)). Flow would extend its slider with a number kind (decimal, whole, even, odd), step, soft and hard limits, the drawing's unit and precision, and an optional range driven by other inputs, all set in the property editor.
**Status:** PARTLY: "values (number, slider, ...)" · **Size:** S · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** COMPLETION · **Depends on:** values (slider); expressions; property editor

<a id="param-05"></a>

#### PARAM-05 Flow inputs panel
An inputs panel gathers every exposed input of the open flow in one dockable list, so the user changes values without hunting for nodes on the canvas, and the author can arrange it for a client review. Grasshopper's [Remote Control Panel](https://www.grasshopper3d.com/xn/detail/2985220:Comment:976452) (release notes, 2013, old) "supports Sliders, Toggles, Buttons and Text Panels" with groups, labels and separators set in an Edit Mode; Blender shows node group inputs in the modifier, and since 4.1 ["Node panels are now displayed in the modifier interface"](https://developer.blender.org/docs/release_notes/4.1/nodes_physics/); [ComfyUI](https://docs.comfy.org/interface/features/subgraph.md) has a parameters panel to edit subgraph widgets "without entering the subgraph"; Houdini authors build an asset's [parameter interface](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/ref/windows/optype.html), "which is how users will control the asset's options". A Rhino user wanted to set inputs and bake from the Remote Panel without opening the definition and learned that input components cannot be published there ([forum, 2020](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/publishing-to-remote-panel/102747)); ComfyUI's release notes advertise "Edit widgets without entering subgraphs" ([blog, 2025](https://blog.comfy.org/p/comfyui-0366-updates)). Flow would add an Inputs pane beside the canvas or in the flow's floating window, listing exposed inputs with their controls and following the run mode; clicking a row selects its node. It shows the same list that the tool and the Player use.
**Status:** PARTLY: "Enter to the property editor"; "A flow can expose a slider" · **Size:** M · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** COMPLETION · **Depends on:** PARAM-03 Expose any value as a tool input; floating or docked window

<a id="param-06"></a>

#### PARAM-06 Named presets of input values
Presets save a named set of input values (for example "Option A: 6 storeys, 12 m setback") and restore it in one click, so a designer can switch between options in a meeting. [Houdini](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/network/parms.html) saves presets from the gear menu: "Save Preset", then choose its name to apply it; Grasshopper's built-in state feature "allows you to save named states of all/selected sliders, boolean toggles, gene pools" ([forum, 2022](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/grasshopper-slider-states-export/149017)); Grasshopper Player's Get components hold preset values for the command line ([forum, 2022](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/grasshopper-player-custom-tools-wish-list/114844?page=9)); [Dynamo 0.8.2](https://dynamobim.org/dynamo082/) (2015, old) introduced Presets "to track the state of your input nodes". Users show demand and pain together: restoring a Grasshopper state moved the controls but "doesn't change their outputs" and could not keep panel text ([forum, 2023](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/save-and-restore-grasshopper-slider-positions/164442)), and others want states that cover every slider and can be driven by script ([forum, 2022 to 2024](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/grasshopper-slider-states-export/149017)). Flow would store presets in the .urbflow over the exposed inputs, add "Save as preset" and a preset dropdown to the inputs panel, the tool and the Player, apply a preset as one canvas-history step followed by a full re-run, and let the test bridge set presets by data.
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** M · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** PARAM-03 Expose any value as a tool input; PARAM-05 Flow inputs panel; canvas history

<a id="param-07"></a>

#### PARAM-07 Handles in the drawing bound to inputs (gizmos)
Handles (gizmos) let the user change a flow input by dragging in the drawing (an arrow for a setback, a dial for an angle, a move-and-rotate handle for a block), with number and handle kept in step both ways. Blender 4.3 added [Linear, Dial and Transform gizmo nodes](https://developer.blender.org/docs/release_notes/4.3/geometry_nodes/) that "are just attached to other already existing input values"; the [manual](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/modeling/geometry_nodes/gizmos.html) explains the "bidirectional dependency" and that gizmos on a modifier "always show when the modifier is active". Houdini assets bind handle parts to parameters in the [Handle Bindings tab](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/ref/windows/optype.html), and [handles](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/basics/handles.html) can stay visible as persistent handles. Blender users asked for more types such as box cages and 2-axis moves, and the developer considered shipping ready gizmo groups ([devtalk, 2023](https://devtalk.blender.org/t/geometry-nodes-gizmos-feedback)); Grasshopper Player users wish for "a universal slider (ideally screen aligned)" and lament "A slider that does not slide is so tragic" ([forum, 2022 to 2025](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/grasshopper-player-custom-tools-wish-list/114844?page=9)). Flow would let an exposed number bind to a handle type (distance along a direction from a point, angle about a point, move/rotate) whose anchor comes from a flow output; handles show while the flow or its tool is active, dragging updates the input with live preview, and release applies as one undo step. Antimony, an open-source node-based CAD tool now in long-term maintenance ([README](https://github.com/mkeeter/antimony)), lets node scripts draw handles in the 3D viewport, and "These UI features are hooked back into the graph", so dragging a point edits the node's inputs ([project page, 2014 to 2015, old](https://www.mattkeeter.com/projects/antimony/3/)); a reader of a 2015 review singled out its "bi-directional interaction between function nodes and the 3D UI" ([Hackaday comments, 2015, old, third party](https://hackaday.com/2015/05/29/otherworldy-cad-software-hails-from-a-parallel-universe/)).
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** L · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** PARAM-03 Expose any value as a tool input; results show in the drawing on the flow's own layer; run modes

<a id="param-08"></a>

#### PARAM-08 Option exploration and comparison
Option exploration runs a flow over many combinations of its inputs, records chosen figures for each result (GFA, height, open-space share) and shows the options side by side as thumbnails, sortable columns and a parallel-coordinates chart, so the team can filter and pick one to bake. Revit's [Generative Design](https://help.autodesk.com/cloudhelp/2025/ENU/Revit-GDiR/files/GUID-78A73C50-C13E-490E-8C3C-BD4204A0633C.htm) runs a Dynamo graph and its Explore Outcomes dialog sorts thumbnails, plots a scatterplot and parallel coordinates and filters by dragging along an axis; Houdini's [Wedge TOP](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/nodes/top/wedge.html) makes range or value-list variations to "help you choose the best looking option"; for Grasshopper, CORE studio's [Design Explorer](https://tt-acm.github.io/DesignExplorer/) is "an open source tool for exploring multi-dimensional parametric studies" (third party), and [Speckle's Design Explorer beta](https://speckle.systems/blog/design-explorer-beta-browse-compare-and-share-all-of-your-grasshopper-options-in-one-place/) adds a live thumbnail grid and detail view (third party). Speckle argues that "The hardest part of multi-option design isn't generating the options" but helping engineers and clients understand them ([blog, 2026, third party](https://speckle.systems/blog/design-explorer-beta-browse-compare-and-share-all-of-your-grasshopper-options-in-one-place/)). Flow would add Explore on a flow: choose inputs with ranges or presets, choose output figures as metrics, run in the background with cancel, then show a table with thumbnails captured from the drawing, filters, and "Use this option" that sets the inputs; each figure keeps its lineage for "Explain this number".
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** L · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** PARAM-03 Expose any value as a tool input; PARAM-06 Named presets; lineage and "Explain this number"; Bake; cancel

<a id="param-09"></a>

#### PARAM-09 Form layout: sections, order and conditional hide or disable
Form layout lets the author shape the input list: folding sections, an explicit order, separators and short notes, and rules that hide or grey out inputs that do not apply (for example "Podium height" only when "Podium" is on). Houdini asset interfaces use folders and tabs with [Disable when and Hide when rules](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/ref/windows/optype.html) such as { enablefeature == 1 }; Blender node groups have [panels and panel toggles](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/interface/controls/nodes/groups.html), and the [Menu Switch](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/modeling/geometry_nodes/utilities/menu_switch.html) hides inactive inputs "to hide irrelevant settings from the node interface or modifier panel"; [Nuke](https://learn.foundry.com/nuke/content/comp_environment/configuring_nuke/managing_controls.html) adds Tab and Divider knobs; [TouchDesigner](https://docs.derivative.ca/Custom_Parameters) puts parameters on pages and can disable them; [Unity](https://docs.unity3d.com/Packages/com.unity.shadergraph@17.0/manual/Blackboard.html) groups properties in categories; [Dynamo Player](https://help.autodesk.com/cloudhelp/2022/ENU/Alias-NURBS-Modeling/files/Generate-designs-with-Dynamo/Alias-Dynamo-Player/GUID-7C9864A5-5738-44F2-A455-F989863043F9.html) orders inputs by named groups. The need grows with size: BlenderNation presents node panels as the answer to "gargantuan Geometry Nodes node groups" with ever more inputs ([2023, third party](https://www.blendernation.com/2023/11/14/blender-4-0-released-check-out-the-new-features-of-blenders-new-era/)), and Blender 4.0 added a tree view to reorder inputs and drop them into panels ([release notes](https://developer.blender.org/docs/release_notes/4.0/node_editor/)). Flow would let the author add sections, separators and notes in the inputs list and set "show when" and "enable when" from a picker (input, comparison, value) rather than typed syntax; the inputs panel, the tool and the Player obey the same rules.
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** M · **Value:** 3 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** PARAM-03 Expose any value as a tool input; PARAM-05 Flow inputs panel

<a id="param-10"></a>

#### PARAM-10 Menu switch: a choice that picks the branch
A menu switch turns a choice input into a branch selector: each option has its own input wire, only the chosen branch is computed, and the option names appear as a dropdown on the flow's tool. Blender 4.1 added the [Menu Switch node](https://developer.blender.org/docs/release_notes/4.1/nodes_physics/) "to allow switching between options in the group interface"; the [manual](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/modeling/geometry_nodes/utilities/menu_switch.html) says only the active input is evaluated, entries can be renamed without breaking links, inputs feeding inactive options are hidden, and each entry also gives a true/false output. Houdini [menus](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/ref/windows/edit_parameter_interface.html) map tokens to labels and can use the "Control Next Parameter" style. Urban flows often switch method (setback by fixed distance or by height ratio; block type A, B or C), and without a switch the author wires every branch and a dispatcher by hand; Blender's release notes frame custom menus as the way to give group users named choices instead of raw numbers ([4.1 notes](https://developer.blender.org/docs/release_notes/4.1/nodes_physics/)). Flow already has a choice value; it would add a Switch node whose options come from a choice, skip and dim inactive branches, show option names on the tool when the choice is exposed, and with PARAM-09 hide the inputs of unchosen branches.
**Status:** PARTLY: "values (... choice ...)" · **Size:** M · **Value:** 3 · **Kind:** COMPLETION · **Depends on:** values (choice); run engine able to skip branches; PARAM-09 Form layout for hiding

<a id="param-11"></a>

#### PARAM-11 Table and list inputs
A table input lets the author expose a small editable table or list (unit mix, land-use shares, plot names) with rows that can be added, removed and reordered, instead of one slider per number. Houdini's [Multiparm Block](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/ref/windows/optype.html) lets users add and delete copies of a set of parameters, shown as a list, a scrolling area or tabs, and the [Wedge TOP](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/nodes/top/wedge.html) uses a Values multiparm for its value lists. Grasshopper has no native table: a user asked for "a table inside the grasshopper environment to input values in rows with columns", got a community plug-in built on a spreadsheet control, and later users asked it for cell formulas, kept cell sizes and high-DPI scaling ([forum, 2021 to 2026](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/input-values-as-table-inside-grasshopper-environment/132863)). Flow already reads a spreadsheet range, which keeps the table outside the flow; it would add a Table value node with typed columns (number with unit, text, choice), rows edited in the property editor or the inputs panel, output as a list or a list of lists, an optional total row and a check that shares add up to 100 per cent. An exposed table would appear in the tool and the Player as a compact grid.
**Status:** PARTLY: "sources (... a spreadsheet range ...)" · **Size:** M · **Value:** 3 · **Kind:** COMPLETION · **Depends on:** values; list and list-of-lists wires; PARAM-03 Expose any value as a tool input

<a id="param-12"></a>

#### PARAM-12 Curve (ramp) input
A curve input lets the user draw how a value changes across a range, for example building height falling with distance from a station, as a few draggable points on a small graph that the flow samples. Houdini has [Float and Color ramp parameters](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/ref/windows/optype.html), and its [ramp editor](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/network/ramps.html) adds, drags and types points with a chosen interpolation; Blender's [Float Curve node](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/modeling/geometry_nodes/utilities/math/float_curve.html) "maps an input float to a curve and outputs a float value"; Grasshopper's Graph Mapper offers 11 graph types and a draggable control point ([iArchway tutorial, 2025, third party](https://iarchway.com/en/graph_mapper/)). Evidence of demand is partly indirect: tutorials such as that one use the mapped curve to space points along a line, a shaping job that otherwise needs an expression, and Houdini lets expressions sample ramps directly. Flow would add a Curve value node with a graph from 0 to 1 (or ranges in drawing units), draggable points, linear or smooth interpolation, starting shapes (linear, ease, step), an x input and a y output; when exposed, the curve shows at full width in the inputs panel and the Player. Users asked to add control points to Grasshopper's Graph Mapper, and David Rutten replied "It's not possible with the existing one. I'm working on much better graph UI for GH2" ([grasshopper3d forum, 2016, old](https://www.grasshopper3d.com/xn/detail/2985220:Comment:1598541)); the Pufferfish plug-in then added "A custom Curve Graph Mapper which accepts any and multiple curves as inputs to graph with" ([Pufferfish group page, 2018, old, third party](https://www.grasshopper3d.com/group/pufferfish)).
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** M · **Value:** 3 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** values; expressions; PARAM-05 Flow inputs panel

<a id="param-13"></a>

#### PARAM-13 Value nodes: number, slider, text, choice, true/false
Value nodes are the typed constants a user sets by hand: number, slider, text, choice (a dropdown of named options) and true/false. Every studied editor has them: Grasshopper's number slider with [four accuracies](https://developer.rhino3d.com/api/grasshopper/html/T_Grasshopper_GUI_Base_GH_SliderAccuracy.htm); Dynamo's number and integer sliders and file paths, which [Dynamo Player](https://help.autodesk.com/cloudhelp/2022/ENU/Alias-NURBS-Modeling/files/Generate-designs-with-Dynamo/Alias-Dynamo-Player/GUID-7C9864A5-5738-44F2-A455-F989863043F9.html) can show; Blender node group inputs with [types, defaults and subtypes](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/interface/controls/nodes/groups.html); Houdini parameters with [menus of token and label items](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/ref/windows/edit_parameter_interface.html); and n8n's [Form Trigger](https://docs.n8n.io/integrations/builtin/core-nodes/n8n-nodes-base.formtrigger/) fields such as Number, Dropdown, Checkboxes and Text. They are the entry point of nearly every parametric task; a Rhino user with "five or six numerical inputs" wanted exactly these offered to a client as prompts ([forum, 2021](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/grasshopper-definition-to-toolbar-button/135082)). Flow already has all five, and typed numbers and quoted text in search become source nodes. Extensions are listed as separate rows: slider settings (PARAM-04), menu switching (PARAM-10), tables (PARAM-11) and curves (PARAM-12).
**Status:** EXISTS: "values (number, slider, text, choice, bool)" · **Size:** - · **Value:** 5 · **Kind:** - · **Depends on:** -

<a id="param-14"></a>

#### PARAM-14 A flow becomes a tool with an exposed slider
Publishing a flow as a tool turns an authored graph into a command that non-authors start from the usual places, with its exposed slider as an option. Rhino's [GrasshopperPlayer](https://docs.mcneel.com/rhino/8/help/en-us/commands/grasshopper.htm) can "load and execute a grasshopper definition ... without showing the Grasshopper window", and its [feature page](https://www.rhino3d.com/features/grasshopper/player/) shows definitions packaged as commands for walls and assemblies; Blender 4.0 [node tools](https://developer.blender.org/docs/release_notes/4.0/geometry_nodes/) let node groups be used "as operators from the 3D view menus" with a redo panel; Houdini assets carry an [icon, help page and parameter interface](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/ref/windows/optype.html); Nuke [gizmos](https://learn.foundry.com/nuke/content/comp_environment/configuring_nuke/creating_sourcing_gizmos.html) are groups exported to their own file; [Marionette](https://app-help.vectorworks.net/2024/eng/VW2024_Guide/Marionette/Step_5_Create_a_Marionette_object.htm) wraps a network into an object and [PARAM-O](https://help.graphisoft.com/AC/29/INT/_AC29_Help/065_PARAM-O/065_PARAM-O-7.htm) builds library parts with settings. Users want this so that others can run a script: a Rhino user's client "isn't Grasshopper-savvy" ([forum, 2021](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/grasshopper-definition-to-toolbar-button/135082)). Flow already does it, with its own command id, a place on the rail and in search, and use as a node in another flow. The gaps are separate rows: prompts (PARAM-02), more input kinds (PARAM-03), presets (PARAM-06) and handles (PARAM-07).
**Status:** EXISTS: "A flow can expose a slider and become a tool with its own command id, on the rail and in search, usable as a node in another flow" · **Size:** - · **Value:** 5 · **Kind:** - · **Depends on:** -

### Templates, snippets, favourites and libraries (LIB)

10 features: 2 EXISTS, 5 PARTLY, 3 MISSING.

<a id="lib-01"></a>

#### LIB-01 Template gallery on New
A template gallery opens on New (and from a menu) with ready-made starter flows shown as cards with a picture, a one-line purpose and tags; choosing one opens a copy that already runs. [ComfyUI](https://docs.comfy.org/interface/features/template.md) opens Workflow Templates from the sidebar or the Workflow menu and checks for missing model files when a template loads; extension authors add templates by dropping JSON files, with a same-name JPG as thumbnail, into [example_workflows](https://docs.comfy.org/custom-nodes/workflow_templates.md). In [n8n](https://docs.n8n.io/workflows/templates/) a new workflow can "start with an empty workflow, or use an existing template", and self-hosted teams can point to their own template library. [Dynamo 3.1](https://dynamobim.org/?p=55003) added new workspaces from a template (Ctrl+T), and [Node-RED](https://nodered.org/docs/user-guide/editor/workspace/import-export) imports example flows provided by installed nodes. Vendors keep investing here: ComfyUI's October 2025 release added tags and filters by model, use case and licence "so you can easily filter out the template you want" ([blog, 2025](https://blog.comfy.org/p/comfyui-0366-updates)). Flow would show a New dialog with an empty flow first, then a set of starter flows for urban tasks (setback envelope, plot subdivision, GFA by use, street-tree spacing), each built only from Studio commands, opened as a copy with sample inputs filled and a sticky note that explains the inputs. Rhino 8's ScriptEditor has a Templates panel, "a directory of scripts you use as starting points for new scripts", where double-clicking a template opens a copy and leaves the template unchanged ([McNeel developer guide](https://developer.rhino3d.com/guides/scripting/editor-templates/)).
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** M · **Value:** 5 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** LIB-06 Item details; sticky notes; library folder (.urbflow)

<a id="lib-02"></a>

#### LIB-02 Save a selection to the library as a snippet
A snippet is a selection of nodes saved into the library in one step, with a name, so it can be dropped into any later flow; it is lighter than writing a whole flow file by hand. Grasshopper's File > Create User Object writes a .ghuser to the user's folder ([forum, 2024](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/update-ghuser-programmatically/182331)), and Grasshopper loads these from [every user object folder](https://developer.rhino3d.com/api/grasshopper/html/P_Grasshopper_Folders_UserObjectFolders.htm); [ComfyUI](https://docs.comfy.org/interface/features/subgraph.md) publishes a subgraph as a Subgraph Blueprint with "Add Subgraph to Library"; [Node-RED](https://nodered.org/docs/user-guide/editor/workspace/import-export) can export "the selected nodes" to its local library; [TouchDesigner](https://docs.derivative.ca/Palette) adds a component by dragging it onto My Components; [Nuke](https://learn.foundry.com/nuke/content/comp_environment/configuring_nuke/creating_sourcing_gizmos.html) exports a group as a gizmo; [Dynamo](https://primer2.dynamobim.org/6_custom_nodes_and_packages/6-1_custom-nodes/3-library) publishes a custom node locally. Users lean on this and want it smoother: one Grasshopper user tried to script user-object creation to keep "naming format, icons, date formats, and authors" consistent and could not save new ones from code ([forum, 2024](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/update-ghuser-programmatically/182331)). Flow already collapses a selection into a sub-flow and saves flows to its library folder; it would add "Save selection to library", which collapses, asks for name, category and description, writes a .urbflow and lists it at once in search and the library panel. A recording could be saved the same way. Modo's Schematic packs selected nodes into one node with Create Assembly, exposes chosen channels as its inputs, and Save Assembly "Opens an OS-specific dialog for you to save the assembly as a preset" that can later be dragged from the Preset Browser into a new scene ([Modo 17.1 docs](https://learn.foundry.com/modo/content/help/pages/animation/schematic_view.html)).
**Status:** PARTLY: "sub-flows (collapse a selection into one node with exposed ports ...)"; "Flows save to a library folder (.urbflow)" · **Size:** S · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** COMPLETION · **Depends on:** sub-flows; library folder (.urbflow); LIB-06 Item details

<a id="lib-03"></a>

#### LIB-03 Team library locations, including a shared drive
Team library locations let Flow read flows from several places at once: the user's own folder plus office folders on a shared drive, with office folders read-only and labelled, so a team works from one approved set of tools. Blender registers any number of [asset libraries](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/files/asset_libraries/introduction.html) by name and location in Preferences; Dynamo's [Node and Package Paths](https://primer2.dynamobim.org/6_custom_nodes_and_packages/6-1_custom-nodes/3-library) list several folders, its 0.8.2 notes say this "even works over shared network folders" ([2015, old](https://dynamobim.org/dynamo082/)), and a [wiki page](https://github.com/DynamoDS/Dynamo/wiki/Loading-Packages-with-Binaries-from-a-Network-Location) covers office packages on a network path; Grasshopper loads user objects from [a list of folders](https://developer.rhino3d.com/api/grasshopper/html/P_Grasshopper_Folders_UserObjectFolders.htm); [n8n](https://docs.n8n.io/workflows/templates/) can use a custom template library; Revit's [Dynamo Player](https://help.autodesk.com/cloudhelp/2026/ENU/Revit-HaveYouTried/files/GUID-E8205058-9CA4-4CFF-95F5-A6AA1CDA7346.htm) manages folder locations directly. Grasshopper teams copy shared user objects from Dropbox by hand, ask for "multiple custom folders for userobjects" and find it hard to tell which version is newest ([forum, 2020](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/userobjects-in-the-cloud/99327)). Flow would add a Library locations list in settings (path, name, read-only), show each location as a section in the library panel and in search, and import from office locations as a link by default so updates arrive through the existing changed-on-disk and reload states.
**Status:** PARTLY: "Flows save to a library folder (.urbflow), import as a copy or a link (read-only linked flows, changed-on-disk and missing states, reload, unlink)" · **Size:** S · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** COMPLETION · **Depends on:** library folder; import as a link; LIB-04 Library panel

<a id="lib-04"></a>

#### LIB-04 Library panel with thumbnails and drag to canvas
A library panel shows saved flows, snippets and tools as a browsable grid or list with pictures, and the user drags an item onto the canvas (as a node) or into the drawing (to run it as a tool there). Blender's [Asset Browser](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/editors/asset_browser.html) is the model: drag a geometry-nodes asset onto an object to add it as a modifier, choose the import method (Link, Append, Pack) and browse catalogs; TouchDesigner's [Palette](https://docs.derivative.ca/Palette) loads a component by drag and drop into any network; [ComfyUI's node library](https://docs.comfy.org/interface/node-library.md) lets the user drag a tile onto the canvas; Revit's [Dynamo Player](https://help.autodesk.com/cloudhelp/2026/ENU/Revit-HaveYouTried/files/GUID-E8205058-9CA4-4CFF-95F5-A6AA1CDA7346.htm) lists graphs in a simple or detailed view with or without thumbnails. Users ask for better browsing once libraries grow: Grasshopper teams sharing user objects could not see which copy was newest or who had which ([forum, 2020](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/userobjects-in-the-cloud/99327)), and a long-time author warned that fast growth can signal "Library rot" ([forum, 2018, old](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/updating-userobjects-how-to-update-other-instances/68301)). Flow would add a dockable Library panel with grid and list views, pictures, a hover card with description and inputs, drag to canvas (copy or link chosen with a modifier key) and drag into the drawing to start the flow as a tool.
**Status:** PARTLY: "Flows save to a library folder (.urbflow), import as a copy or a link" · **Size:** M · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** COMPLETION · **Depends on:** library folder; import as a copy or a link; LIB-06 Item details; flow-as-tool (PARAM-14)

<a id="lib-05"></a>

#### LIB-05 Bundled starter library (Essentials)
A bundled starter library ships ready-made, tested sub-flows with the program, so a new user can assemble useful flows from bigger blocks before learning the low-level commands. Blender ships the [Essentials asset library](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/files/asset_libraries/introduction.html) with hair node groups, a Smooth By Angle node group and brushes, and in 4.1 the old Auto Smooth option ["has been replaced by a modifier node group asset"](https://developer.blender.org/docs/release_notes/4.1/modeling/); [ComfyUI's node library](https://docs.comfy.org/interface/node-library.md) has an Essentials tab on Comfy Cloud and nightly builds; TouchDesigner's [Palette](https://docs.derivative.ca/Palette) comes with a default library of components; Revit's [Dynamo Player](https://help.autodesk.com/cloudhelp/2026/ENU/Revit-HaveYouTried/files/GUID-E8205058-9CA4-4CFF-95F5-A6AA1CDA7346.htm) comes with sample graphs such as Add Levels. Demand shows in Blender's gizmo design thread, where after users shared helper groups the developer replied "We already talked about the possibility that we ship this and potentially other similar node groups with Blender" ([devtalk, 2023](https://devtalk.blender.org/t/geometry-nodes-gizmos-feedback)). Flow would ship a read-only "Urbanto Essentials" library location with sub-flows for common urban steps (offset a plot by a setback, split a block into plots, stack floors to a height limit, sum GFA by use, place trees along a line), each with exposed inputs, a description and an example, updated with program releases and inserted as links.
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** M · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** LIB-03 Team library locations; LIB-06 Item details; sub-flows with exposed ports

<a id="lib-06"></a>

#### LIB-06 Item details: thumbnail, description, author and icon
Item details give every library entry a picture and a short card (thumbnail, description, author, dates, an icon for its tool and the list of inputs), so users can pick the right flow without opening it. Blender [generates a preview automatically](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/files/asset_libraries/introduction.html) when an item is marked as an asset, and its [Asset Browser](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/editors/asset_browser.html) stores optional Description, Author, Copyright and License fields; Houdini assets have an [Icon field and a Help tab](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/ref/windows/optype.html) whose text appears in the help browser; Node-RED subflows carry a Markdown [description, category, colour and icon](https://nodered.org/docs/user-guide/editor/workspace/subflows); [ComfyUI templates](https://docs.comfy.org/custom-nodes/workflow_templates.md) use a same-name JPG as thumbnail; Revit's [Dynamo Player](https://help.autodesk.com/cloudhelp/2026/ENU/Revit-HaveYouTried/files/GUID-E8205058-9CA4-4CFF-95F5-A6AA1CDA7346.htm) adds graph author and a documentation link. A Grasshopper user tried to script consistent "naming format, icons, date formats, and authors" across a set of user objects because there was no tool to manage them ([forum, 2024](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/update-ghuser-programmatically/182331)). Flow would store a details block in the .urbflow (title, description, author, created and changed dates, tags, icon), capture the thumbnail from the flow's result in the drawing on save (with "Use current view" to replace it), and show the card in the library panel, the Player and the rail tooltip.
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** M · **Value:** 3 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** library folder (.urbflow); results show in the drawing on the flow's own layer

<a id="lib-07"></a>

#### LIB-07 Categories, tags and search across the library
Categories and tags organise a growing library: a folder-like catalog tree that is independent of where files sit, free tags, and search that covers names, descriptions and tags of every item in every library location; the category also decides where a flow-tool appears in menus. Blender's [asset catalogs](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/files/asset_libraries/catalogs.html) are "completely independent of the location of your blend-files" and decide in which Add menu submenu a node-group asset appears, and [Asset Browser](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/editors/asset_browser.html) search also matches tags; Dynamo's [Group field](https://primer2.dynamobim.org/6_custom_nodes_and_packages/6-1_custom-nodes/3-library) places a published node in a library category; [ComfyUI templates](https://blog.comfy.org/p/comfyui-0366-updates) gained tags and filters by model and use case; [n8n](https://docs.n8n.io/workflows/templates/) can search and browse a custom template library. Libraries of flow-tools grow fast: one Grasshopper Player user had built about 80 custom tools by February 2021 ([forum, 2021](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/grasshopper-player-custom-tools-wish-list/114844)), and ComfyUI added tags "so you can easily filter out the template you want" ([blog, 2025](https://blog.comfy.org/p/comfyui-0366-updates)). Flow search already matches node names, keywords and tags; it would add a category field and tags on each library item, a catalog tree in the library panel, and include library flows and snippets (not only published tools) in Tab search with their category shown.
**Status:** PARTLY: "Search at the cursor ... fuzzy on name, keywords and tags"; "on the rail and in search" · **Size:** M · **Value:** 3 · **Kind:** COMPLETION · **Depends on:** search at the cursor; LIB-04 Library panel; LIB-06 Item details

<a id="lib-09"></a>

#### LIB-09 Versioned library items
Versioned library items keep several versions of a flow side by side: new uses get the latest, existing uses keep working on the version they were built with until the user chooses to update, and each use shows which version it is. Houdini puts the version in the asset's internal name (namespace::name::version); ["Houdini will automatically make only the latest version available"](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/assets/namespaces.html) in the interface, "Instances of the old version will still work", and the Asset Manager can prefer an older definition; Node-RED subflows store a version in their [module properties](https://nodered.org/docs/user-guide/editor/workspace/subflows) for packaging. Grasshopper teams sharing user objects found it hard to know which version is newest and who has which, and one replied that a shared folder brings "a lack of version control" ([forum, 2020](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/userobjects-in-the-cloud/99327)); a long-time developer warned of "Library rot" as shared components multiply ([forum, 2018, old](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/updating-userobjects-how-to-update-other-instances/68301)). Flow already tracks a linked flow's changed-on-disk and missing states with reload; it would add a version number and change note when saving to the library, keep earlier versions beside the item, pin each link to a version, and show "newer version available" with a summary of changed inputs before updating.
**Status:** PARTLY: "read-only linked flows, changed-on-disk and missing states, reload, unlink" · **Size:** M · **Value:** 2 · **Kind:** COMPLETION · **Depends on:** import as a link; LIB-03 Team library locations; LIB-06 Item details

<a id="lib-10"></a>

#### LIB-10 Library folder of saved flows
A library folder is a place on disk where saved flows live so they can be reused in any drawing. [Node-RED](https://nodered.org/docs/user-guide/editor/workspace/import-export) saves selected nodes or whole flows to "the local flow library" and imports from it; [Dynamo](https://primer2.dynamobim.org/6_custom_nodes_and_packages/6-1_custom-nodes/3-library) publishes custom nodes to a local folder that it checks "each time it loads"; [TouchDesigner](https://docs.derivative.ca/Palette) keeps a user's own components under My Components; [Blender](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/files/asset_libraries/introduction.html) asset libraries are folders of blend-files registered in Preferences; Nuke stores [gizmos](https://learn.foundry.com/nuke/content/comp_environment/configuring_nuke/creating_sourcing_gizmos.html) as .gizmo files in the user's .nuke folder. Users want reuse without copying files around by hand: Grasshopper teams describe keeping shared user objects in Dropbox and copying them into Rhino's folders ([forum, 2020](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/userobjects-in-the-cloud/99327)). Flow already saves flows to a library folder as .urbflow files, and a saved flow can be published as a tool. The improvements are separate rows: snippets (LIB-02), team locations (LIB-03), a library panel (LIB-04) and item details (LIB-06).
**Status:** EXISTS: "Flows save to a library folder (.urbflow)" · **Size:** - · **Value:** 5 · **Kind:** - · **Depends on:** -

<a id="lib-11"></a>

#### LIB-11 Import a flow as a copy or a link
Importing as a copy or a link decides whether a reused flow is frozen into the current flow or follows its source file. Blender's [Asset Browser](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/editors/asset_browser.html) offers Link (the asset is "read-only"), Append (the file "now has its own copy", so later source changes are not reflected) and Pack; Nuke [gizmos](https://learn.foundry.com/nuke/content/comp_environment/configuring_nuke/creating_sourcing_gizmos.html) are read from their file when a script loads, so editing the gizmo updates every script that uses it; ComfyUI's [Subgraph Blueprints](https://docs.comfy.org/interface/features/subgraph.md) are inserted as isolated copies; Houdini asset instances follow their [definition and version](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/assets/namespaces.html). The pain of copy-only reuse is documented: a Grasshopper developer asked how to update all placed instances after overwriting a user object and got only a cluster-specific workaround ([forum, 2018, old](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/updating-userobjects-how-to-update-other-instances/68301)). Flow already supports both, with read-only linked flows, changed-on-disk and missing states, reload and unlink. Versions (LIB-09) and team locations (LIB-03) build on this.
**Status:** EXISTS: "import as a copy or a link (read-only linked flows, changed-on-disk and missing states, reload, unlink)" · **Size:** - · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** - · **Depends on:** -

### Collaboration and sharing (COLLAB)

13 features: 1 EXISTS, 3 PARTLY, 9 MISSING.

<a id="collab-01"></a>

#### COLLAB-01 Missing-item report and placeholders on open
When something a flow relies on is absent at open, the editor keeps a placeholder node with its wires and settings and lists each missing item with a fix. Grasshopper raises an ["Unrecognized Objects" dialog](https://developer.rhino3d.com/api/grasshopper/html/P_Grasshopper_CentralSettings_TryDownloadMissingPlugins.htm), and Rhino adds [package restore](https://developer.rhino3d.com/guides/yak/what-is-yak/) to fetch the missing plug-ins; Dynamo keeps unresolved nodes and since 2.5.0 [resolves them without reopening the graph](https://github.com/DynamoDS/Dynamo/wiki/Release-Notes) once the package is installed; ComfyUI's [Errors tab](https://docs.comfy.org/interface/right-side-panel) lists missing node packs (Install All) and models (Download); Blender [creates placeholder data-blocks](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/files/linked_libraries/link_append.html) for a broken library so references survive; Node-RED 4.1 [records a flow's modules](https://nodered.org/blog/2025/07/29/version-4-1-released) and tells the importer what to install. Pain: a Node-RED user [could not tell which module to install](https://discourse.nodered.org/t/how-do-you-find-missing-node-types/16625) and all flows stopped (forum thread, 2019, old), and ComfyUI's docs say shared workflows [often fail to run after loading](https://docs.comfy.org/basic-concepts/dependencies). Flow would extend its linked-flow missing state to every reference (flow tools, sub-flows, sources naming a layer, saved view, attribute key or range absent from this drawing), show one "Needs attention" list with remap-by-name, and never drop nodes.
**Status:** PARTLY: "changed-on-disk and missing states" · **Size:** M · **Value:** 5 · **Kind:** COMPLETION · **Depends on:** linked flows (missing state), sources, flow tools with their own command id

<a id="collab-04"></a>

#### COLLAB-04 Pack a flow with everything it needs
Packing writes one file carrying the flow plus every linked flow and flow tool it uses, so a colleague can open it without the sender's library. Blender's [Pack Resources](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/files/blend/packed_data.html) puts external files inside the blend-file, which "allows sharing a full project as a single file"; Houdini can [save node definitions to the HIP file](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/assets/install.html) so a scene works where libraries are missing; n8n's [package export](https://docs.n8n.io/build/manage-workflows/n8n-packages/export-a-package) walks sub-workflow dependencies and lets you fail, include them or keep references. The pain is files that open broken elsewhere: a Blender user writes "quite often I open a blend file and everything is pink" after moving between machines ([forum thread, 2019, old, third party](https://blenderartists.org/t/file-management-issues/1176668)), and ComfyUI's docs explain that a workflow ["depends on other files besides the workflow itself"](https://docs.comfy.org/basic-concepts/dependencies). Flow would add Pack and Send: linked flows and flow tools become embedded copies marked with their origin, drawing-side needs (layers, saved views, attribute keys) are listed as a checklist inside the file, and Unpack restores the links when the library is present.
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** M · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** library folder (.urbflow), import as a copy or a link, COLLAB-01 missing-item report

<a id="collab-05"></a>

#### COLLAB-05 Version history with restore
Each save keeps an earlier version that can be listed, previewed, restored or cloned later, independent of the canvas undo stack. n8n keeps [workflow history](https://docs.n8n.io/workflows/history/) with Restore, Clone to new workflow, Open in new tab and named versions that are never pruned; Node-RED [Projects](https://nodered.org/docs/user-guide/projects/) keep flows in a git repository with a Commit History sidebar; Blender's [Save Versions](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/editors/preferences/save_load.html) keeps .blend1 and .blend2 backups beside the file. Demand is strong in team use: Node-RED's 2025 survey found that [version control and diffing frustrates 42% of production users](https://nodered.org/blog/2025/12/01/modernization-survey-results) and is their "#1 blocker" at 40% (vendor survey). For new users the gain is confidence: they can experiment and still roll back after closing the file. Flow would keep the last N saves of each .urbflow in a hidden sibling folder (no server or git needed), show them in a History panel with thumbnail, date, author and optional name, and offer Restore (one canvas-history step) and Open as copy. Cables.gl keeps rolling automatic patch backups ("no more than 20 automatic backup versions per patch"), never deletes manual ones and restores a backup as a new patch, though only for supporters with storage left ([cables.gl docs](https://cables.gl/docs/4_export_embed/patch_backups/patch_backups)).
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** M · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** library folder (.urbflow), canvas history separate from drawing history, COLLAB-09 flow info (author, name)

<a id="collab-06"></a>

#### COLLAB-06 Copy and paste nodes as text, between flows and apps
Copying a selection puts a readable text version of those nodes and wires on the clipboard, which pastes into another flow, an email, a forum post or a chat with an AI assistant, and back. Node-RED's [Import and Export dialogs](https://nodered.org/docs/user-guide/editor/workspace/import-export) copy flow JSON to the clipboard and accept pasted JSON; n8n lets you [copy nodes and paste them](https://docs.n8n.io/workflows/export-import/) and stores workflows as JSON; ComfyUI [embeds the workflow in generated images](https://docs.comfy.org/development/api-development/workflow-metadata) so a PNG reopens its recipe; Dynamo 2.14 added [commands to create a workspace from JSON content](https://github.com/DynamoDS/Dynamo/wiki/Release-Notes). Plain text is how people help each other: an n8n moderator answered that "to share a workflow you'd need to share the JSON code/file" ([forum thread, 2022, third party](https://community.n8n.io/t/how-to-share-workflow-in-private-n8n-instance/13405)). The Flow list does not mention copy and paste of nodes at all, so the basic in-canvas case is included here. Flow would write the selection as a .urbflow fragment, paste it at the cursor as an import-as-copy with new ids (raising COLLAB-01 when references do not resolve), and keep pasted shapes working as today's source.
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** M · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** .urbflow format, import as a copy, COLLAB-01 missing-item report

<a id="collab-07"></a>

#### COLLAB-07 Linked-flow states: changed on disk, missing, reload, unlink
A linked flow shows when its file has changed on disk or gone missing, and offers Reload and Unlink. Blender's Outliner offers [Reload and Relocate](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/files/linked_libraries/link_append.html) for a linked library, Relocate fixing one that "was moved or renamed"; Dynamo's Workspace References panel (renamed in 2.5.0, extended with local definitions and external files in 2.13, per the [release notes](https://github.com/DynamoDS/Dynamo/wiki/Release-Notes)) lists what a graph depends on. Broken links are a recurring cost: a Blender user relies on Find Missing Files to repair links after moving files between machines ([forum thread, 2019, old, third party](https://blenderartists.org/t/file-management-issues/1176668)). Flow already has read-only linked flows with changed-on-disk and missing states, reload and unlink, which is more than most node editors offer for their own sub-graphs. COLLAB-01 extends the same states to every other kind of reference inside a flow, and COLLAB-04 removes the need for links when a flow is sent outside the office.
**Status:** EXISTS · **Size:** - · **Value:** 3 · **Kind:** - · **Depends on:** -

<a id="collab-08"></a>

#### COLLAB-08 Comments attached to nodes
A short comment stored on one node, shown as a bubble or badge beside it, that moves, copies and travels with the node, unlike a free sticky note. Unreal Blueprints have [node comments](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/comments-in-unreal-engine) typed into a Node Comment field and shown in a bubble above the node; Houdini can [show a node's comment next to it](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/network/organize.html), "useful for networks that will be shared with other people"; Dynamo lets you pin a note to a node (2.14 added "unpin from node", 4.1 fixed orphaned pinned notes, per the [release notes](https://github.com/DynamoDS/Dynamo/wiki/Release-Notes)); Node-RED gives every node a Markdown [Description tab](https://nodered.org/docs/user-guide/editor/workspace/nodes) with a docs badge. The gap shows where review tools exist: n8n's new review feature states that comments ["aren't anchored to a specific node in this release"](https://docs.n8n.io/build/manage-workflows/workflow-reviews) (vendor docs). Flow would add a Comment field in the property editor, a small badge that expands to the text on hover, inclusion in copy, pack and diff, and a "show all comments" toggle; sticky notes and groups remain for larger annotations.
**Status:** PARTLY: "sticky notes" · **Size:** S · **Value:** 3 · **Kind:** COMPLETION · **Depends on:** property editor (Enter), sticky notes

<a id="collab-09"></a>

#### COLLAB-09 Flow info card: description, author, version, thumbnail
Each flow carries a small info record (title, a paragraph of description, author, version, thumbnail, date and the Studio version it needs) shown in the library and on hover. Dynamo has a Graph Properties panel with a description field and custom properties (3.6 to 4.1, per the [release notes](https://github.com/DynamoDS/Dynamo/wiki/Release-Notes)) and reads each graph's [thumbnail from the .dyn file](https://primer2.dynamobim.org/3_user_interface/3-graph-thumbnails) for its Home page; Houdini assets carry a [version in the node type name](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/assets/namespaces.html) so old and new versions co-exist; Node-RED flows have a [name and Markdown description](https://nodered.org/docs/user-guide/editor/workspace/flows) shown in the Information sidebar. Demand evidence is indirect: Dynamo kept extending the panel across releases, and Grasshopper users lose meaning when reusable objects drop their custom names ([forum thread, 2020, third party](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/cluster-to-user-object-retain-custom-nicknames/111831)). Flow would add an Info tab to the flow window and show the card in the library folder view, in search results for flow tools, in version history and in the missing-item list ("needs a newer Studio").
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** S · **Value:** 3 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** library folder (.urbflow), flow tools on the rail and in search

<a id="collab-10"></a>

#### COLLAB-10 Diffable, stable text file format
The saved flow is plain text in a stable order, with layout kept apart from logic, so ordinary diff tools show what really changed. Dynamo moved from XML to [JSON in 2.0](https://github.com/DynamoDS/Dynamo/wiki/Release-Notes) for reuse in "web-friendly environments"; Houdini can save [scenes as text](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/hom/hou/saveMode.html) "friendly to version control" (full commercial licence only) and [expand assets into plain-text directories](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/ref/utils/hotl.html) so "version control systems can show changes as diffs"; Node-RED and n8n store flows as JSON; Grasshopper offers XML .ghx beside binary .gh, though a user found that a text diff of .ghx works but misses a lot ([forum thread, 2026](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/understanding-old-gh-code-compare-two-versions-of-a-gh-file/220526)). Mixing layout into logic still hurts: a team reported "just opening and saving a GH definition will result in a conflict" ([forum thread, 2021, third party](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/version-control-for-gh/128645)), and a request asked for a format with "all the logically irrelevant info stripped out" ([forum thread, 2018, old](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/gh-file-revisions-property/54672/3)). Flow would guarantee that .urbflow is UTF-8 text written deterministically (sorted ids, no timestamps in the body), with canvas positions in a trailing section, and document the format.
**Status:** PARTLY: "Flows save to a library folder (.urbflow)" · **Size:** M · **Value:** 3 · **Kind:** COMPLETION · **Depends on:** .urbflow format

<a id="collab-11"></a>

#### COLLAB-11 Upgrade outdated nodes when opening an older flow
When a flow saved in an older Studio version uses a command whose inputs or behaviour changed, the editor marks those nodes and offers an upgrade that keeps wires and values, with a backup. Grasshopper has Solution > Upgrade Components, but "not all components have automated upgraders" ([forum thread, 2015, old](https://grasshopper3d.com/forum/topics/upgrading-gh-files)) and "there is no tool for auto-upgrading all components" ([forum thread, 2023](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/igh-upgradeobject-not-called-to-upgrade/104756)); Dynamo 2.0 migrated 1.x files and kept a backup copy, and 3.0 auto-migrates Formula nodes to Code Blocks ([release notes](https://github.com/DynamoDS/Dynamo/wiki/Release-Notes)); Houdini [asset versions](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/assets/namespaces.html) let "instances of the old version will still work" while new placements get the latest; the Comfy Registry lets authors [deprecate a version with a message](https://docs.comfy.org/registry/overview). That registry page states the pain: new node versions "often break workflows that rely on them" (vendor docs). Flow would version each command's input declaration, run registered migrations on open (reported in the COLLAB-01 list), keep the old behaviour runnable when no migration exists, and save a backup the first time a file is upgraded. Cables.gl keeps the version in the op name (`Ops.Gl.Texture_v2`) and "Whenever cables sees that old versions of ops are used, it will show a hint and offer an update mechanism in the editor" ([cables.gl docs](https://cables.gl/docs/5_writing_ops/dev_renaming/dev_renaming)), where clicking the warning "will open a search with all the Ops that need attention and should be updated" ([cables.gl blog, 2022](https://blog.cables.gl/december-2022-release/)). Grasshopper 2 accepts Grasshopper 1 components dragged or pasted onto its canvas, where "they can either be hosted or auto-upgraded to the nearest GH2 equivalent" ([McNeel, Grasshopper 2 page, undated](https://www.rhino3d.com/stories/grasshopper-2/)).
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** M · **Value:** 3 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** commands declare their inputs, COLLAB-01 missing-item report, COLLAB-09 flow info (Studio version)

<a id="collab-12"></a>

#### COLLAB-12 Visual diff between two versions of a flow
A side-by-side or overlaid view of two versions of a flow that colours added, removed, changed and moved nodes, steps through changes, and shows per-node value differences. Unreal's [Diff tool](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/ue-diff-tool-in-unreal-engine) compares Blueprint revisions with red, green, cyan and grey highlights because for assets "a textual representation would not be constructive"; n8n [workflow diffs](https://docs.n8n.io/administer/use-source-control-and-environments/compare-versions) mark nodes N, M or D with a JSON diff per node, reused in [workflow reviews](https://docs.n8n.io/build/manage-workflows/workflow-reviews); Node-RED's [Review changes](https://www.github.com/node-red/node-red/pull/4692) workflow merges another user's deploy; Grasshopper has third-party tools such as the [Grasshopper Comparator](https://github.com/SerjoschDuering/Grasshopper_Comparator) (work in progress), and a forum claim that Grasshopper 2 compares versions was not verified. A user updating a 2021 project said comparison would "make a world of difference" ([forum thread, 2026](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/understanding-old-gh-code-compare-two-versions-of-a-gh-file/220526)), and Node-RED production users rank version control and diffing their top frustration ([survey, 2025](https://nodered.org/blog/2025/12/01/modernization-survey-results)). Flow would open any two versions from COLLAB-05 in a split canvas with the same tidy layout and a change list.
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** L · **Value:** 3 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** COLLAB-05 version history, COLLAB-10 stable text format, tidy (layered layout)

<a id="collab-13"></a>

#### COLLAB-13 Packages of flow tools with versions and dependencies
A package groups several flow tools with a name, version, author and dependencies, so an office or community can publish, install, update and remove them inside the program. Dynamo's [Package Manager](https://primer2.dynamobim.org/6_custom_nodes_and_packages/6-2_packages/1-introduction), Rhino's [Package Manager](https://docs.mcneel.com/rhino/8/help/en-us/commands/packagemanager.htm) built on [Yak](https://developer.rhino3d.com/guides/yak/what-is-yak/), Blender's [extensions](https://developer.blender.org/docs/release_notes/4.2/extensions/) introduced in 4.2 LTS ([manual](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/editors/preferences/extensions.html)), the [Comfy Registry](https://docs.comfy.org/registry/overview) behind ComfyUI-Manager and Node-RED's [Palette Manager](https://nodered.org/docs/user-guide/editor/palette/manager) all do this. Demand is real but mixed: the Comfy Registry exists because new node versions "often break workflows", while Node-RED production users report "uncertainty about third-party node quality and security (32%)" ([survey, 2025](https://nodered.org/blog/2025/12/01/modernization-survey-results)). For urban designers the first need is an office library, which the shared library folder already covers. Later, Flow would ship packages as zip files of .urbflow tools with a manifest (version, required Studio version, depends-on), install them into the library folder, read an office feed address, and carry flows only, never third-party code.
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** L · **Value:** 2 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** library folder (.urbflow), flow tools with their own command id, COLLAB-09 flow info, COLLAB-11 upgrade outdated nodes

<a id="collab-14"></a>

#### COLLAB-14 Share a flow to a web viewer
Publishing a flow, or just its exposed sliders, to a browser page where a client or colleague without Studio can move sliders and see the result. [ShapeDiver](https://shapediver.com/how-it-works) turns a Grasshopper definition into "an app anyone can use in a browser" with private links and embed codes; [Speckle](https://docs.speckle.systems/connectors/grasshopper/grasshopper) publishes Grasshopper geometry as model versions that open in a web browser; Comfy Cloud creates [shareable workflow links](https://docs.comfy.org/cloud/share-workflow) that include the workflow's assets. Evidence of demand is indirect: whole commercial services exist around this need (vendor pages), and none of the studied programs has a strong forum trail for it from urban designers. It would help client consultation, but live runs need a server, a cloud geometry engine and a data-protection review. Flow would first export a static viewer: the results baked at chosen slider steps, with a slider table that swaps between them, and leave live cloud runs for much later.
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** L · **Value:** 2 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** a flow can expose a slider, Bake, COLLAB-09 flow info

<a id="collab-15"></a>

#### COLLAB-15 Autosave and recovery after a crash or hang
Autosave keeps a recovery copy of each open flow and offers it at the next start after a crash or hang. Blender's [Auto Save](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/editors/preferences/save_load.html) "periodically saves a temporary backup of the current file", restored with [Recover Auto Save](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/files/blend/open_save.html) "in the event of a crash"; Dynamo 2.18 added "backup settings in preference panel to manage backup interval and location" ([release notes](https://github.com/DynamoDS/Dynamo/wiki/Release-Notes)). Grasshopper 1 autosaves to an AutoSave folder, yet a user with a new, unsaved definition had nothing to recover after a crash ([McNeel forum, 2024](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/grasshoper-crash-recovery-file/52621)). The Pancake plug-in's Hang Protection saves an emergency file when Grasshopper has not responded for "61s ~ 65s" ([author's forum reply, 2024, third party](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/pancake-put-emergency-files-in-subfolder/127945)); a user wrote "The autosave feature has saved me at least 30 hours of work" ([McNeel forum, 2020, old](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/pancake-2-0-release/94010)). Flow would save every open flow, named or not, to a recovery folder every few minutes and after canvas-history steps, including while a run blocks the interface, and offer Restore or Discard with time and thumbnail at the next start.
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** M · **Value:** 3 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** canvas history separate from drawing history; .urbflow files; cancel (Esc); PERF-06 Background solving

### Learning and help (LEARN)

10 features: 1 EXISTS, 3 PARTLY, 6 MISSING.

<a id="learn-01"></a>

#### LEARN-01 Node help panel (F1)
A docked help panel showing, for the selected node, a plain-language description, each input and output with type and default, usage notes and a link to the full manual, opened with F1 or right-click Help. Dynamo's [Documentation Browser](https://help.autodesk.com/cloudhelp/2026/ENU/Revit-HaveYouTried/files/GUID-D6E187C0-874C-4C9A-B6B7-EC8404F75B9B.htm) opens from right-click Help and [F1](https://github.com/DynamoDS/Dynamo/wiki/Dynamo-Keyboard-Shortcuts), with node information, inputs and outputs and an example graph; ComfyUI shows [node docs](https://docs.comfy.org/interface/features/node-docs) from the selection toolbox or the Info tab; Node-RED's [Help sidebar](https://nodered.org/docs/user-guide/editor/sidebar/help) covers every node; Blender opens the [manual page](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/getting_started/help.html) for the control under the cursor with F1; every Houdini node has a [help page](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/nodes/sop/scatter.html). Users still ask for this: Grasshopper users want help "from the Help menu item of the component itself" ([forum thread, 2026](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/grasshopper-1-0-online-documentation/217843)), years after David Rutten said there was no list describing all components ([forum thread, 2013, old](https://www.grasshopper3d.com/forum/topics/basic-description)). Flow would show the command's own description and declared inputs (data Flow already holds), a link to the command's manual page and a "used in" list of library flows; the panel follows the selection and works from the keyboard.
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** M · **Value:** 5 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** commands declare their inputs, accessible names, property editor

<a id="learn-05"></a>

#### LEARN-05 Wire and data-shape legend with concept cards
A legend and short concept cards explain what each wire colour and stroke means and how items, lists and lists of lists combine. Blender's manual explains [socket shapes](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/interface/controls/nodes/parts.html): single values as rectangles, fields as diamonds, plus grids and lists; Dynamo's Primer teaches [lists of lists](https://primer2.dynamobim.org/5_essential_nodes_and_concepts/5-4_designing-with-lists/3-lists-of-lists) with physical analogies such as a box of card decks. The concept is the classic stumbling block: a Grasshopper educator called data trees "the single most confusing aspect of it for students" ([forum thread, 2012, old](https://www.grasshopper3d.com/xn/detail/2985220:Comment:744373)). Flow already encodes the shape in wire strokes, but nothing on the canvas says what the strokes mean. Flow would add a legend button beside the minimap, a "what is on this wire" hover card naming the shape of the data now flowing, and a one-screen concept card on how lists match, linked from list-related warnings.
**Status:** PARTLY: "Wires typed and coloured by type with strokes for one item, a list, a list of lists and a lazy output" · **Size:** S · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** COMPLETION · **Depends on:** typed wires with strokes, STATE-02 warnings that explain

<a id="learn-06"></a>

#### LEARN-06 Empty-canvas hints and next-step suggestions
A new, empty flow shows a few large starting actions instead of a blank grid ("Start from the selection", "Start from a layer or spreadsheet", "Record commands", "Open an example"), and hints fade once the first wire exists. n8n's empty canvas offers an [Add first step](https://docs.n8n.io/build-your-first-workflow) button that asks how the workflow should start; ComfyUI [loads a default text-to-image workflow](https://docs.comfy.org/get_started/first_generation) on launch so a newcomer begins from something that runs; Dynamo Home offers New, Open, templates and samples ([release notes](https://github.com/DynamoDS/Dynamo/wiki/Release-Notes), 3.2 to 4.1). Beginners stall at the blank canvas: the Grasshopper learner above built more than forty definitions because not knowing which component exists leaves you "out of luck" ([forum thread, 2022](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/exercise-in-using-all-the-standard-grasshopper-components/137497/1)). Flow would show these actions on an empty canvas, put Recording first because running the commands a CAD user already knows is the gentlest start, and offer next-step chips at an unconnected output that open the existing compatible-node search.
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** S · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** Recording, sources (selection, layer, spreadsheet range), search filtered to compatible nodes

<a id="learn-07"></a>

#### LEARN-07 Example flow for each node
Each node's help offers one or more small example flows that open read-only or insert into the current flow. Houdini node help lists examples with [Load and Launch buttons](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/nodes/sop/scatter.html), and authors can [add their own examples](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/help/createexamples.html); Dynamo's Documentation Browser can [add a node's example graph to the workspace](https://help.autodesk.com/cloudhelp/2026/ENU/Revit-HaveYouTried/files/GUID-D6E187C0-874C-4C9A-B6B7-EC8404F75B9B.htm) as a group and switches to manual run; Node-RED node packages ship [example flows](https://nodered.org/docs/creating-nodes/examples) that appear in the Import menu; ComfyUI's [Templates](https://docs.comfy.org/interface/features/template) include example workflows from custom nodes. Users build these themselves when vendors do not: a Grasshopper learner made [example definitions for all standard components](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/exercise-in-using-all-the-standard-grasshopper-components/137497/1) (forum thread, 2022), and McNeel China's online docs now add "small examples to illustrate suitable usage scenarios" ([forum thread, 2026](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/grasshopper-1-0-online-documentation/217843)). Flow would store examples as ordinary .urbflow files tagged with a command id, insert them as a copy inside a titled group with run mode set to manual, and start with the 30 most used commands. In the Grasshopper 2 beta "Help images show actual example files, which can be opened by dragging the image onto the GH canvas", and McNeel aims "to provide example files for every component which ships with GH2" ([McNeel, Grasshopper 2 page, undated](https://www.rhino3d.com/stories/grasshopper-2/)).
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** M · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** LEARN-01 help panel, import as a copy, groups with title, run modes

<a id="learn-08"></a>

#### LEARN-08 Start page with samples and learning links
When Flow opens with no flow loaded, a start page shows recent flows, starter samples with thumbnails, guided tours and office-chosen links (videos, the manual, the forum). Dynamo Home shows recent files and guided tours (3.2), lets a BIM manager configure video tutorials (3.3), adds sample graphs and datasets (3.4) and shows templates with thumbnails (4.1), per the [release notes](https://github.com/DynamoDS/Dynamo/wiki/Release-Notes); ComfyUI's [Workflow Templates](https://docs.comfy.org/interface/features/template) browser and [Help Center](https://docs.comfy.org/interface/help-center) collect examples, docs and community links; Blender's [splash screen](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/interface/window_system/splash.html) offers new-from-template, recent files and a first-run quick setup. Dynamo framed its help work as lowering "barriers to entry" ([vendor blog, 2020, old](https://dynamobim.org/dynamo-core-2-6-release/)), and Node-RED found hobbyists "put a lot of value on the documentation and help system" ([survey, 2025](https://nodered.org/blog/2025/12/01/modernization-survey-results)). Flow would show the start page inside an empty flow window (dismissable, reachable again from Help), with samples drawn from the templates library (LIB group) and an editable office links file.
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** M · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** library folder (.urbflow), templates (LIB group), COLLAB-09 flow info (thumbnail), LEARN-09 guided tours

<a id="learn-09"></a>

#### LEARN-09 Guided tours
Short interactive walkthroughs that highlight one control at a time and wait for the user to do each step (add a node, wire it, run it). Dynamo ships a Getting Started guided tour "showing users how to add and connect nodes and run the graph" (2.15), a Packages tour and a first-time experience (2.13), per the [release notes](https://github.com/DynamoDS/Dynamo/wiki/Release-Notes), reachable from the Help menu's [Interactive Guides](https://primer2.dynamobim.org/3_user_interface) and from Dynamo Home. Evidence is mostly vendor investment: Dynamo has kept refining its tours through 4.1, which closes side panels when a tour starts, and Node-RED's survey points to "problems getting acquainted with Node-RED, both when starting out" and later ([survey, 2025](https://nodered.org/blog/2025/12/01/modernization-survey-results)). Tours help less than help text that is always there, so the value is moderate. Flow would anchor tour steps on its accessible names and check progress with the same data the test bridge reads, so a step knows the user really made the wire. Three tours to start: a first flow from the selection, Recording, and "Explain this number".
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** M · **Value:** 3 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** accessible names on every control, test bridge, Recording, Explain this number

<a id="learn-10"></a>

#### LEARN-10 Help for your own flow tools
When a flow becomes a tool, its author can write a description, help for each exposed input and output, an example and warnings for wrong input, which users see in tooltips, the help panel and search. Houdini authors write help in the asset's [Help tab](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/help/nodes.html) in wiki markup, with parameter tooltips, and attach their own [examples](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/help/createexamples.html); Node-RED node help follows a [style guide](https://nodered.org/docs/creating-nodes/help-style-guide) and may be Markdown since 2.1.0; ComfyUI custom nodes add [Markdown docs per node](https://docs.comfy.org/custom-nodes/help_page); Dynamo custom nodes take a [description and port comments](https://primer2.dynamobim.org/6_custom_nodes_and_packages/6-1_custom-nodes/2-creating) that appear on hover; Blender group authors raise messages with the [Warning node](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/modeling/geometry_nodes/output/warning.html). Without this, reusable tools lose meaning: a Grasshopper user found that custom input names "disappear after I create a user object" ([forum thread, 2020, third party](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/cluster-to-user-object-retain-custom-nicknames/111831)). Flow would add Description and per-port Help fields to the step that turns a flow into a tool, a "warn when" expression per input, and show them through LEARN-01 and PORT-02.
**Status:** PARTLY: "become a tool with its own command id, on the rail and in search" · **Size:** M · **Value:** 3 · **Kind:** COMPLETION · **Depends on:** flow tools with their own command id, expressions, LEARN-01 help panel, PORT-02 tooltips

<a id="learn-11"></a>

#### LEARN-11 Accessible names on every control
Every button, port, node and field has a name that screen readers and automation can read. Among the studied node editors this is rare: Node-RED 5.0 (June 2026) has ["started applying appropriate ARIA attributes across the editor"](https://nodered.org/blog/2026/06/09/version-5-0-released) and calls accessibility "a recurring theme". Research on block editors explains why it matters: blind users need the "label", "level", "size" and "position" of what they are on, and most block environments "rely exclusively on drag-and-drop" ([paper, 2018, old](https://cs.wellesley.edu//~blocksplus/2018/pdfs/blocksplus18-paper21.pdf)). Accessible names also help sighted new users indirectly, because tours (LEARN-09), tooltips (PORT-02) and the test bridge can all point at a control by its name. Flow already has accessible names on every control, together with keyboard movement between nodes. Spoken descriptions of where a node sits in the flow (its inputs and what feeds them) would be a natural follow-up, and could reuse the "Explain this number" tree.
**Status:** EXISTS · **Size:** - · **Value:** 3 · **Kind:** - · **Depends on:** -

<a id="learn-12"></a>

#### LEARN-12 Keyboard shortcut reference overlay
A searchable overlay, opened with Shift+? or from Help, that lists every canvas shortcut next to its action name. Node-RED opens its [keyboard shortcuts list with Shift-?](https://nodered.org/docs/user-guide/editor/workspace/keyboard) and lets users rebind them; ComfyUI has a [Shortcuts tab in its bottom panel](https://docs.comfy.org/interface/bottom-panel) toggled with Ctrl+Shift+K; Dynamo keeps a [shortcut list](https://github.com/DynamoDS/Dynamo/wiki/Dynamo-Keyboard-Shortcuts) on its wiki. Demand evidence is indirect: Node-RED 5.0 [added and tidied missing core actions and shortcuts](https://nodered.org/blog/2026/06/09/version-5-0-released), and ComfyUI moved its list into the app (vendor release notes and docs). Flow has a large keyboard vocabulary (Tab, arrows, Enter, F2, D, B, H, Ctrl+G, Ctrl+Shift+G, Shift+S, Ctrl+L, O, F, Ctrl+Enter, Alt-drag) that a new user cannot discover from the canvas alone. Flow would generate the overlay from the same table that defines the shortcuts, so it never drifts out of date, and show each shortcut in tooltips and context menus as well.
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** S · **Value:** 3 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** keyboard shortcuts, accessible names

<a id="learn-13"></a>

#### LEARN-13 Replay how a flow was built (build steps)
Build steps record the order in which a flow was made as named stages that a reader plays forward and back, watching nodes and results appear. GenerativeComponents stores each model as [transactions](https://docs.bentley.com/LiveContent/web/GenerativeComponents%20For%20BuildingDesigner%20Help-v6/en/DboxGcTransactions.html) that can be auto-recorded, named, suppressed and played singly ("execute the next step in the sequence of stored transactions"), and clicking a step "plays or reverses the transactions up to the selected transaction" ([Replaying Transactions](https://docs.bentley.com/LiveContent/web/OpenBuildings%20GenerativeComponents%20Help-v11/en/GUID-E5470239-2548-E917-ADE8-C0800F2DB4B3.html)). Bentley tells learners "You can play through the transactions in a file to understand them, then copy and modify them" ([GC Help](https://docs.bentley.com/LiveContent/web/GenerativeComponents%20For%20BuildingDesigner%20Help-v6/en/GUID-76F359E9-140C-43D1-8786-4E98B9825978.html), vendor docs). A Grasshopper newcomer wanted the same, asking to "'step through' a GH definition one component at a time", and David Rutten replied "Unfortunately there cannot be a step-by-step feature" because streams split and merge ([grasshopper3d forum, 2013, old](https://grasshopper3d.com/xn/detail/2985220:Comment:878230)); recording the order of authoring, not of solving, sidesteps that. Flow would tag each node with the stage in which it was added (from canvas history, one stage per group or checkpoint), save stages in the .urbflow, and add a Steps bar with stage names that shows only the nodes and drawing results up to the chosen stage; flows without history would take stages from tidy's layers.
**Status:** PARTLY: "canvas history separate from drawing history" · **Size:** M · **Value:** 3 · **Kind:** COMPLETION (canvas history) · **Depends on:** canvas history; groups with title; PERF-03 Run up to this node; tidy (layered layout)

### Performance and feedback (PERF)
