14 features: 2 EXISTS, 8 PARTLY, 4 MISSING.

<a id="perf-01"></a>

#### PERF-01 Per-node run time on the canvas
Each node shows how long it took on the last run, with totals for groups. Blender's [node timings overlay](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/modeling/geometry_nodes/inspection.html) shows "how long a node took to execute the last time", with frame totals; it arrived in [Blender 3.1](https://developer.blender.org/docs/release_notes/3.1/nodes_physics/). Grasshopper 1's Profiler widget prints a time under each component above a threshold ([grasshopper3d forum, 2013, old](https://www.grasshopper3d.com/xn/detail/2985220:Comment:972930)). Houdini's `lastCookTime()` ["Returns the duration of the node's last cook in milliseconds"](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/hom/hou/OpNode.html), and Dynamo's TuneUp, built in by [Dynamo 3.3](https://dynamobim.org/?p=55328), shows per-node and per-group times. Users need it to find the bottleneck: David Rutten's answer to a slow definition was to use the profiler ([grasshopper3d forum, 2013, old](https://www.grasshopper3d.com/forum/topics/slow/showLastReply)), and a 2022 user asked for node and total times in Grasshopper like those he logs in Dynamo ([McNeel forum, 2022](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/node-execution-time-total-execution-time/147023)). Flow would time every node, draw "1.2 s" under nodes above a threshold using the count-badge drawing, tint the slowest node, and sum times per group.
**Status:** PARTLY: "a long run recommends a mode" · **Size:** S · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** COMPLETION · **Depends on:** Run modes; groups; output port count badges

<a id="perf-02"></a>

#### PERF-02 Progress and "running now" on each node
During a run the node being computed is outlined, long nodes fill a bar of items done, and the flow shows "n of m nodes done". ComfyUI's server sends an `executing` message ["When a new node is about to be executed"](https://docs.comfy.org/development/comfyui-server/comms_messages) and a `progress` message with `value` and `max`, which a client can draw on that node. Houdini PDG shows ["A progress badge and count"](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/tops/cooking.html) with dots for work items, and with known work items "Houdini can give more accurate estimates of percent completed" ([TOPs introduction](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/tops/intro.html)). The rgthree pack adds a top-of-window bar for ComfyUI ([rgthree-comfy, third party](https://github.com/rgthree/rgthree-comfy)). Users want to know the program has not frozen: a 2012 request's "main wish was to let the user know that something is still happening" ([grasshopper3d forum, 2012, old](https://www.grasshopper3d.com/forum/topics/wish-progress-bar)), and in 2014 Rutten said a bar could only count finished components ([grasshopper3d forum, 2014, old](https://www.grasshopper3d.com/xn/detail/2985220:Comment:1050165)). Flow would outline the running node, fill bars on loops and for-each, and show "Running 12 of 40 nodes, Esc to cancel".
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** M · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** Cancel; loop and for-each counts; Background solving (PERF-06) so the canvas repaints

<a id="perf-03"></a>

#### PERF-03 Run up to this node
The user picks one node and runs only the nodes it needs, leaving the rest of the flow alone. ComfyUI's built-in [Partial Execution](https://docs.comfy.org/interface/features/partial-execution) "Only runs the branch of the workflow from the starting node to the output node"; earlier the rgthree pack added "Queue Selected Output Nodes" ([rgthree-comfy README, third party](https://github.com/rgthree/rgthree-comfy)). n8n's Execute step ["executes the specific node and any preceding nodes required to fill in its input data"](https://docs.n8n.io/workflows/executions/manual-partial-and-production-executions/). Users want to test one branch quickly: rgthree's README says "Sometimes you want to just queue one or two paths to specific output node(s) without executing the entire workflow", and n8n 1.92.0 (2025-05-05) extended partial runs so users can "test changes incrementally" ([n8n release notes 1.x](https://docs.n8n.io/release-notes/1-x/)). In manual mode Flow would add "Run to here" on the selected node (for example Ctrl+Shift+Enter): it runs only the upstream nodes, previews that branch, and dims downstream nodes as out of date until the next full run.
**Status:** PARTLY: "Run modes: automatic, manual (Ctrl+Enter)" · **Size:** M · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** COMPLETION · **Depends on:** Run modes; an out-of-date node state (STATE group)

<a id="perf-04"></a>

#### PERF-04 Re-run only what changed (result cache)
Every node keeps its last output; after an edit only nodes downstream of the change run and the rest reuse cached results. ComfyUI: ["Only parts of the graph that change from each execution to the next will be executed"](https://github.com/comfyanonymous/ComfyUI), with skipped nodes listed in an [`execution_cached` message](https://docs.comfy.org/development/comfyui-server/comms_messages). n8n calls a node "dirty" when its output is one ["n8n now considers stale or unreliable"](https://docs.n8n.io/workflows/executions/dirty-nodes/). Houdini PDG does "the minimum amount of work necessary to 'recook'" ([TOPs introduction](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/tops/intro.html)), and Dynamo's TuneUp separates nodes that ran from those [skipped for caching](https://github.com/DynamoDS/TuneUp). Users want it because big files get slow on every edit: one Grasshopper user wrote "I am struggling reducing calculation times" and switched branches off by hand ([McNeel forum, 2017](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/optimizing-complex-grasshopper-definition/50644)). Flow's dependency graph already recomputes live measures; Flow would extend it to every node's output, keyed on input values and the drawing objects a source reads, and show a "reused" tick, so automatic mode stays fast on large sites.
**Status:** PARTLY: "Live values: measures and attributes recompute when inputs change over a dependency graph" · **Size:** M · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** COMPLETION · **Depends on:** Live values; drawing change notices for sources (CAD group)

<a id="perf-05"></a>

#### PERF-05 Freeze or pin a node's output
Freezing stops a node computing but keeps passing its last result downstream, unlike disable; pinning saves that result in the file. Dynamo's Freeze is ["suspending the execution of the node"](https://primer2.dynamobim.org/4_nodes_and_wires), and "Freezing a node also freezes the nodes that are downstream of it"; [Dynamo 3.5 added freezing groups](https://github.com/DynamoDS/Dynamo/wiki/Release-Notes). Houdini's Lock flag ["'freezes' and caches the node's output geometry"](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/network/flags.html). n8n's [data pinning](https://docs.n8n.io/data/data-pinning/) reuses a saved node output in test runs, editable as JSON. Users want it while one step is slow: the Dynamo Primer says boolean operations "can be slow to calculate" and suggests freezing them ([Dynamo Primer, solids](https://primer2.dynamobim.org/5_essential_nodes_and_concepts/5-2_geometry-for-computational-design/6-solids)), and a Grasshopper user built switches on every branch to stop them computing ([McNeel forum, 2017](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/optimizing-complex-grasshopper-definition/50644)). Flow would add Freeze beside D disable and B bypass: the node keeps its last output, shows a snowflake badge with the freeze time, and feeds downstream nodes; Pin stores the output in the .urbflow so heavy analysis does not re-run on open.
**Status:** PARTLY: "D disable, B bypass" · **Size:** M · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** COMPLETION · **Depends on:** Result cache (PERF-04); saving results and lineage in .urbflow

<a id="perf-06"></a>

#### PERF-06 Background solving with a responsive canvas
Runs compute off the interface thread, so pan, zoom and Esc keep working, and a new edit cancels the stale run. For Grasshopper 2, David Rutten wrote ["As solutions will no longer be running on the UI thread, this should no longer be necessary"](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/wish-stupidity-filter-solver-time-out-limit/69819) and "A long-running solution can start and will automatically be canceled when the next one starts" (McNeel forum, 2018 and 2021). ComfyUI lists "asynchronous queueing" ([ComfyUI README](https://github.com/comfyanonymous/ComfyUI)); Houdini PDG items cooked in-process are "being processed in the background" ([TOPs introduction](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/tops/intro.html)); Dynamo 4.1 "Improved UI responsiveness by deferring expensive operations to the Dispatcher" ([Dynamo release notes](https://github.com/DynamoDS/Dynamo/wiki/Release-Notes)). Users want it because a blocking solver costs work: a Grasshopper user wrote "the only solution is often to kill the whole Rhino task" ([McNeel forum, 2018](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/wish-shortcut-to-lock-solver/54021)). Flow would compute node results on a worker and write to the drawing on the main thread at the end, as the one undo step it already makes, so commands need to split "compute" from "apply".
**Status:** PARTLY: "cancel" · **Size:** L · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** COMPLETION · **Depends on:** Cancel; commands that separate computing from applying; one undo step per run

<a id="perf-07"></a>

#### PERF-07 Runaway-run guard
Before a heavy node runs, Flow checks how many items will arrive and asks first when the number is huge, for example when a cross match multiplies two long lists into millions of items. No studied program ships this as described, so the row is flagged as a forum wish only. On the McNeel forum a user asked for a solver time-out after one accidental wire started a multi-million calculation that froze Grasshopper, and a later reply asked for a warning before operations that create very large outputs ([McNeel forum, 2018 to 2021, forum wish only](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/wish-stupidity-filter-solver-time-out-limit/69819)); Rutten answered that background solving in Grasshopper 2 would make a time-out less needed. New users make exactly this mistake when they wire a list into the wrong port. Flow already counts items on ports and limits loop time, so it would read upstream counts before a costly command and show an inline bar: "This will make 2.4 million items. Run anyway / Switch to manual".
**Status:** PARTLY: "a long run recommends a mode" and the loop's "time limit" · **Size:** S · **Value:** 3 · **Kind:** COMPLETION · **Depends on:** Output port count badges; Run modes; a cost hint per command

<a id="perf-08"></a>

#### PERF-08 Cache to disk node
A cache node writes the upstream result to a file once, then reads the file instead of computing until the user refreshes it. Houdini's [File Cache SOP](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/nodes/sop/filecache.html) can "write out cache files and then read those files on disk instead of cooking its input". Blender's [Bake node](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/modeling/geometry_nodes/geometry/operations/bake.html) "bakes parts of the node tree for better performance", packed in the .blend file or on disk. Dynamo's Data.Remember caches "the output of any node" in the .dyn file when the graph is saved ([Generative Design primer](https://www.generativedesign.org/03-hello-gd-for-revit/03-07_using-revit-alongside-gd-for-revit/03-07-02_data-remember-node-inputs)). Users want slow inputs not to run on every open: Blender's 4.1 notes present the Bake node as a way to "save and load data from inside node groups so that its inputs don't have to be recomputed" ([Blender 4.1 release notes](https://developer.blender.org/docs/release_notes/4.1/nodes_physics/)). Flow would add a Cache node storing shapes, attributes and lineage beside or inside the .urbflow, showing date and size, with Refresh; a heavy district import then opens instantly. Bake, which copies results to a layer, is different.
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** M · **Value:** 3 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** Freeze or pin (PERF-05) saving format; lineage; library folder (.urbflow)

<a id="perf-09"></a>

#### PERF-09 Skip branches nobody uses (lazy evaluation)
Only outputs that something shown, baked or exposed needs are computed, and a switch computes only the branch it picks. Blender 3.0's evaluator states ["Lazy evaluation is supported, so where possible, only the inputs and outputs that are required are computed"](https://developer.blender.org/docs/release_notes/3.0/nodes_physics/), adding that "the Separate Geometry node can be twice as fast if only one output is used". Without it users build switches by hand: a Grasshopper user with a slow file put a Dispatch with the same boolean on every parallel branch to stop branches computing ([McNeel forum, 2017](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/optimizing-complex-grasshopper-definition/50644)), and a developer wanted downstream components to solve once, only when all inputs were ready ([McNeel forum, 2018](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/skip-solving-some-components-on-the-canvas-c/75419)). Flow already draws a "lazy output" stroke on wires; Flow would make the scheduler lazy too, skipping nodes whose outputs feed nothing shown, baked or exposed, and computing only the chosen input of a switch. Skipped nodes would show a hollow badge so users understand why they have no peek.
**Status:** PARTLY: "a lazy output" · **Size:** M · **Value:** 3 · **Kind:** COMPLETION · **Depends on:** Result cache (PERF-04); Show and Select pills; Bake

<a id="perf-10"></a>

#### PERF-10 Profile report (slowest nodes list)
A panel records a run and lists nodes sorted by time, with item counts, share of the total and click-to-select. Houdini's [Performance Monitor](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/ref/panes/perfmon.html) "is a profiler that records events and generates statistical reports", shown as a tree of node cooks. TouchDesigner's [Performance Monitor dialog](https://docs.derivative.ca/Performance_Monitor_Dialog) lists "Per operation cook time" with "A green bar graph per line of output", and can report when a frame takes longer than a set number of milliseconds. Dynamo's TuneUp lets users ["Sort nodes by execution order (this is the default), name, or execution time"](https://dynamobim.org/?p=55328). Users want it because labels on nodes do not scale to big files: Grasshopper itself has no sorted list, so a forum member shared his own findBottleNeck.gh script that "filters troublemakers" ([McNeel forum, 2022](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/node-execution-time-total-execution-time/147023)). Flow would add a Profile tab listing node, group, time, share of total and items in and out, compared with the previous run; clicking a row selects and frames the node, and the test bridge could export the table. Grasshopper's MetaHopper plug-in has a Bottleneck Navigator that "opens up a window that lets you see all the components in your definition", sorted by runtime, and zooms to the one picked ([MetaHopper group page](https://www.grasshopper3d.com/group/metahopper), third party); a Mac user asked for it when it was missing there ([McNeel forum, 2021, old](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/bottleneck-navigator-on-macos/127018)).
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** S · **Value:** 2 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** Per-node run time (PERF-01); fit; test bridge

<a id="perf-11"></a>

#### PERF-11 Hold gate (data dam)
A hold node keeps the last data it passed and lets new data through only after a pause, a click or a true value, so a heavy branch waits while the rest stays live. Grasshopper's Data Dam can ["either temporarily delay or permanently block the flow of data"](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/optimizing-complex-grasshopper-definition/50644); for a permanent block "you have to manually click on the dam in order to solve the next part of the file" (David Rutten, McNeel forum, 2017). Houdini's On Mouse Up mode ["will not recook while you are tuning a parameter with a viewport or ladder handle until you release the mouse"](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/hom/hou/updateModeSetting.html), though for the whole scene. Users want more than a click: a thread asks to release a dam from data, and notes that dams "leak" by passing data once when a file opens ([McNeel forum, 2019 to 2021](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/data-dam-toggle-trigger/83158)). Flow's delay mode covers the whole flow; a Hold node would apply it to one branch, with a Release button, an optional bool input and a "holding since 10:42" badge, and would not release on file open.
**Status:** PARTLY: "delay" · **Size:** S · **Value:** 2 · **Kind:** COMPLETION · **Depends on:** Run modes; Freeze or pin (PERF-05)

<a id="perf-12"></a>

#### PERF-12 Multithreaded nodes
Nodes split list work across processor cores, and independent branches run at the same time. Grasshopper has had opt-in multithreaded components since Rhino 6: ["Multi-threaded components are decorated with small dots in the upper left corner"](https://developer.rhino3d.com/guides/grasshopper/multi-treaded-components/), switched from the right-click menu. Blender 3.0 states ["Multiple branches of the node tree are now executed in parallel where possible"](https://developer.blender.org/docs/release_notes/3.0/nodes_physics/), and Houdini's VEX ["automatically supports threading and parallel computation"](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/vex/snippets.html). Users notice idle cores: one asked why Rhino and Grasshopper use only about 25% of the CPU and whether two Python components could run in parallel; McNeel's reply was "No. Grasshopper 2 will handle this differently" ([McNeel forum, 2022](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/rhino-and-grasshopper-only-uses-up-to-25-of-cpu/136710)). Flow would run per-item work in for-each and pure geometry commands (offset, area, intersection, sun checks) in parallel when a command declares itself thread-safe, keeping drawing writes on the main thread. The gain is mostly for large site models, so it ranks below responsiveness. McNeel now says of Grasshopper 2 that "Component iterations run in parallel across all available cores, resulting in faster solutions" ([McNeel, Grasshopper 2 page, undated](https://www.rhino3d.com/stories/grasshopper-2/)).
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** L · **Value:** 2 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** Background solving (PERF-06); thread-safe commands; for-each

<a id="perf-13"></a>

#### PERF-13 Run modes with a long-run hint
The flow recomputes automatically, only on request, or after a short delay, and the program suggests a mode when runs get slow. Dynamo's execution bar offers Automatic, Manual and Periodic; Manual ["Script only runs when the 'Run' button is clicked. Useful for making changes to a complicated and 'heavy' script"](https://primer2.dynamobim.org/3_user_interface). Houdini has [Auto Update, On Mouse Up and Manual](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/basics/cooking.html) because for complex scenes "you may want to change how often Houdini cooks". Grasshopper has a Lock Solver command. Users want a quick way to stop the solver when a definition is slow or miswired; a Grasshopper user asked for a shortcut because "the only solution is often to kill the whole Rhino task" ([McNeel forum, 2018](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/wish-shortcut-to-lock-solver/54021)). Flow already has automatic, manual (Ctrl+Enter) and delay modes, and a long run recommends a mode. Per-node run time (PERF-01) would let the hint name the slow node ("Massing took 6 s; switch to manual?"), and the runaway-run guard (PERF-07) would catch problems before a run instead of after.
**Status:** EXISTS · **Size:** - · **Value:** 5 · **Kind:** - · **Depends on:** -

<a id="perf-14"></a>

#### PERF-14 Cancel a running solution
The user stops a run in progress without closing the program. Houdini: ["You can press ⎋ Esc to cancel the current cook"](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/basics/cooking.html), and its TOP cook controls cancel nodes that are cooking ([TOPs cooking](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/tops/cooking.html)). ComfyUI binds Ctrl + Alt + Enter to ["Interrupt"](https://docs.comfy.org/interface/shortcuts). Grasshopper 1 checks Escape only at set points; David Rutten wrote "If you're in an infinite loop, I'll never check escape again" ([McNeel forum, 2021](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/wish-stupidity-filter-solver-time-out-limit/69819)). Users have asked for this for a long time: in a 2012 Grasshopper thread a user requested a cancel option, and a forum regular replied that abort options would come in version 0.9 ([grasshopper3d forum, 2012, old](https://www.grasshopper3d.com/forum/topics/wish-progress-bar)). Flow already has cancel and Esc, and its loop container has a time limit. The lesson from Grasshopper is that long commands and loops must check for cancel inside their own work, not only between nodes, and that a cancelled run should leave the drawing unchanged, which Flow's one undo step per run makes possible.
**Status:** EXISTS · **Size:** - · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** - · **Depends on:** -

### Scripting and extension (SCRIPT)

14 features: 2 EXISTS, 7 PARTLY, 5 MISSING.

<a id="script-01"></a>

#### SCRIPT-01 AI assistant that builds or edits a flow from a prompt
A chat panel where the user describes a result in words and the assistant adds, wires and sets nodes, then runs the flow. n8n's [AI Workflow Builder](https://docs.n8n.io/advanced-ai/ai-workflow-builder/) "enables you to create, refine, and debug workflows using natural language descriptions of your goals"; it arrived as a beta in n8n 1.115.0 on 2025-10-06 ([n8n release notes 1.x](https://docs.n8n.io/release-notes/1-x/)). Dynamo 4.2's Autodesk Assistant ["can read and directly modify nodes on the canvas"](https://primer2.dynamobim.org/12_ai_tools); through DynamoMCP it adds and connects nodes, sets inputs, runs the graph, reads warnings and tidies the layout ([Dynamo Core 4.2 release, 2026-08-26](https://dynamobim.org/?p=56571)). For Grasshopper, third-party Raven is "A general purpose Grasshopper Assistant that can read your scripts, create parametric models and even use plugins" ([McNeel forum, 2025 to 2026, third party](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/whats-the-latest-on-ai-and-grasshopper/212902); Raven's own page not verified); that thread opens with "Are people making custom components using gpt to code?". Flow would show proposed nodes as a ghost preview with a summary, apply them as one canvas history step, and leave them unrun, so the drawing changes only when the user runs the flow.
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** L · **Value:** 5 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** Agent API (SCRIPT-06); search names, keywords and tags; canvas history

<a id="script-02"></a>

#### SCRIPT-02 Expressions typed into any input
Any number or text field on a node accepts a formula such as `width*2+3`, evaluated once or kept live. Blender: ["You can enter mathematical expressions into any number field"](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/interface/controls/buttons/fields.html), and one starting with # becomes a driver that keeps re-evaluating, such as `#frame`. Houdini: ["You can enter expressions into a parameter so its value is computed"](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/network/expressions.html), with `ch()` to read other parameters. TouchDesigner parameters ["can be driven by Python expressions"](https://docs.derivative.ca/Parameter) in Expression Mode. In Nuke, typing "= in the parameter field" opens the expression dialog ([Linking Expressions](https://learn.foundry.com/nuke/content/comp_environment/expressions/linking_expressions.html)); n8n puts `{{ ... }}` in parameters ([Expressions](https://docs.n8n.io/code/expressions/)). Grasshopper allows expressions on inputs, but they "always refer to themselves as 'x', regardless of their name", a rule that turned a new user's `c/2` red ([McNeel forum, 2020](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/expression-turning-red-in-grasshopper/99531)). Flow already turns typed numbers into source nodes; it would also accept `=` in the property editor or an inline port field, evaluate plain arithmetic once, keep `=` formulas live using real input names, and mark such ports with an fx badge. Natron's "Set Expression…" on any parameter takes a Python line that can "reference the value of other parameters" by node name, such as `Blur1.size` ([Natron 2.5 docs](https://natron.readthedocs.io/en/rb-2.5/devel/paramExpressions.html)), and the node then shows "a green “E” indicator" ([Natron 2.5 docs](https://natron.readthedocs.io/en/rb-2.5/devel/groups.html)). In Antimony "Each datum's text field is evaluated as a chunk of Python code" in a namespace "that includes nodes by name", so a field can hold `p0.x * 2`, and an invalid field turns red with the traceback shown on hover ([USAGE.md, old project](https://github.com/mkeeter/antimony/blob/develop/doc/USAGE.md)).
**Status:** PARTLY: "expressions" and "typed numbers and quoted text become source nodes" · **Size:** M · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** COMPLETION · **Depends on:** Expression editor (SCRIPT-03); property editor; Live values

<a id="script-03"></a>

#### SCRIPT-03 Expression editor with live preview and plain errors
Editing a formula offers input names and functions, previews the result on the first items while typing, and explains errors in words. n8n's expressions give ["an immediate preview of the computed values"](https://docs.n8n.io/code/expressions/). Houdini can "open the expression in a multi-line floating editor" with Ctrl+E ([Houdini expressions](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/network/expressions.html)), and Nuke has a multi-line field plus a Py button ([Linking Expressions](https://learn.foundry.com/nuke/content/comp_environment/expressions/linking_expressions.html)). Users stumble on syntax: in 2013 a Grasshopper user asked what syntax input expressions use after `x*2` failed, met the error "Parameter name does not conform to expression syntax variable naming rules", and Rutten called the name-based design a poor early choice ([grasshopper3d forum, 2013, old](https://www.grasshopper3d.com/forum/topics/expression-syntax-1)). Blender developer Jacques Lucke notes "where most attempts fail, is to properly design and justify the expression language" ([Blender devtalk, 2024](https://devtalk.blender.org/t/expression-evaluation-function-node/35374)). Flow's expression nodes and port formulas would share one editor: input names as clickable chips, a function list with one-line help, the hover peek as live result, and errors like "unknown name 'C'; the inputs here are x and width".
**Status:** PARTLY: "expressions" and "a hover peek of the first items" · **Size:** M · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** COMPLETION · **Depends on:** Expression node (SCRIPT-13); output port hover peek

<a id="script-04"></a>

#### SCRIPT-04 Per-item attribute formula (wrangle-style)
A node runs a short formula once for every item (parcel, building, floor), reading and writing attributes by name. Houdini's Attribute Wrangle ["Runs a VEX snippet to modify attribute values"](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/nodes/sop/attribwrangle.html), on "every point/primitive/vertex" or the detail, and snippets "read/write the value of an attribute using `@‹attribute_name›`" ([VEX snippets](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/vex/snippets.html)). n8n's Code node has a ["Run Once for Each Item"](https://docs.n8n.io/code/code-node/) mode. Users want text formulas because chains of maths nodes become hard to read: a Blender user wrote "some of my node graphs are becoming unreadable" and argued from Houdini that "using textual math expressions is just a lot easier to read and maintain", though others disagreed ([Blender devtalk, 2024](https://devtalk.blender.org/t/expression-evaluation-function-node/35374)). Flow would add an "Attribute formula" node: shapes in, lines such as `gfa = area * floors` and `label = name + " " + round(gfa)`, attribute keys suggested from incoming shapes, and attributes written as one undo step. It reuses Flow's expression language and for-each, and covers the floor area, plot ratio and unit mix sums that most urban-design flows need.
**Status:** PARTLY: "an attribute key", "expressions" and "for-each" · **Size:** M · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** COMPLETION · **Depends on:** SCRIPT-02; SCRIPT-03; attribute writing (CAD group)

<a id="script-05"></a>

#### SCRIPT-05 AI explains a flow, a node or an error
The user asks "what does this flow do?" or "why is this node red?" and gets a plain answer linked to the nodes, with a suggested fix. Dynamo 4.2's Assistant can "explain the open graph, find the right node, troubleshoot problems" ([Dynamo Core 4.2 release, 2026-08-26](https://dynamobim.org/?p=56571)); the Dynamo Primer shows it fixing a type mismatch from the prompt "please fix the warning on the canvas" ([Dynamo Primer, AI tools](https://primer2.dynamobim.org/12_ai_tools/1-what-is-autodesk-assistant/2-what-can-autodesk-assistant-do)). n8n's older Ask AI suggested "Explain what this workflow does" but "is no longer actively developed" ([n8n docs](https://docs.n8n.io/manage-cloud/ai-assistant/)); since n8n 2.28 (2026-06-23) opening the assistant from a failed run starts a thread "asking the agent to investigate" ([n8n release notes](https://docs.n8n.io/release-notes/)). The Primer's example, a text value wired into a number input, is a common beginner error. Flow already records lineage and opens "Explain this number" as a tree; an assistant would turn that tree into sentences ("12,400 m² is 8 blocks times their floor areas, made by Massing") and explain node errors with links that select nodes.
**Status:** PARTLY: "Every result carries lineage; 'Explain this number' opens a tree" · **Size:** M · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** COMPLETION · **Depends on:** Agent API (SCRIPT-06); model connection from SCRIPT-01; node error states (STATE)

<a id="script-06"></a>

#### SCRIPT-06 Agent API (MCP) to build and run flows
A documented tool interface lets a built-in or outside AI agent, or a script, list nodes, add and wire them, set values, run, and read warnings and results. DynamoMCP's tools ["author a Dynamo graph: they place nodes, wire them together, set values, and run the result"](https://primer2.dynamobim.org/12_ai_tools/1-what-is-autodesk-assistant/4-revitmcp-and-dynamomcp), and the page argues "A graph is a record of the work, not just its result". Unreal Engine 5.8's experimental [Unreal MCP](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/unreal-engine/unreal-mcp-in-unreal-editor?lang=en-US) "embeds an MCP server inside the Unreal Editor process", which the [5.8 release notes](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/unreal-engine/unreal-engine-5-8-release-notes?lang=en-US) say "enables agentic AI systems to connect". Houdini's `hou` module lets Python ["get information from and control Houdini"](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/hom/index.html). Demand shows in n8n 2.33 (2026-07-28), which added a guided "Connect a client" dialog for popular AI assistants, IDEs and command-line tools ([n8n release notes](https://docs.n8n.io/release-notes/)). Flow's test bridge already drives everything by data; Flow would publish it as a local MCP server, off by default, behind a permission prompt, with every change in canvas history and drawing changes only on run.
**Status:** PARTLY: "A test bridge drives everything by data" · **Size:** M · **Value:** 3 · **Kind:** COMPLETION · **Depends on:** Test bridge; canvas history; a permission prompt

<a id="script-07"></a>

#### SCRIPT-07 Python script node
A node with a code editor where the user writes Python, adds inputs and outputs, and gets results like any node. Rhino 8's Script component ["is designed to work with any of the supported languages"](https://developer.rhino3d.com/guides/scripting/scripting-component/), Python 3 and C#, with ports added by zooming and type hints per input; Rhino 8 ["brings a new code editor, support for CPython and .NET 7"](https://www.rhino3d.com/8/new/). Dynamo's Python node reads `IN` and assigns `OUT` ([Dynamo Primer](https://primer2.dynamobim.org/8_coding_in_dynamo/8-3_python/1-python)); Houdini's [Python SOP](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/nodes/sop/python.html) "Runs a Python snippet to modify the incoming geometry"; Node-RED's [Function node](https://nodered.org/docs/user-guide/writing-functions) runs JavaScript; n8n's [Code node](https://docs.n8n.io/code/code-node/) runs JavaScript or Python; Sverchok's [Scripted Node Lite](https://nortikin.github.io/sverchok/docs/nodes/script/script1_lite.html) declares sockets in a header. Users want it because, per the Dynamo Primer, "a visual program can become cluttered, and can at times fall short in functionality", and Python can "replace many nodes with a few concise lines of code". Flow would add one Python 3 node with ports declared in the editor, Flow's shape, list and attribute types, and printed output in a node log; breakpoints as in [Rhino's editor](https://www.rhino3d.com/features/developer/scripting/) can follow. In Antimony "Each node is defined by a customizable script" ([project page, 2014 to 2015, old](https://www.mattkeeter.com/projects/antimony/3/)): Python calls such as `input(name, type)` and `output(name, value)` create the node's ports ([SCRIPTING.md, old project](https://github.com/mkeeter/antimony/blob/develop/doc/SCRIPTING.md)), and a script node opens an editor with panes for the script, its output and its errors ([USAGE.md](https://github.com/mkeeter/antimony/blob/develop/doc/USAGE.md)).
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** L · **Value:** 3 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** An embedded Python runtime; typed wires; a trust prompt for shared flows

<a id="script-08"></a>

#### SCRIPT-08 AI writes an expression or a script
Inside an expression or script node the user describes the intent, such as "height from storeys at 3.2 m plus a 1 m roof", and gets code with a preview. n8n's Code node had an Ask AI tab where ["n8n sends your query to ChatGPT, then displays the result in the Code tab"](https://docs.n8n.io/code/ai-code/), Cloud only; n8n 2.39 (2026-09-08) hid the tab "as part of deprecating AI code generation", keeping its general assistant ([n8n release notes](https://docs.n8n.io/release-notes/)). Dynamo's Assistant "is able to generate nodes, custom code blocks and python nodes" ([Dynamo Primer, AI tools](https://primer2.dynamobim.org/12_ai_tools/1-what-is-autodesk-assistant/2-what-can-autodesk-assistant-do)). For Grasshopper, the third-party Runchat plugin adds nodes calling outside AI services, including LLM-written RhinoCommon code ([McNeel forum, 2026, third party](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/whats-the-latest-on-ai-and-grasshopper/212902)), where a user writes "My own 3D math and python capabilities will never equal the output that Claude and Cursor are able to deliver". Flow would put an "Ask" line in the expression editor and the Python node that proposes code, previews it on the first items, and applies only on a click; n8n's retreat suggests routing this through the one general assistant.
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** M · **Value:** 3 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** SCRIPT-01 model connection; SCRIPT-03; SCRIPT-07 for code

<a id="script-09"></a>

#### SCRIPT-09 Public SDK for third-party nodes
A documented programming interface lets plug-in developers add their own node types. In Grasshopper a component ["inherits from `GH_Component`"](https://developer.rhino3d.com/guides/grasshopper/your-first-component-windows/) and overrides `RegisterInputParams`, `RegisterOutputParams` and `SolveInstance`. With Dynamo's Zero-Touch, ["Dynamo will read the public methods of a *.dll* file and convert them to Dynamo nodes"](https://primer2.dynamobim.org/6_custom_nodes_and_packages/6-2_packages/5-zero-touch). In ComfyUI custom nodes ["The server, written in Python, handles all the real work"](https://docs.comfy.org/custom-nodes/overview), with an optional JavaScript client part, shipped through the Comfy Registry. Users want it because ecosystems grow from it: ComfyUI's docs say custom nodes let you "implement new features and share them with the wider community", and one third-party pack, rgthree-comfy, shows 3.6k GitHub stars ([GitHub, third party](https://github.com/rgthree/rgthree-comfy)). Studio commands already run from data, declare their inputs, preview and apply as one undo step, so Flow would publish that command contract as an SDK: a plug-in registers a command once and it appears as a command, in search and as a Flow node with typed ports, preview and undo.
**Status:** PARTLY: "Nodes are the program's own commands (every command runs from data, declares its inputs, previews, applies as one undo step)" · **Size:** L · **Value:** 3 · **Kind:** COMPLETION · **Depends on:** Plug-in loading in the host; packages (COLLAB)

<a id="script-10"></a>

#### SCRIPT-10 Package installs for script nodes
A script node declares the outside libraries it needs, and the program installs them. In Rhino 8, ["You can specify the packages required for your scripts inside the script source"](https://developer.rhino3d.com/guides/scripting/scripting-component/), with `#r: numpy` for Python 3 and a NuGet line for C#; McNeel says "Add any package from PyPI with a single line at the top of your script and the editor installs it for you" ([Rhino scripting](https://www.rhino3d.com/features/developer/scripting/)). Self-hosted n8n ["can import and use built-in and external npm modules in the Code node"](https://docs.n8n.io/code/code-node/), Cloud cannot; Node-RED loads extra modules through its settings file ([Writing Functions](https://nodered.org/docs/user-guide/writing-functions)). Users want it because useful libraries live outside the program: Dynamo's migration guide lists "A total inability to use modern PyPI packages" as a reason to leave IronPython 2.7 ([Dynamo Primer, migrating to Python 3](https://primer2.dynamobim.org/1_developer_primer_intro/0-introduction)). Flow would read a `# requires: shapely, pandas` line in the Python node, install into a separate environment, save pinned versions with the .urbflow, allow an office mirror, and warn before installing for a linked flow from someone else.
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** M · **Value:** 2 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** Python script node (SCRIPT-07); .urbflow; linked flows

<a id="script-11"></a>

#### SCRIPT-11 Publish a script as a command
A script, or a flow containing one, becomes a named node and command with icon, category and help that colleagues use without seeing code. Rhino 8 script projects ["Convert scripts into Rhino commands and publish as Rhino plugin"](https://developer.rhino3d.com/guides/scripting/projects-create/) and "Convert scripts into Grasshopper components", with icons and panels; McNeel says teams "install them like any other plugin, no scripting required" ([Rhino scripting](https://www.rhino3d.com/features/developer/scripting/)). In Houdini users "Right click the subnet and choose Create digital asset", and the asset appears in the Tab menu ([Houdini, creating assets](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/assets/create.html)). Users do this but hit friction: people building plugins from the Script Editor asked about legacy components and icons, and after an update were told to "Open the gh files in 8.11, save, and rebuild your plugins" ([McNeel forum, 2024](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/creating-rhino-gh-plugins-in-script-editor/189738)). Flow already turns a flow with an exposed slider into a tool with its own command id; flows containing Python nodes would publish the same way, carrying help text, an icon and the package list.
**Status:** PARTLY: "A flow can expose a slider and become a tool with its own command id" · **Size:** M · **Value:** 2 · **Kind:** COMPLETION · **Depends on:** SCRIPT-07; SCRIPT-10; library folder

<a id="script-12"></a>

#### SCRIPT-12 Flow to text and back (node to code, copy as script)
Selected nodes convert to equivalent text, and a flow can be written out as readable code that rebuilds it. In Dynamo users "select an array of nodes in your Dynamo graph, right-click on the canvas and select 'Node to Code'. Dynamo condenses these nodes into a code block", which the Primer calls "a really helpful tool to learn how to script in Dynamo" ([Dynamo Primer, DesignScript syntax](https://primer2.dynamobim.org/8_coding_in_dynamo/8-1_code-blocks-and-design-script/2-design-script-syntax)). Houdini's `asCode()` ["Prints the Python code necessary to recreate a node"](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/hom/hou/OpNode.html), optionally for a whole network. Enso's IDE works with ["the visual form of Enso"](https://github.com/enso-org/enso) and offers "code editing options for developers". Users ask for the reverse too: in Blender's expression-node discussion a user proposed "A new Create nodes from expression operator" ([Blender devtalk, 2024](https://devtalk.blender.org/t/expression-evaluation-function-node/35374)). Flow would add "Copy as text" for a selection, a compact readable form for forum help, version comparison and assistant context, and "Paste text as nodes" to rebuild it; turning a selection into one Python node would come later. GenerativeComponents keeps each node as a script statement: "Edit Statement — Opens a Script Editor window with selected node's statement code to edit", and another command opens the whole transaction list as script ([GC Help v6, Transactions dialog](https://docs.bentley.com/LiveContent/web/GenerativeComponents%20For%20BuildingDesigner%20Help-v6/en/DboxGcTransactions.html)).
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** L · **Value:** 2 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** Test bridge data format; SCRIPT-07

<a id="script-13"></a>

#### SCRIPT-13 Expression node
A node that evaluates a typed formula over its inputs. Grasshopper's Expression component is listed as "Evaluate an expression", with inputs x and y and a result R ([grasshopperdocs, third party](https://grasshopperdocs.com/components/grasshoppermaths/expression.html)); "You can quickly edit the expression by double-clicking the Eval component" ([Building Simplexity Lab tutorial, 2022, third party](https://dev.bsl.arch.hku.hk/tutorials/introduction/basic-components/complex-expressions/)). Max's expr object will ["Evaluate an expression using a C-like language"](https://docs.cycling74.com/reference/expr), taking inlets as `$i1` or `$f1`. Dynamo's code blocks hold DesignScript formulas ([Dynamo Primer, DesignScript syntax](https://primer2.dynamobim.org/8_coding_in_dynamo/8-1_code-blocks-and-design-script/2-design-script-syntax)). Users want formulas in text because chains of maths nodes grow long: a Blender user asked for a node that evaluates math in text form because his graphs were becoming unreadable, and a reply pointed to an open work-in-progress pull request for such a node ([Blender devtalk, 2024](https://devtalk.blender.org/t/expression-evaluation-function-node/35374)). Flow already has expression nodes. The open work is around them: formulas in any input (SCRIPT-02), a helpful editor (SCRIPT-03) and per-item attribute formulas (SCRIPT-04), all reusing one language so users learn it once.
**Status:** EXISTS · **Size:** - · **Value:** 5 · **Kind:** - · **Depends on:** -

<a id="script-14"></a>

#### SCRIPT-14 Commands appear as nodes automatically
Every command of the host program is available as a node without anyone writing a wrapper. Dynamo's Zero-Touch import reads the public methods of a library and converts them to nodes ([Dynamo Primer, Zero-Touch](https://primer2.dynamobim.org/6_custom_nodes_and_packages/6-2_packages/5-zero-touch)), and the DynamoMCP page notes that "Every node in the Dynamo library is itself one or more API calls, already written, tested, and documented" ([Dynamo Primer, DynamoMCP](https://primer2.dynamobim.org/12_ai_tools/1-what-is-autodesk-assistant/4-revitmcp-and-dynamomcp)). Rhino 8 script projects turn each script into a command or component ([Rhino scripting projects](https://developer.rhino3d.com/guides/scripting/projects-create/)). Users want it because a node editor reaches only as far as its library: the same Dynamo page explains that a small set of graph tools "reaches the entire Dynamo library" because each node is already an API call. Flow has this in its strongest form, since its nodes are the program's own commands, and every command runs from data, declares its inputs, previews and applies as one undo step; other programs reach it through wrappers, packages or plug-ins. The open work is exposing the same contract to outside developers (SCRIPT-09) and to agents (SCRIPT-06).
**Status:** EXISTS · **Size:** - · **Value:** 5 · **Kind:** - · **Depends on:** -

### CAD integration (CAD)

12 features: 1 EXISTS, 8 PARTLY, 3 MISSING.

<a id="cad-01"></a>

#### CAD-01 Managed bake that replaces its previous output
A managed bake remembers which drawing objects each flow output created, so the next bake replaces or updates those objects instead of piling up copies. Rhino 8 Grasshopper's [Content Cache](https://www.rhino3d.com/docs/guides/grasshopper/baking/) "keeps track of the objects that have been baked" and replaces them under the same object ID. Elefront does it through a Bake Name, which "ensures that re-baking deletes old geometry instead of duplicating" ([Columbia GSAPP tutorial](https://smorgasbord.cdp.arch.columbia.edu/modules/26-establishing-underlying-organizational-structure/261-leveraging-object-attributes), third party). Dynamo has [object binding](https://primer2.dynamobim.org/dynamo-for-civil-3d/advanced-topics/object-binding.md) with a choice of where the binding is stored. Rhino.Inside.Revit has [Element Tracking](https://www.rhino3d.com/inside/revit/1.0/guides/rir-grasshopper) with Disabled, Replace and Update modes. McNeel's [baking page](https://www.rhino3d.com/features/grasshopper/baking) admits that users used to "keep copies or delete old geometry before baking again". A [forum thread](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/bake-a-grasshopper-object-automatically/54578) (2018, old) answers a request for automatic baking by recommending Elefront because it overwrites earlier bakes. In Flow, each Bake would stamp a stable bake id on what it makes, next to the Made-by data, and offer three choices: add copies, replace the previous bake (the default), or update in place. Re-baking after a slider change would swap objects as one undo step, and a Release command would hand the objects back to the drawing as ordinary objects.
**Status:** PARTLY: "Bake copies results to a layer" · **Size:** M · **Value:** 5 · **Kind:** COMPLETION · **Depends on:** Bake copies results to a layer; Made-by badge; apply as one undo step

<a id="cad-02"></a>

#### CAD-02 Query drawing objects by rule
A rule-based query source gathers drawing objects by combined filters: layer with sublayers, object type, name, attribute key and value, and block name. It stays current as matching objects are added or deleted. Rhino 8 Grasshopper's [Query Model Objects](https://www.rhino3d.com/docs/guides/grasshopper/group-sort-filter) filters by layer, and a "*" wildcard takes in sublayers. Rhino.Inside.Revit combines category, parameter and level [filters with Query Elements](https://www.rhino3d.com/inside/revit/1.0/guides/revit-elements). Dynamo has [All Elements of Category and All Elements of Family Type](https://primer2.dynamobim.org/7_dynamo_for_revit/2-selecting). Elefront has referenced objects "back into Grasshopper" by key-value attributes since its [2013 release](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/elefront-for-grasshopper/1026) (old). Demand is current. In March 2026 a user asked whether objects can be queried "by really any attribute or do you need to grab them all and then filter" ([thread](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/query-model-object-by-user-key-value/216510)). In September 2026 another wanted to load objects by layer without retyping its name and found the native tools "a bit unintuitive" ([thread](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/cant-use-queried-layer-to-query-objects-how-load-objects-by-layer/222608)). Flow would merge its separate layer and attribute-key sources into one Drawing query node. The rules would be picked from the drawing's own layer tree and key list, never typed. A count badge would show the matches, and the node would recompute when matching objects change.
**Status:** PARTLY: "sources (a layer, the selection, a saved view, an attribute key, ...)" · **Size:** M · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** COMPLETION · **Depends on:** Sources (a layer, an attribute key); Live link per drawing object (CAD-05)

<a id="cad-03"></a>

#### CAD-03 Bake with attributes (layer, colour, name, attribute values)
Baking with attributes means each baked result gets its layer, colour, line style, name and attribute key-value pairs from flow data, and missing layers are created. [New in Rhino 8](https://www.rhino3d.com/8/new/) says "Rhino Data Types allow you to bake geometry with custom attributes". The [Model Object component](https://www.rhino3d.com/docs/guides/grasshopper/object-attributes/) assigns a name and layer before the bake. Elefront's Bake Objects has an attributes input, so the data "can be queried later" ([GSAPP tutorial](https://smorgasbord.cdp.arch.columbia.edu/modules/26-establishing-underlying-organizational-structure/261-leveraging-object-attributes), third party). Users keep asking for this. A [2022 thread](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/changing-adding-parameters-to-rhinoceros-objects-using-a-gh-or-plugins/141959) asks how to add parameters to Rhino objects from Grasshopper, and the answer is a plugin (Elefront or Human). In the [Rhino 8 blocks thread](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/rhino-8-feature-build-blocks-better-using-grasshopper/149081), a user in January 2023 asked for user text that persists after baking. Flow's Bake would gain optional per-item ports for layer path, colour, name and attribute pairs, with defaults in the property editor. Baked plots would then carry, say, use=residential and their GFA, so schedules, live values and Explain this number can read them later. In Grasshopper 2 values carry metadata "such as a zone, a material, or a layer name", and "Baking is now metadata-aware", so "the relevant object attributes, such as layer or name, will be assigned to the shapes sent to the Rhino document" ([McNeel, Grasshopper 2 page, undated](https://www.rhino3d.com/stories/grasshopper-2/)).
**Status:** PARTLY: "Bake copies results to a layer" · **Size:** M · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** COMPLETION · **Depends on:** Bake copies results to a layer; Managed bake (CAD-01)

<a id="cad-04"></a>

#### CAD-04 Write attributes onto existing drawing objects
This node sets, merges or deletes attribute keys, and the name, layer or colour, on objects already in the drawing, without re-creating them. Rhino 8 added a [User Text component](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/three-new-gh1-components-in-rhino-8-wip/154053) with Merge, Set and Delete modes; its [feature page](https://www.rhino3d.com/features/grasshopper/usertext/) says it can "add, change, or remove user text data from any Rhino object". [New in Rhino 8](https://www.rhino3d.com/8/new/) lists Content Cache to "Update existing Rhino objects from Grasshopper". Dynamo edits Revit elements with [Element.SetParameterByName](https://primer2.dynamobim.org/7_dynamo_for_revit/3-editing). Beginners hit this gap. A [2020 thread](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/changing-referenced-object-attributes/114775) from a self-described Grasshopper newcomer could only create new objects with attributes, not change existing ones, and got no reply. In the [2023 announcement thread](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/three-new-gh1-components-in-rhino-8-wip/154053), McNeel said updating an existing object's user text in place "isn't possible" yet. Flow would get a Set attributes command node. Its inputs would be objects from any source, key, values and mode. Its preview would show a table of old and new values, and Apply would write them as one undo step. Live values would then recompute every measure that reads those attributes. Before Rhino 8, plug-ins filled this gap: the 2020 newcomer had found Elefront's "Modify Rhino Object Attributes" but no example of its use, and a 2018 request to update user text on existing objects was answered with Human's "Modify Attributes" with "Create Attributes" and "I use Elefront plugin" ([McNeel forum, 2018, old](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/update-user-text-with-grasshopper/61207)).
**Status:** PARTLY: "an attribute key" (source) and "Live values: measures and attributes recompute" · **Size:** M · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** COMPLETION · **Depends on:** Sources; an attribute key; Live values; preview and one undo step

<a id="cad-05"></a>

#### CAD-05 Live link per drawing object, with missing-object state
With a live link, each source tracks the exact drawing objects it reads by id. Editing one re-runs the parts of the flow that depend on it, and deleting one shows a clear missing state instead of an error or a hang. Grasshopper references work this way: "When you move the point in Rhino, its reference in Grasshopper updates as well" ([McNeel developer guide](https://developer.rhino3d.com/guides/grasshopper/gh-algorithms-and-data-structures/algorithms-data/)). Dynamo's Select Model Element "remains dynamically linked to that Revit object" ([primer](https://primer2.dynamobim.org/7_dynamo_for_revit/2-selecting)). Houdini Engine's World Inputs re-cook with Update Automatically on by default ([docs](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/unreal/inputs.html)). The pain points are real. Grasshopper hung while looking for about 700 absent referenced curves ([thread](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/grasshopper-hangs-when-references-absent/4354), 2013, old). In January 2023, Rhino 8 users reported that Query Model Objects keeps pushing output and triggering repeated runs, and asked for an auto-update toggle with a manual update button ([thread](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/rhino-8-feature-build-blocks-better-using-grasshopper/149081)). Flow already recomputes live values over a dependency graph, and its linked flows have missing states. Flow sources would subscribe to changes on the objects they read and mark themselves stale. They would re-run downstream in automatic mode. A deleted object would get the linked-flow treatment: a missing state with re-pick, drop or freeze.
**Status:** PARTLY: "sources (a layer, the selection, ...)", "Run modes: automatic", "Live values: measures and attributes recompute when inputs change over a dependency graph" · **Size:** M · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** COMPLETION · **Depends on:** Sources; Run modes: automatic; Live values dependency graph; linked-flow missing states as a pattern

<a id="cad-06"></a>

#### CAD-06 Block definitions and instances
This feature reads block instances as data (block name, placement, scale, attributes) and creates or updates block definitions and instances from a flow. Urban designers need it for trees, lamp posts, cars, people and standard building types. [New in Rhino 8](https://www.rhino3d.com/8/new/) lists "Create block definitions and instances in Grasshopper", and McNeel's [announcement](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/rhino-8-feature-build-blocks-better-using-grasshopper/149081) (October 2022) says attributes are "passed along to each of your block instances". Dynamo for Civil 3D's [light pole sample](https://primer2.dynamobim.org/dynamo-for-civil-3d/sample-workflows/roads/light-pole-placement.md) places block references along a corridor at stations read from Excel. Houdini Engine turns packed primitives into instanced meshes and foliage ([outputs](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/unreal/outputs.html)). The pain was real before this. In a [2019 thread](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/desperately-need-help-to-bake-blocks-into-rhino/88520) (old), the bake components of three plugins failed for blocks. The 2022 announcement thread drew requests for nested blocks and for dimensions inside blocks. Flow would get a Place blocks command node with inputs for block name, points or planes, rotation, scale and attributes, plus a Block instances source that outputs placements. Previews would draw on the flow's layer, count badges would show instance counts, and a managed bake would keep instances in step after re-runs.
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** M · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** Managed bake (CAD-01); Bake with attributes (CAD-03)

<a id="cad-07"></a>

#### CAD-07 Data-driven labels and annotation
Data-driven annotation means labels, tags, dimensions and hatches whose text and style come from flow data, such as "Plot 4 · 2,350 m² GFA", and which update on every run. [New in Rhino 8](https://www.rhino3d.com/8/new/) lets Grasshopper "Define the look of annotations". In a [2024 thread](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/rhino-8-grasshopper-drafting-tools-detail-view/176986), Rhino 8 components create hatches, lines and text inside layouts, but users still cannot automate scale text because they cannot reach detail views. Demand shows in the [Rhino 8 blocks thread](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/rhino-8-feature-build-blocks-better-using-grasshopper/149081): a user asked in 2022 for dimensions and leaders, and McNeel answered that annotation objects were planned. Flow commands already run from data, so the program's own label and hatch commands are nodes. What Flow lacks is a Label node made for data. It would use a text template with placeholders such as {name} and {GFA:0,0} m², take its look from the drawing's text styles, and place labels at each item's centre or a chosen point. Labels would live-update through the dependency graph, preview on the flow's layer and bake as real annotation.
**Status:** PARTLY: "Nodes are the program's own commands (every command runs from data ...)" · **Size:** M · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** COMPLETION · **Depends on:** Nodes are the program's own commands; Live values; Units and site coordinates (CAD-08); Managed bake (CAD-01)

<a id="cad-08"></a>

#### CAD-08 Units and site coordinates
Unit awareness means values show and convert the drawing's units (m, m², ha, %). Flows could also run around a local site origin when the drawing uses georeferenced coordinates kilometres from zero. Dynamo removed units from values in 2015 ("Units are not attached to the input or output") and kept a Convert Between Units node ([post](https://dynamobim.org/?p=13242), 2015, old). In 2017 it added a Geometry Working Range setting that warns when inputs fall outside the range ([post](https://dynamobim.org/?p=48087), 2017, old). In Archicad PARAM-O, "every number value is in meters", but a Length node follows the project's working units ([help](https://help.graphisoft.com/AC/24/INT/_AC24_Help/PARAM-O/PARAM-O-5.htm)). One user's cadastral sites sit several kilometres from the origin, with slow move-and-return workarounds ([thread](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/the-pain-with-models-far-away-from-the-origin/26790), 2015, old). A Ladybug user's building about 9,000 km from the origin gave broken meshes, and the answer was "always try and move the geometry to the origin" ([thread](https://discourse.ladybug.tools/t/how-to-properly-treat-real-world-coordinates/14581), 2021, third party). In Flow, sliders, port peeks and badges would show units from the document settings, and conversions would happen at the ports. A flow could also run in a local frame around a site origin read from the drawing and move results back on apply, with a warning when inputs sit far from the origin. Woods Bagot's open-source Wombat plug-in for Grasshopper has a Unit Warning component that "Provides a warning when the Rhino document unit system doesn't match the user-specified unit system" and offers to switch the document to the expected units ([Wombat source on GitHub](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/woodsbagot/WombatGH/master/WombatGH/UnitWarning.cs), third party).
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** M · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** Values (number, slider); count badges and hover peek; Explain this number

<a id="cad-09"></a>

#### CAD-09 Flow-driven parametric object in the drawing
A flow-driven object places a flow in the drawing as an object. Each instance keeps its own parameter values, which are edited in the property editor. The object re-runs when those values or its control shapes change, and it copies like any other object. Vectorworks [Marionette objects](https://app-help.vectorworks.net/2026/eng/VW2026_Guide/Marionette/Editing_Marionette_objects.htm) have instance parameters "edited directly from the Object Info palette" plus editable control geometry. [Archicad PARAM-O](https://help.graphisoft.com/AC/25/INT/_AC25_Help/065_PARAM-O/065_PARAM-O-1.htm) saves its result "as a regular ARCHICAD object". Houdini Engine assets re-cook when their inputs change ([docs](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/unreal/inputs.html)). VisualARQ's [Grasshopper Styles](https://help.visualarq.com/architectural-objects/grasshopper-styles/) turn a definition's inputs into object properties. Users have asked Rhino for this: a [2017 request](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/grasshopper-objects/42624) (old) wanted definitions bound to objects with parameters in Properties, and named simpler use for beginners as one benefit. An [AECbytes article](https://aecbytes.com/tipsandtricks/2017/issue78-marionette.html) (2017, third party, old) notes that Marionette users change "the parameters of the Marionette object" rather than redraw. Flow can already turn a flow into a tool with exposed sliders. A Place as object step would keep the tool's result live: its sliders would show per instance in the property editor and its input shapes (such as a plot boundary) would act as grips. Explode would release plain shapes.
**Status:** PARTLY: "A flow can expose a slider and become a tool with its own command id" · **Size:** L · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** COMPLETION · **Depends on:** Flow as a tool with its own command id; exposed sliders; Managed bake (CAD-01); Live link per drawing object (CAD-05)

<a id="cad-10"></a>

#### CAD-10 Freeze a drawing source into a stored snapshot
Freezing turns a live layer or selection source into shapes stored inside the flow file. The flow then works in another drawing, or after the originals change, and can be re-linked later. Grasshopper separates referenced data from internal data, where "Data is stored inside the GH file". McNeel's [developer guide](https://developer.rhino3d.com/guides/grasshopper/gh-algorithms-and-data-structures/algorithms-data/) warns that otherwise "the Rhino file needs to be saved and passed along with the GH file to avoid any loss of data". Dynamo for Civil 3D lets a graph [store its binding](https://primer2.dynamobim.org/dynamo-for-civil-3d/advanced-topics/object-binding.md) "Store in Graph for Dynamo" or "Store in Drawing For Dynamo", which decides what happens when the graph runs in another drawing. The need shows in a 2013 thread (old) where a definition with about 700 referenced curves [hung when opened in an empty file](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/grasshopper-hangs-when-references-absent/4354). Internalising was the suggested fix, with a warning that it makes files grow fast. Flow already has pasted shapes as a source. A Freeze command on any drawing source would convert it to pasted shapes kept in the .urbflow and show their size. Re-link would point it back at a layer or the selection. Library flows shared as tools would then carry example input.
**Status:** PARTLY: "pasted shapes" · **Size:** S · **Value:** 3 · **Kind:** COMPLETION · **Depends on:** Sources (pasted shapes); flows save to a library folder (.urbflow)

<a id="cad-11"></a>

#### CAD-11 Sheets, layouts and viewports from a flow
This feature lets a flow create and update sheets. A flow would start from a title block, place viewports of saved views at a scale, and fill title-block fields from data, for example one sheet per design option or per plot. Autodesk's Generative Design sample for Revit [places views on sheets](https://www.generativedesign.org/04-sample-workflows/04-05_bim-workflows/04-05-01_placement-of-views-on-sheets) and notes that reordering them by hand "would take hours, if not days". Rhino 8 Grasshopper reaches layouts, but detail views were still missing in [March 2024](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/rhino-8-grasshopper-drafting-tools-detail-view/176986). The [Drafthorse](https://github.com/jkamm/Drafthorse_rh8) plugin fills the gap with New Layout and Modify Layout components. Users keep asking for this. A [2022 thread](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/automate-layout-creation-in-grasshopper/136824) needed many layouts and details from Grasshopper after a plugin broke in Rhino 7, and a Python workaround put details in the wrong place. Flow already takes "a saved view" as a source. It would add a Sheets source and a Make sheet command node with inputs for title block, name and number, a list of viewports (saved view, scale, frame) and field values. A managed bake would keep the sheets in step after re-runs, with one undo step per apply.
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** L · **Value:** 3 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** Sources (a saved view); Managed bake (CAD-01); Data-driven labels (CAD-07)

<a id="cad-12"></a>

#### CAD-12 Drawing sources: layer, selection, saved view, attribute key, spreadsheet range, pasted shapes
Drawing sources are the nodes that bring the drawing into a flow. Grasshopper references Rhino objects and also stores internal data ([developer guide](https://developer.rhino3d.com/guides/grasshopper/gh-algorithms-and-data-structures/algorithms-data/)). Rhino 8 [Query Model Objects](https://www.rhino3d.com/docs/guides/grasshopper/group-sort-filter) takes objects by layer. Dynamo picks a Revit element with [Select Model Element](https://primer2.dynamobim.org/7_dynamo_for_revit/2-selecting). [Dynamo in Forma](https://primer2.dynamobim.org/dynamo-in-forma-beta/run-graphs-in-dynamo-player.md), Autodesk's site-planning tool, sends the user to "a selection mode where you can select elements in the scene". Users want these sources to be simple: in a [September 2026 thread](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/cant-use-queried-layer-to-query-objects-how-load-objects-by-layer/222608), a user wanted to load a layer's objects without retyping the layer name. Flow already has all six source kinds: a layer, the selection, a saved view, an attribute key, a spreadsheet range and pasted shapes. Typed numbers and quoted text also become source nodes. The features above build on these sources: rule-based queries (CAD-02), per-object live links (CAD-05) and freezing (CAD-10). Pickers that list what is actually in the drawing would keep sources easy for new users.
**Status:** EXISTS · **Size:** - · **Value:** 5 · **Kind:** - · **Depends on:** -

### Automation (AUTO)

10 features: 1 EXISTS, 4 PARTLY, 5 MISSING.

<a id="auto-01"></a>

#### AUTO-01 Option sweep with a results table
An option sweep runs a flow over ranges, lists or random values of its exposed sliders. For each option it records chosen numbers (GFA, FAR, open space) and a picture, then shows a table to sort, compare and restore from. Houdini's [Wedge TOP](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/nodes/top/wedge.html) can "vary settings across a range, choose from a list of values, or pick random values". Revit [Generative Design](https://help.autodesk.com/cloudhelp/2022/ENU/Revit-Model/files/GUID-887BCC49-06E9-4C52-AECD-BE92646DF2F5.htm) runs Dynamo graphs with Randomize (default 40 outcomes) and Cross-product. [Colibri](https://github.com/tt-acm/Colibri.Grasshopper) turns Grasshopper definitions "into Design Explorer-compatible data sets" (2016). Users want a built-in version. In a [2020 thread](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/combining-anemone-and-colibri/99181), Colibri recorded only the first loop iteration of each variant, and the user fell back to brute force. Autodesk's [sheet sample](https://www.generativedesign.org/04-sample-workflows/04-05_bim-workflows/04-05-01_placement-of-views-on-sheets) says a manual search "would take hours, if not days". Flow already has exposed sliders, loops with a time limit and a long-run mode recommendation. An Options panel would show the run count and an estimated time before starting, then run each option without baking. Each row would keep the chosen outputs and a capture, sortable by any figure. Use this option would set the sliders and run once.
**Status:** PARTLY: "loop with pass count, stop expression and time limit" and "A flow can expose a slider" · **Size:** M · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** COMPLETION · **Depends on:** Exposed sliders; loop container (pass count, time limit); long-run mode recommendation; Write results to a spreadsheet (AUTO-02)

<a id="auto-02"></a>

#### AUTO-02 Write results to a spreadsheet or report on each run
This output node writes a table of results to an .xlsx sheet, a named range or a .csv file, with one row per item and one column per attribute. It refreshes the same place on each run. Houdini's [CSV Output TOP](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/nodes/top/csvoutput.html) "Writes work item attributes to a CSV file". n8n's [Convert to File](https://docs.n8n.io/integrations/builtin/core-nodes/n8n-nodes-base.converttofile/) writes CSV, XLS, XLSX and ODS. [Colibri](https://github.com/tt-acm/Colibri.Grasshopper) writes data sets from Grasshopper runs. The need shows in massing work. In a [2017 forum post](https://www.grasshopper3d.com/xn/detail/2985220:Comment:1677087) (old, third party), a designer wanted a script that reads a massing model, calculates its floor area and exports the result to Excel. The only reply pointed to the forum search. Flow reads "a spreadsheet range" but cannot write one. A Write range node would fill a named range or sheet, take its headers from attribute keys and offer overwrite or append. It would write on apply, on demand or before save (AUTO-05). Each written cell could keep its lineage, so Explain this number still works from the spreadsheet.
**Status:** PARTLY: "a spreadsheet range" (as a source only) · **Size:** M · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** COMPLETION · **Depends on:** Sources (a spreadsheet range); lineage; Run on drawing events (AUTO-05)

<a id="auto-03"></a>

#### AUTO-03 Re-run when an input file changes (file watcher)
A file watcher notices when a file the flow reads, such as an area brief spreadsheet a colleague maintains, is saved. It then marks the source changed and re-runs the flow, or offers a reload. Node-RED's [watch node](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/node-red/node-red/master/packages/node_modules/%40node-red/nodes/locales/en-US/storage/23-watch.html) "Watches a directory or file for changes". n8n's [Local File Trigger](https://docs.n8n.io/integrations/builtin/core-nodes/n8n-nodes-base.localfiletrigger/) starts a workflow when a file or folder is "added, changed, or deleted". In Dynamo for Civil 3D, if the graph runs in Automatic mode and "you edit the Excel file, the Block References update automatically" ([primer](https://primer2.dynamobim.org/dynamo-for-civil-3d/sample-workflows/roads/light-pole-placement.md)). Grasshopper's David Rutten said in [2013](https://www.grasshopper3d.com/xn/detail/2985220:Comment:842127) (old) that Grasshopper "typically updates in the wake of events, not timers" and uses a file watcher. Earlier, a [2011 thread](https://www.grasshopper3d.com/xn/detail/2985220:Comment:202141) (old) dealt with Read File output not updating when its file changed. Flow already tracks changed-on-disk and missing states for linked flows. The same watch on spreadsheet-range and file sources would mark them stale. In automatic mode the flow would re-run; in other modes the source would show a Reload pill. Thornton Tomasetti's TT Toolbox for Grasshopper shipped "an Excel reader/listener that keeps a live link to an Excel spreadsheet and automatically detects changes" next to its Excel writer ([Digital Engineering 24/7, 2014, old, third party](https://www.digitalengineering247.com/article/tt-toolbox-1-5-grasshopper-plug-available)), and CORE studio's own page lists "A lightning fast Excel Reader/ Writer" ([CORE studio group page](https://www.grasshopper3d.com/group/corestudio), third party).
**Status:** PARTLY: "a spreadsheet range" and "changed-on-disk and missing states, reload" · **Size:** S · **Value:** 3 · **Kind:** COMPLETION · **Depends on:** Sources (a spreadsheet range); changed-on-disk states and reload; Run modes

<a id="auto-04"></a>

#### AUTO-04 Batch run over many drawings
A batch run takes a folder or list of drawings and runs one flow on each: open, run, apply, then save, save a copy or export, with a log. Houdini's [File Pattern TOP](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/nodes/top/filepattern.html) "generates a work item for each file or directory name that matches a pattern". The third-party [Revit Batch Processor](https://github.com/bvn-architecture/RevitBatchProcessor) offers "Fully automated batch processing of Revit files with your own Python or Dynamo task scripts". Rhino can start with a [/runscript switch](https://docs.mcneel.com/rhino/8/help/en-us/information/startingrhino.htm) and run definitions through the [scriptable -GrasshopperPlayer](https://docs.mcneel.com/rhino/8/help/en-us/commands/grasshopper.htm). Without built-in support, users struggle. A [2021 thread](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/grasshopper-batch-execution/119959) about 1,000 files ended with a script that "does absolutely nothing". In a [2019 thread](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/open-and-run-grasshopper-from-a-batch-file/80261) (old), a batch file worked only after path and naming tricks. In Flow, a Run on files dialog would pick a library flow, the drawings, and input values, either fixed or one spreadsheet row per file. It would also set what happens after each run: save, save as copy or export. Files would run one after another, and a log would record each file's result.
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** M · **Value:** 3 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** Flow as a command (command id); Batch error handling (AUTO-07); flow library

<a id="auto-05"></a>

#### AUTO-05 Run on drawing events (open, before save, before export)
Event-bound flows run on their own when something happens to the drawing: when it opens, before it saves, or before export or print. A typical use is refreshing an area schedule. Blender's [application handlers](https://docs.blender.org/api/current/bpy.app.handlers.html) run code "on loading a new blend file" (load_post) and before saving (save_pre). Houdini runs 456.py "whenever a scene file is loaded" and runs beforescenesave.py before saving ([docs](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/hom/locations.html)). Rhino can "Run a script at startup" ([start-up switches](https://docs.mcneel.com/rhino/8/help/en-us/information/startingrhino.htm)). Users improvise this. In a [2018 thread](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/trigger-definition-to-run-on-start/76609) (old), a user wanted a definition to start by itself so a non-Grasshopper colleague could drop files in a folder. The workaround hooked a Python component to Rhino's start-up event and needed a guard against endless loops. In Flow, the settings would gain a Run when option: drawing opens, before save, before export. A panel would list every event-bound flow with an off switch. Each run would apply as one undo step and announce itself, so the automation never surprises a new user.
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** M · **Value:** 3 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** Flow as a command; apply as one undo step

<a id="auto-06"></a>

#### AUTO-06 Command-line and headless runs
A command-line run starts a saved flow on a drawing from a terminal or script, passes input values, writes output files, and needs no user interface. [DynamoCLI](https://github.com/DynamoDS/Dynamo/wiki/Dynamo-Command-Line-Interface) opens a .dyn "in a headless mode that will run the graph". Houdini ships [hython](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/hom/commandline.html), which loads .hip files given on the command line. Blender's [--background](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/advanced/command_line/arguments.html) runs "often used for UI-less rendering" together with --python. n8n starts saved workflows with [n8n execute --id](https://docs.n8n.io/hosting/cli-commands/). Rhino.Compute serves Rhino and Grasshopper "through a stateless REST API" ([guide](https://developer.rhino3d.com/guides/compute/)). Users ask for this to set parameters from outside. A [2019 thread](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/open-and-run-grasshopper-from-a-batch-file/80261) (old) wanted radius and count set from a batch file, and a [2021 thread](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/grasshopper-batch-execution/119959) wanted to run "headless". Flow's test bridge already drives everything by data. A documented command line would make that bridge public, for example: urbanto --run-flow massing.urbflow --open site.dwg --set Height=24 --out areas.csv --save. Exit codes and a log file would complete it. Batch runs (AUTO-04) and scheduled runs (AUTO-09) would be built on this command line. NatronRenderer renders a saved project with no interface, where `-w` picks a Write node and frame range, `-i` swaps a Read node's file and `-c` runs Python first so values can be set from the command line ([Natron 2.5 docs](https://natron.readthedocs.io/en/rb-2.5/devel/natronexecution.html)).
**Status:** PARTLY: "A test bridge drives everything by data" · **Size:** M · **Value:** 2 · **Kind:** COMPLETION · **Depends on:** Test bridge drives everything by data; flow command ids

<a id="auto-07"></a>

#### AUTO-07 Batch error handling: skip, retry and failure log
Batch error handling lets the user choose, for a long batch or sweep, whether an error stops the run, skips the item and continues, or retries. At the end it lists the items that failed, with their inputs and messages. n8n node settings offer "On Error" choices (Stop Workflow, Continue, Continue using error output) and Retry On Fail ([docs](https://docs.n8n.io/workflows/components/nodes/)), plus [error workflows](https://docs.n8n.io/flow-logic/error-handling/) that run when an execution fails. Houdini's [Local Scheduler](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/nodes/top/localscheduler.html) sets On Task Failure to Report Error, Report Warning, Retry Task or Ignore. The [Revit Batch Processor](https://github.com/bvn-architecture/RevitBatchProcessor) (third party) runs each file in its own session so that a crash does not block further processing. Silent failure is the pain. In a [2021 batch thread](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/grasshopper-batch-execution/119959), the script ran without error and "does absolutely nothing". Flow's for-each container, option sweeps and batch runs would get an On error setting: stop, skip and continue, or retry n times. A summary such as "3 of 120 failed" would link to each failed item, its input values and the node, and the log could be saved.
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** S · **Value:** 2 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** for-each container; Batch run (AUTO-04); Option sweep (AUTO-01)

<a id="auto-08"></a>

#### AUTO-08 Run history
Run history lists past runs with time, trigger (manual, automatic, event, batch), duration, status and changed inputs. Selecting a row restores that run's input values. n8n keeps [execution lists](https://docs.n8n.io/workflows/executions/) per workflow and for all workflows, defining an execution as "a single run of a workflow".
