ComfyUI's [Job History](https://docs.comfy.org/interface/job-history) shows running, completed and failed jobs, with actions such as "Open as workflow in a new tab", and its server keeps a [/history](https://docs.comfy.org/development/comfyui-server/comms_routes) route. In Houdini PDG, "Work items track the results created by their work" ([TOPs intro](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/tops/intro.html)). Demand shows mostly once runs pile up. A [2026 ComfyUI-Manager discussion](https://github.com/Comfy-Org/ComfyUI-Manager/discussions/2872) (third party) built a node because the total queue was hard to see past about 200 items. Flow's canvas history records edits, not runs. A Runs tab in the Flow window would list each run with its trigger and changed slider values. Restore would put those values back and recompute. This would complement sweeps (AUTO-01) and events (AUTO-05) and help users answer "what changed since this morning".
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** M · **Value:** 2 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** Run modes; exposed sliders; canvas history

<a id="auto-09"></a>

#### AUTO-09 Scheduled runs
A scheduled run starts a flow at set times, for example a nightly refresh of a shared area schedule or model check. n8n's [Schedule Trigger](https://docs.n8n.io/integrations/builtin/core-nodes/n8n-nodes-base.scheduletrigger/) works "in a similar way to the Cron software utility". Node-RED's [Inject node](https://nodered.org/docs/user-guide/nodes) can "trigger flows at regular intervals" using cron. Dynamo has a [Periodic run mode](https://dynamobim.org/?p=32455) that runs a graph "every x milliseconds" when a node needs it. Houdini PDG suggests cooking on the farm for a nightly process ([TOPs intro](https://www.sidefx.com/docs/houdini/tops/intro.html)). The evidence of demand comes from tools rather than complaints: the third-party [Revit Batch Processor](https://github.com/bvn-architecture/RevitBatchProcessor) can "run automatically on a schedule (using the Windows Task Scheduler) without the GUI". For a desktop CAD program, Flow should not run a clock of its own. Once the command line exists (AUTO-06), a Schedule button would register a Windows scheduled task that runs a library flow on a chosen drawing. The task would be listed and could be removed from the same place.
**Status:** MISSING · **Size:** S · **Value:** 1 · **Kind:** NEW · **Depends on:** Command-line and headless runs (AUTO-06)

<a id="auto-10"></a>

#### AUTO-10 Recording commands into a flow
Recording captures commands as the user runs them and turns them into something that can be replayed and edited. GenerativeComponents can "record a series of transactions in the DGN file" and shows them in a Transactions dialog where they "can be edited and manipulated" ([Bentley help](https://DOCS.BENTLEY.COM/LiveContent/web/OpenBuildings%20GenerativeComponents%20Help-v10/en/GUID-EADF9FA6-7D93-497B-902C-896C9E1E9088.html)). Blender's [Info editor](https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/editors/info_editor.html) "logs the executed operators", and the entries can be copied. Grasshopper has no recorder. In a [2023 thread](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/grasshopper-record-the-rhino-commands-being-used/152845), a user needed a third-party plugin and Python just to list the Rhino commands used. Flow already has recording: run commands and tools with the pointer and get a flow of them. That is a strong first step for new users who think in commands rather than nodes. A recorded flow can feed straight into the automation features: make it a command (PARAM-14), then run it on files (AUTO-04) or on events (AUTO-05).
**Status:** EXISTS · **Size:** - · **Value:** 4 · **Kind:** - · **Depends on:** -

## Gaps and open questions

- **Blocked pages.** food4rhino.com, where most Grasshopper plug-ins are published, returned HTTP 403 or 405 to automated reads during this research. Plug-ins are therefore cited from their GitHub pages, the authors' McNeel forum posts or third-party sites, each flagged. These pages were also blocked or missing, and nothing relies on them:
  - Autodesk University classes and Autodesk forums (403);
  - docs.unrealengine.com (403);
  - grasshopperprimer.com (TLS certificate failure);
  - Enso's old documentation (404);
  - the Max/MSP debugging page (404).
- **Thin coverage.** Some programs have little public documentation for the features asked about. They are cited where something reliable was found, and the rest is listed here: Natron, Modo's schematic view and Bentley GenerativeComponents are cited from vendor documentation only, with no user-demand threads found; Antimony from its 2014 to 2015 project pages (the project is now in maintenance mode); Pancake from its author's forum posts, because its own site was not reachable from this session; Grasshopper 2 from an undated McNeel overview page and the developer's forum posts, because it has no full manual; Unity VFX Graph and Unreal PCG appear only where their documentation shows something beyond Shader Graph and Blueprints.
- **Weak evidence of demand.** Some rows rest on a forum wish or a third-party plug-in rather than a shipping feature, for example [PERF-07](#perf-07) and [PREV-09](#prev-09). Their paragraphs say so.
- **Sizes are estimates.** Sizes assume Flow's existing parts work as the request describes them. Studio's code was not seen, so sizes are estimates for planning, not quotes.
- **Untested behaviour.** Nothing here was tested in the studied programs; behaviour is quoted from their documentation and user reports.

## Choices made

- **Repository and branch.** Work is in `atkinsdigitalteam/Urbanto` on branch `report/C18`, made from `main`. Batch 1 (pull request #1) is not merged yet, so this branch holds only the C18 files.
- **What features are judged against.** Every status is judged only against the Flow description in the request. Words quoted after PARTLY are from that description. Studio's source was not asked for or seen. The Studio versions are proposals ("Flow would ..."), and the UI sketches are illustrations, not designs of existing screens.
- **How the research was split.** Seven research agents each took two or three of the 16 groups. Two more agents then filled gaps for plug-ins and programs the first pass barely cited. I merged the results, removed duplicates, checked that every row matches its paragraph, and wrote the Answer, the top 25 and the ten things not to copy.
- **Rubrics.** Status, size, value and kind follow the table in "How to read this catalogue". Value is weighted towards new users, as the request asks.
- **Sort order.** Sorting by value over size uses weights S = 1, M = 2, L = 4. This scale is deliberately compressed: hours, days and weeks are really about 1 : 5 : 20, but a 1 : 5 : 20 scale would put every S item above every value-5 M item. Ties go to higher value, then smaller size, then group order. EXISTS rows have no size, so they come last, in group order.
- **Duplicates.** 16 features found by two agents were merged into one row. The dropped ids are not reused, so some groups have gaps in their numbering. References to dropped ids in other paragraphs were rewritten to the kept id. The merges were: AUTO-11 into PARAM-14; CAD-13 into PREV-01; CAD-14 into PREV-10; COLLAB-02 into LIB-10; COLLAB-03 into LIB-11; DATA-03 into WIRE-01; DATA-04 into PORT-04; DATA-11 into WIRE-03; LEARN-02 into STATE-02; LEARN-03 into STATE-04; LEARN-04 into PORT-02; LIB-08 into FIND-08; PORT-11 into SCRIPT-02; PORT-12 into CONT-07; PREV-08 into CAD-01; STATE-11 into PERF-05.
- **Edits to agents' text.**
  - The evidence from "Warnings and errors that explain" was merged into STATE-02.
  - The node-hover point from "Descriptive tooltips" was merged into PORT-02.
  - Three food4rhino download counts were removed because the page could not be re-checked.
- **Program versions.** Versions are as documented on 2026-10-31, as cited in each paragraph: for example the Blender 5.2 LTS manual (features from 4.x on), Houdini 22.0, Nuke 17.1, Unreal Engine 5.8, n8n 2.x, Dynamo 3.x and 4.x, Rhino 8, and Rhino 9 where it is cited.
- **Sources and flags.** "Old" means older than about five years, with the year given. "Third party" means not the vendor or author of the feature. Forum threads are named as forum threads.
- **The top 25.** It is a judgement, not the first 25 rows. It follows dependencies and the new-user goal, takes no L item, and assumes two to three developers for two months.
- **The ten things not to copy.** They were chosen from 28 candidates that the research agents gave with evidence. The others are listed after the ten.
- **Link check.** On 2026-10-31 an automated check requested every page cited in the first full build. 479 of 499 returned HTTP 200, 19 GitHub pages refused automated requests from this machine (HTTP 403; the research agents had read them with a fetch tool), one university PDF timed out, and none returned 404. The pages added by the two gap-filling agents were checked by those agents when they read them.
- **CSV columns.** The CSV has the same columns as the master table plus `what_it_is`, a one-sentence description, so it can be filtered and ticked on its own. It is UTF-8 with a header row.

## 2. CSV file

The same 195 rows, in the same order, are in [C18-flow-feature-catalogue.csv](C18-flow-feature-catalogue.csv) (UTF-8, comma-separated, header row). Columns: rank, id, name, group, source_programs (separated by semicolons), status, size, value, value_over_size, completion_or_new, depends_on, what_it_is. Add a column to tick what to build.

## 3. Top 25 for the next two months

This list is not simply the first 25 rows of the master table. It follows dependencies and the goal of making Flow easier for new users, and it takes no size L item.

- **Size:** it has 15 S items and 10 M items. By the rubric that is about 35 to 115 developer-days, so it assumes two to three developers.
- **Order:** the items are in build order, so each one leans on an earlier one. The groups match the five changes in the Answer.

**Weeks 1 to 3: problems that explain themselves**

1. [STATE-01](#state-01) Error and warning states on nodes (M, value 5). Every later message, roll-up and help link needs a node state to hang on.
2. [STATE-02](#state-02) Plain-language message with a suggested fix (M, 5). Turns an error into a next step, which is what Dynamo learned from its 30 most common errors.
3. [PORT-02](#port-02) Port tooltips with type, description and value (S, 5). Extends the existing hover peek to every port, inputs included, plus a one-line node description.
4. [PORT-03](#port-03) Mark required and optional inputs (S, 5). Tells a newcomer what a node still needs before it can run. It works from the inputs every command already declares.
5. [PORT-07](#port-07) Full names and plain labels (S, 4). Removes abbreviations, a barrier big enough that Grasshopper users installed Bifocals to fix it.
6. [WIRE-02](#wire-02) Say why a connection is refused (S, 5). Ship the reason text first; the one-click conversion follows [WIRE-03](#wire-03).
7. [STATE-07](#state-07) Out-of-date marker in manual mode (S, 4). Manual and delay modes otherwise show old numbers as if they were current.
8. [STATE-06](#state-06) Warnings roll up into groups, sub-flows and tools (S, 4). Collapsed sub-flows and flow tools would otherwise hide problems inside them.

**Weeks 3 to 5: find and understand nodes**

9. [FIND-03](#find-03) Browsable node library panel (M, 5). Lets users browse instead of guessing names; the Dynamo primer calls this the best way to discover nodes.
10. [FIND-04](#find-04) Search results that show what a node does (M, 5). Puts the description where the choice is made.
11. [FIND-05](#find-05) Starter set for new users (S, 4). A "Start here" tab of the 20 or so commands most urban flows need, editable by the office.
12. [FIND-08](#find-08) Recent and favourite nodes (S, 4). A cheap win on the search users already rely on.
13. [LEARN-01](#learn-01) Node help panel, F1 (M, 5). One place for the description, inputs, current warning and an example.

**Weeks 5 to 6: see what each node does**

14. [DATA-02](#data-02) Viewer node and pinned peek (S, 5). Pins the existing hover peek so a value stays visible while the user edits upstream.
15. [PREV-03](#prev-03) Per-node preview on and off (S, 5). Stops intermediate steps cluttering the drawing; new flows preview only end-of-chain results.
16. [PREV-04](#prev-04) Highlight the selected node's results and frame them (S, 4). Links canvas selection to the drawing in one glance.
17. [PREV-09](#prev-09) Pick a shape in the drawing to jump to its node (S, 4). Builds on the Made-by badge. Top-level nodes come first; nodes inside sub-flows follow [CONT-04](#cont-04).
18. [NAV-05](#nav-05) Select upstream or downstream and dim the rest (S, 4). Lets a user read one chain without reading the whole graph.
19. [PERF-01](#perf-01) Per-node run time on the canvas (S, 4). Explains the slow runs that the long-run hint only flags.

**Weeks 6 to 8: start from something that works, and run without wiring**

20. [PORT-01](#port-01) Edit default values right on the node (M, 5). Removes the need to wire a value node for every number.
21. [LIB-01](#lib-01) Template gallery on New (M, 5). Gives a new user urban starter flows that run first time.
22. [LEARN-06](#learn-06) Empty-canvas hints and next-step suggestions (S, 4). The empty canvas points to Recording, a template or a source.
23. [PARAM-03](#param-03) Expose any value as a tool input, with label, tooltip and default (M, 5). Widens "expose a slider" to every value type. The Player needs it.
24. [PARAM-01](#param-01) Player: run a library flow from a simple form (M, 5). Lets colleagues use flows without opening the canvas.
25. [CAD-01](#cad-01) Managed bake that replaces its previous output (M, 5). Stops duplicate geometry piling up on every bake, which today's copying Bake allows.

## 4. Ten things node editors do that Studio should not copy

1. **Search that appears only once you start typing, with no visible field.**
   - **Where:** Blender 4.0 removed the visible search row from its Add menus. Users wrote "panic, where has the search box gone?" ([Blender Artists, 2023, third party](https://blenderartists.org/t/searching/1484515)).
   - **Why not copy:** a new user cannot use a feature they cannot see. Flow's Tab, Space and double-click search should always open with a visible field and a Library button ([FIND-03](#find-03)).
2. **Controls that appear only when zoomed far in.**
   - **Where:** Grasshopper hides the +/- buttons that add inputs until the user zooms in. A user called finding them "a nice discovery after digging for a while" ([McNeel forum](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/setting-a-solid-building-a-selector-to-choose-a-solid/162665)).
   - **Why not copy:** editing controls ([PORT-10](#port-10)) must be visible at normal zoom. Zoom should only change how much detail is shown ([NAV-12](#nav-12)), never what can be done.
3. **Raw exception text as the message users see.**
   - **Where:** Sverchok's documentation says raw Python errors help "sometimes not" ([Sverchok docs](https://nortikin.github.io/sverchok/docs/tree_evaluation_system.html)).
   - **Why not copy:** text such as "Internal Error Pleas [sic] Report: Dereferencing a non-pointer" ([forum, 2018, old, third party](https://forum.robotsinarchitecture.org/index.php?msg=2043)) names the program's internals, not the user's plots. Flow messages should be written in drawing terms ([STATE-02](#state-02)), with the raw text behind a Details link.
4. **Branch-path syntax and the Path Mapper as the way to reshape data.**
   - **Where:** McNeel's own guide calls Grasshopper's Path Mapper "perhaps the least intuitive to use" ([McNeel guide](https://developer.rhino3d.com/guides/grasshopper/gh-algorithms-and-data-structures/advanced-data-structures/)).
   - **Why not copy:** urban designers think in blocks and plots, not `{0;3;0}` addresses. Flow should keep plain-named port modifiers ([DATA-06](#data-06)) and a structure viewer ([DATA-08](#data-08)).
5. **Silently repeating the last item when list lengths differ.**
   - **Where:** Grasshopper's default longest-list matching reuses the last item of the shorter list without any sign. Users find "the last item is duplicated repeatedly" only afterwards ([McNeel forum](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/ghpython-multiple-inputs-are-all-given-same-list-length-when-input-to-functions/101324)).
   - **Why not copy:** Flow should show the matching rule on the node and warn with the counts when lengths differ ([DATA-01](#data-01)).
6. **A Bake that adds another copy every time.**
   - **Where:** McNeel's own baking page says users had to keep copies or delete old geometry before baking again ([Rhino Grasshopper baking](https://www.rhino3d.com/features/grasshopper/baking)).
   - **Why not copy:** new users re-bake after every slider change and stack duplicates they cannot tell apart. Flow's "Bake copies results to a layer" should default to replacing its previous bake, with "add copies" as an explicit choice ([CAD-01](#cad-01)).
7. **Form order taken from where nodes sit on the canvas.**
   - **Where:** Dynamo Player lists inputs "in the order they appear in Dynamo starting from the top to bottom" ([Dynamo Player inputs](https://help.autodesk.com/cloudhelp/2022/ENU/Alias-NURBS-Modeling/files/Generate-designs-with-Dynamo/Alias-Dynamo-Player/GUID-7C9864A5-5738-44F2-A455-F989863043F9.html)).
   - **Why not copy:** tidying the canvas would then reshuffle the form that non-authors see. Flow has Tidy (Ctrl+L) and one-step group moves, so a tool's input order must be stored explicitly ([PARAM-09](#param-09)).
8. **Sub-flows that cannot contain loops.**
   - **Where:** Grasshopper clusters solve once, so looping plug-ins cannot live inside them ([McNeel forum](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/loop-in-grasshopper/90030)).
   - **Why not copy:** Flow's loop and for-each containers must nest freely inside sub-flows and tools. Otherwise packaging a working flow silently breaks it.
9. **Installing third-party code automatically when a file is opened.**
   - **Where:** ComfyUI's missing-node prompt offers Install All, and one custom node stole browser passwords through its dependencies ([Gigazine, 2024, third party](https://gigazine.net/gsc_news/en/20240611-comfyui-llmvision-malware)).
   - **Why not copy:** opening a flow should never install anything. Missing items should open as visible placeholders with a report ([COLLAB-01](#collab-01)).
10. **Solving on the interface thread, with Escape checked only between steps.**
    - **Where:** in Grasshopper 1, a runaway solve freezes the window. Users report that "the only solution is often to kill the whole Rhino task" ([McNeel forum](https://discourse.mcneel.com/t/wish-shortcut-to-lock-solver/54021)).
    - **Why not copy:** in a CAD program that loses drawing work. Flow already has cancel and a long-run hint; it should keep solving off the interface thread ([PERF-06](#perf-06)) and guard runaway runs ([PERF-07](#perf-07)).

Also considered, but not in the ten:
- unitless numbers (Dynamo);
- cloud-trained node suggestions that upload graphs (Dynamo);
- carrying on after an error with the last valid data (n8n);
- silent re-routing of a displaced wire (Blender);
- expand that resets values (Max/MSP);
- always-on node thumbnails (Unreal Material Editor);
- typed rule syntax for hiding inputs (Houdini);
- user interfaces built from nodes (Human UI);
- a save format that mixes layout into logic (Grasshopper);
- short version history (n8n);
- live multi-user canvas editing (Unreal);
- several Python engines side by side (Dynamo);
- a separate AI tab per node (n8n);
- code that runs on open without a trust step (Blender);
- a cache component whose modes are picked by a text input (Rhino 8);
- live links that break when the editor closes (Dynamo).

They were left out because they are less likely to hurt a new user in a CAD program, or the ten above already cover the same lesson.
